## Summary

The Failure & Trail System is the pure-reader renderer that turns the Life-Support Graph's failure descriptors into physical marks on the ship (mist, frost fan, scorch, puddle, hairline -> crack -> bent rib), in three sizes, thickest at the source, with density from flow and length from age; it holds no state, registers no block, hook or command, writes only canvas, material and particle output, and is the truth channel that no hallucination may add to, alter or remove. Read this GDD if you design, art-direct or build anything that shows a fault, tells a player where a fault is, lights a fault, or sits beside the trails (Emitter State, Lighting, the Perception Filter, Horror Entities, In-World Instruments, Onboarding, Derelict Authoring): it fixes the descriptor-to-visual table of record for all eighteen descriptor ids (ids 1-10, 20-26 and 27 PATCH_WEEP; *Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review*), the growth, reach, dullness and budget formulas with their worked values, the tell-window and faster-than-walking invariants, and its acceptance criteria (AC-1 to AC-188, with lettered splits; *Amended 2026-10-27 — design review*). It does not simulate propagation or atmosphere, plays no sound, and has no UI of its own.

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Feature` (systems-index enumeration #9, Recommended Design Order 8; Category Gameplay) · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Life-Support Graph`, `Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera` (both designed); `Command & Simulation Layer` (designed, transitive); `Ship Section & Layout Data Model` (designed, hard: anchor geometry and room surfaces, LY-8 and LY-23). Depended on by: Onboarding (index, undesigned) and Emitter State (EM-17, soft, designed); neighbours that repeat this system's rows: Lighting, Perception Filter, Horror Entities, In-World Instruments, Adaptive Audio, Interaction, Derelict Authoring, Sanity, Boarding & Salvage, Modular Ship Assembly, Suit & HUD (all designed); still undesigned: Onboarding, Settings, Co-op. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: neighbour statuses refreshed.)*

## Overview

The Failure & Trail System turns the Life-Support Graph's failure descriptors into physical marks on the ship: it is the **skin that shows the bruise**. It is a pure reader and a pure renderer. Every frame it reads the Graph's true failure state (`failures()` descriptors, the compartment bands and true temperature, `fuel_puddle_ml`) through the one read channel `read_view`, and draws, for each descriptor that has a consequence-channel medium, a mark in that loop's own vocabulary at the fitting, seam, opening frame or rib where the failure sits: **mist drifts** where air escapes, a **static crystalline frost fan** where coolant weeps, a **sharp radial scorch** where power arcs or a machine overheats, a **glossy, flat, ember-edged puddle** where fuel drips, a **hairline that becomes a crack and then a bent rib** where the hull gives. Every mark is thickest at its source, in one of three sizes (48, 96 and 144 base px) chosen by severity, with density chosen by flow and length chosen by age. The system holds no state: it registers no block, no step hook and no command, owns no propagation and no atmosphere (both are Graph state), writes only `CanvasItem`, `Material` and particle properties, and every visual property of every mark is a pure function of the Graph's current values and the render clock. That statelessness is what makes it a **truth channel**: a trail is on the Graph's closed consequence-channel list, so the Perception Filter may neither remove, alter nor add one, and a hallucination that lies about a machine's sprite or gauge cannot lie about the floor beside it.

The player never operates this system and never sees a menu for it. They meet it as the ship's handwriting. They wake in the cryo bay and their chest lamp catches a frost fan running from a lagged elbow toward the far wall; they follow it to its thickest point and find the fitting; they close the valve and watch the drip stop while the fan stays, still and dulling; they weld on a derelict section and watch mist spread along the seam bead; they hear a hiss behind a dark panel and walk to it because the mist will be there once the lamp arrives. Trails are the visible half of the Graph's two-channel warning: the fairness gate guarantees that a sev >= 1 descriptor exists for at least `W_MIN_WARNING_S` (10 s) before any endogenous loss, and this system is what makes that descriptor *seen* **where the descriptor is on the consequence channel** (the FREEZE, BREACH, OVERLOAD, OVERHEAT-shimmer and seam-weep pre-stages, and the PATCH_WEEP of a patched subject; a wearing LEAK's own pre-stage is still the WORN descriptor, which is module-body and drawn by the module presenter, not here: FT-7, FT-32) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, with a growth rate bounded so a far-elbow trail crosses a six-tile room (288 base px) inside the 10-30 s tell window, and so a sev-4 trail on a single edge is strictly faster than walking and no faster than running. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the growth promises are measured from the trail's own per-mode onset, the Graph's GA-T, which the Graph adopted in LS-14 (FT-8, FT-30).)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* The same grammar, dulled and stilled, is the verdict of a dead derelict: a frost fan with no leak is coolant, a radial scorch at a junction box is power, an ember-edged stain is fuel, branching hairlines through ribs are the hull, and nothing at all where the air went.

The index describes the system as a pure reader that maps "graph failure state and footprint sources" to trail visuals. This GDD reconciles the second half: the Graph persists no footprint record and emits no footprint-tagged descriptor (the M17 `deposit_trace` kinds surface only as ordinary hp, temperature and section-rate changes), so this system draws **Graph descriptors only**; a hidden entity's web-grammar marks (smear, branching rime, dust direction, filament twitch) belong to Horror Entities and share only the floor-marks draw band. Without this system the Graph is invisible: Pillar 1's design test ("a hidden stat vs a visibly leaking pipe -> the pipe") has no pipe to draw, the tell Pillar 2 promises is a number nobody sees, and Pillar 4's guarantee that "no lie is unfalsifiable" has no physical witness to check a false gauge against. The MVP hypothesis (`design/gdd/game-concept.md`, MVP item 2: "a visible O2 / heat / power graph with propagating, physically rendered failures") cannot be tested without it. It is a Feature-layer system (it holds a render of Core state, not state) with three designed hard dependencies, the Life-Support Graph, the Pixel-Perfect Render Pipeline & Camera and the Ship Section & Layout Data Model (LY-8, LY-23), whose anchor geometry and room surfaces this system reads (Dependencies). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: Layout is designed.)* The MVP slice (what ships first and what waits) is FT-38.

*Implementation questions raised while designing and deferred to ADRs (-> `/architecture-decision`): the split between the notification handler and the render callback (PR-8 rule 3); stepped sprite frames vs `GPUParticles2D` for moving media (spike-gated); the per-instance reveal-threshold and coverage-rank plumbing that keeps alpha binary; a pure `RefCounted` resolver `descriptor -> TrailSpec` so the decision logic is headless-testable; z-index numbering inside the floor-marks band; and the Perception Filter's truth-channel mask route (Filter-owned).*

## Player Fantasy

**Framing: direct, with a forensic depth and a witness guarantee.** The player knowingly reads failure as marks the ship leaves on itself and follows them to their source. *(creative-director consulted; Framing A "follow the mark to the wound" adopted, with B "the room remembers" as its depth in the past tense and C "the one thing you can still trust" as its guarantee. The Graph adopted the same layered shape; here the direct layer is the player's own act of reading.)*

> The ship cannot speak, but it cannot lie. When a loop fails, the failure writes itself onto the hull in the loop's own hue: soft drifting mist where air escapes, a static crystalline frost fan where coolant weeps, a sharp radial scorch where power arcs, a glossy ember-edged puddle under a fuel flange, a hairline that becomes a crack and then a bent rib where the hull gives. Every mark is thickest at its source. The player's chest lamp finds it; their eye follows it; their hands end at the fitting that failed.
>
> Competence is reading. A veteran crew is not faster with a wrench; it sees the frost the moment the pipe lets go (the frost fan is the first thing the loop draws, and it can precede the wall gauge's reading), knows a scorch from a stain, and knows an old isolated leak (still, dulled, no drip) from a live one. On a dead derelict the same marks are a verdict: frost fan, coolant; radial scorch, power (conditional on the Graph emitting a dead electrical descriptor: ET-4, Open Questions); ember stain, fuel; branching hairlines, the hull; and nothing at all where the air went (the O2 verdict is carried by the ATMO band and the draught, not by a mark: default applied 2026-10-27, pending user confirmation, Open Questions 6b) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: "sees the frost before the gauge drops" had no mechanism, because a wearing pipe carries only the WORN descriptor, which this system never draws and the Filter may corrupt; the claim is now "the moment the pipe lets go". Whether a consequence-channel sev-1 sweat companion exists for WORN is routed to the Graph owner.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: reading before failure is true only for some failures, and the fantasy now says which. **A floor pre-stage exists** (drawn here, consequence channel, readable before the loss) for: a freezing pipe (`4 FREEZE` sev 1), a hull plate or seam giving way (`5 BREACH` hairline, sev 1-2), a cable or box warming (`7 OVERLOAD`, sev 1-2), a machine heating (`3 OVERHEAT` shimmer, sev 2), a new weld (the seam weep), a strained hatch (`26`) and a **patched** pipe, duct or cable (`27 PATCH_WEEP`, the small weep that tells the patch is still only a patch). **No floor pre-stage exists** for the most common failure, a wearing pipe, duct or tank before it lets go (a `1 LEAK` that has not yet crossed `LEAK_HP`, or a `2 SHORT` not preceded by OVERLOAD): its only earlier tells are on the module-body channel (sprite wear, gauge), which the Filter may corrupt, so there the floor tells the truth only after the line has let go. Whether the Graph adds a consequence-channel sweat companion for WORN is the open Pillar 2 / Pillar 4 decision of Open Questions 45, owed to the Graph owner; until it is ruled the fantasy promises "reads the moment it lets go" for a wearing pipe and "reads before" only for the list above.)*
>
> And the floor never lies. When sanity slips, the gauge can read green, the scrubber can spin, the pipe's edge can bleed violet; the mist at its outlet does not change. No hallucination adds, alters or removes a mark. When the machine and the floor disagree, the floor wins.

**Emotion.** *Dread-literacy.* The player is a tracker reading a wounded body. The power is not control and not speed; it is **certainty earned by looking**: the click of following a mark back to the fitting that made it. Under threat that certainty is the only one the crew has left. The Graph's fantasy is caretaking under threat (the circulation); this system's is the skin that shows the bruise. In the Pillar 1 voice: *"Pipes leak, frost forms, air hisses; the physical world is ground truth."* and *"Physical consequences. A pipe may look fixed, but the room still gets cold, the air still thins, the pressure still drops."* (partial quotation by design: the concept's human-channel bullet for Pillar 1 pre-dates the chest-mounted lamp decision and is not quoted here). Design test from the concept: *a hidden stat vs a visibly leaking pipe -> the pipe*; and from the visual anchor: *a "COOLANT LOW" banner vs a frosted elbow with a drip -> the elbow.*

**MDA and motivation.** Primary aesthetic: Challenge, delivered as competence by literacy (reading faster, not repairing faster). *(The concept ranks Discovery first and Challenge second at game level; this system ranks Challenge first locally because its job is the concept's Challenge row, a visible life-support graph that fails in traceable ways, while the verdict grammar, ET-4, delivers Discovery; this is not drift.)* Secondary: Discovery (the verdict grammar on derelicts: "what killed this ship?"). Tertiary: Fellowship (the floor is common ground two crewmates can both point at; **conditional on the VS-B MIRROR path**, which is not in the MVP: at MVP, single-player, Fellowship is a promise and not a delivered aesthetic *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*). Competence is served by legible cause, traceable origin and no hidden threshold; autonomy by a mark that tells the player what is *true* and never what to *do* (valve, patch, retreat or ignore stays theirs); relatedness by identical trails on every client, which lets crewmates verify each other (a VS-B property; see the Dependencies row for Co-op).

**Anchor moment: the first mark (cryo bay, minute one).** You wake. Your chest lamp (beam origin = the per-facing `chest_point`, hands free) falls across the deck and catches a fan of Coolant Ice frost running from a lagged elbow to the far wall. Nothing in the room moves faster than the drip at its root. Your eye does the work before you do: the fan is thickest at the elbow, so you walk to it. You close the upstream valve: the drip stops, but the fan stays, **still, not gone** (the Graph's isolated-leak descriptor has `intensity_pm` 0 while its mode stays, `LSG:127`). The heater warms the bay (8 mC per Graph step, about 2.5 minutes to COMFORT, `LSG:839`) and the fan's reach pulls back toward a local stamp at the elbow, while the coolant wet run, which owes nothing to temperature, stays on the floor from the elbow to the far wall and keeps the way back to the wound *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. The wall gauge finally agrees with the pipe. The tutorial is physical, and the fixture is authored so the leak's mode onset (`mode_since_step`, GA-T) is already at least 56 steps old when you arrive, so the frost already reaches the far wall at wake (the self-contained fixture `FX-CRYO` is owned by this GDD, FT-40; a flagged requirement on the Onboarding GDD, see Open Questions). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the fixture authors the mode onset, not `fail_since_step`, which belongs to FAILED nodes and is not what a LEAK counts from.)* Feel: quiet competence, "I read that."

**Experience targets (retained-capture scenes; every number is a locked value or a flagged placeholder).**

| # | Moment | What the player sees and feels | What guarantees it |
|---|---|---|---|
| ET-1 | The first mark (above) | One trail, thickest at its source, a valve that stops the drip but not the fan, the room slowly warming; quiet competence | One trail per source, no orphan trail (`LSG:970`); the T -2000 mC cryo-bay fixture (`LSG:839`) as the self-contained `FX-CRYO` (FT-40); FT-3, FT-11, FT-17. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the "stays, still" half needs the isolation freeze of GA-T; the "far wall at wake" half needs the mode onset of GA-T.)* |
| ET-2 | The sentence that grows (a weld seam weeps O2) | Soft drifting mist at the Hull Grey bead, spreading along the seam line while you watch; dread with time to act | The seam weeps from the step it exists (`LSG:228`); the Graph states the weep as `SEAM`, mode `1 LEAK`, `medium` GAS, **sev 1** while `A_seam` > 0, `intensity_pm` per LS-14 field provenance, counted from the SeamRecord's `built_step` (`LSG:170`), which is a true onset, so ET-2 is reachable from the Graph's present fields and does not wait for GA-T; growth such that a 6-tile room (288 base px) is crossed in at most 28.8 s at the sev-1 floor and 14.4 s at the `GROW_PX_PER_STEP[1]` placeholder of 2 px/step; the weep is held legible at `A_seam` 1 (about 15 permille) by the mist legibility floor `COVER16_FLOOR` (D.4); FT-8, FT-9, FT-14. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)* |
| ET-3 | Retreat (breach rhythm) | A rib cracks; at sev 4 the mark jumps from hairline to bent rib, and the mark's **age front** then runs along that one edge faster than walking (`V_WALK` 2 px/tick = 12 px per Graph step) and no faster than running (`V_RUN` 3 px/tick = 18 px per Graph step): the `[PLACEHOLDER]` 15 px/step is 150 px/s. On a single edge, walk and the front passes you; run and you keep ahead of it. **What moves, and what does not** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the l head (the bent rib) stays at the source, because the chain tapers (FT-6, D.1), so what travels along the edge is the narrowing tail of s-sized pieces, mostly a hairline; and the front is the **extent of the mark, which is its age, not a hazard boundary**: nothing becomes dangerous because the mark reached it (the hazard is the descriptor's own `sev` and flow, the Graph's). Behind it the cascade front lights the next edge one hop per Graph step, which is far faster than `V_RUN` and is the Graph's propagation, not a race the player can win; "run and you beat it" is therefore true of **one edge only**. Flight. **Reachable only from a fresh onset:** the sev-4 mark starts at `grow_steps` 0 at the moment `sev` reaches 4 (a sev rise on an aged plate, or an exogenous RUPTURE on a pristine plate, restamps the mode onset: GA-T, adopted in LS-14) and it is the new bent rib that begins at the source, never an aged mark arriving full-grown; the Graph confirmed GA-T, so the earlier contingency (cutting the endogenous half of this promise) has lapsed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings; re-review)* | `ART:224` ("a failure trail propagating along a graph edge faster than you can walk"); FT-6, FT-8, FT-9, FT-10. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| ET-4 | The verdict (a dead derelict) | A greyed frost fan at a lagged elbow, a matte radial scorch at a junction box (**conditional**: see below), an ember-edged stain at a flange, and **no mist anywhere** (O2 failure leaves no trail: read it from absence); you know what killed the ship before you touch anything. Forensic discovery | Dead faults are Graph faults at `hp` <= `HP_FAIL` on an unpowered branch with `intensity_pm` 0 and a back-dated mode onset (`LSG:226`; Derelict Authoring D.11 back-dates both `fail_since_step` and, under GA-T, `mode_since_step`), so the dried trail is a pure function and not a decal; FT-17, FT-18. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: (1) a `2 SHORT` clears when its breaker opens or the fault is repaired (`LSG:127`, LS-11), so an unpowered dead electrical fault emits a `2 SHORT` only if the Graph chooses to; until the Graph owner states which mode a dead unpowered electrical fault emits (WORN would draw nothing) the "matte scorch" leg of the verdict has no descriptor and is not promised (Open Questions). (2) On a derelict the most lethal loop, O2, leaves no residue: a healthy ship and an O2-killed ship both show zero mist pixels. This is the shipped default and is carried by the ATMO band and the draught (Suit, Audio); a dry Hull Grey nozzle-collar residue is not added: default applied 2026-10-27, pending user confirmation, Open Questions 6b.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| ET-5 | The floor disagrees (hallucination) | The scrubber renders whole, its rotor turning, its gauge green (a false state on the corruptible module-body channel); at its outlet fitting, soft mist still drifts, exactly as at full sanity. Vertigo, then grip: *the floor is still mine* | The closed consequence-channel list (`LSG:168`); FT-2, FT-24; the bit-identical capture (`PIPE:989`) |
| ET-6 | The stilled leak (end of a long session) | The corridor carries the ship's recent history: a coolant fan at an elbow whose valve you closed, dulling now; the seam bead still weeping where the weld has not yet worn in; the soot of a heat event. You can count the wounds the crew survived, as marks that are *true*, not as a score | Isolated leaks keep their mode with `intensity_pm` 0 (`LSG:127`); seam weeps persist until wear-in (`LSG:170`); FT-17. A patched pipe is **not** one of these: it carries the small PATCH_WEEP, see the anti-promises and FT-32 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |

*Session emotional arc carried by trails:* calm reading (ET-1) -> watchful tending (ET-2) -> forensic curiosity (ET-4) -> flight (ET-3) -> vertigo and grip (ET-5) -> ownership by history (ET-6). Flow entry is ET-1's single trail. The intentional flow breaks are the s -> m -> l jumps: a severity change is a discrete `F/` key event, so the jump is sudden by construction (FT-6).

**Design test.** *When a choice makes a failure easier to notice from a marker vs easier to follow from the mark it leaves on the ship -> the mark. When a choice makes a trail more dramatic vs keeps it a pure function of the Graph's true state -> the true state.* Corollaries, each of which becomes a rule or an acceptance line rather than a mechanic: (1) a trail is never drawn where the Graph has no descriptor, and a consequence-channel descriptor with sev >= 1 is never left undrawn on screen at the current zoom step (FT-3, FT-19); (2) the thickest point of every trail is its source, so following the gradient always ends at the failing fitting, seam, opening or rib (FT-3); (3) a trail stays on a floor, wall face, seam, opening frame or fitting, never on a machine body sprite, a gauge or a crewmate (FT-24); (4) the only way to remove a trail is to change the Graph (valve, patch, breaker, cut, warmth, time); no verb, toggle, setting, sanity stage or hallucination removes one (FT-2); (5) a trail is seen only by spending light, and in the dark the hiss, the cold and the draught still tell (FT-26). *Worked application:* "add a soft cyan outline to a leaking pipe so players spot it at maximum zoom-out" is a marker, not a mark, and adds a world-space highlight layer the art bible rejected; **rejected**. If legibility fails at the ladder floor the fix lives in the stamp itself (the 50 % legibility gate, FT-34), never in an added layer.

**Under hallucination the fantasy deepens rather than breaks.** Each sanity stage raises the *value* of the trail instead of eroding it. The ladder below is the table of record for the Player Fantasy; each row cites the rule that already guarantees it.

| Sanity drift stage (concept, Visual Identity Anchor) | What lies | What the trail does | The player learns | Guaranteed by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Instruments disagree | gauge needle, inspect card | Unchanged; the gauge's physical witness (the Graph's LS-22 table, `LSG:205`) is still drawn when lit | "check the pipe, not the needle" | the consequence-channel list (`LSG:168`); In-World Instruments pairs with this system as the truth check (provisional, `IDX:39`) |
| 2. The light lies (the cone looks longer, phantom shadows) | the Filter's colour pass over the perceived cone | Trails are lit only by the **true** reveal radius (Lighting, a truth channel per the kernel's non-claim, `KERN:44`); beyond it the floor stays dark even where the cone *seems* to reach | "if the frost isn't lit, my light isn't there": the trail falsifies the light lie | FT-26; `PIPE:206` mask (provisional on Lighting) |
| 3. Loop hues bleed violet at pipe silhouettes (`ART:232`, `ART:238`) | pipe edges | The trail keeps its loop hue and contrast; violet on a pipe beside an unviolet frost fan is itself a tell | "the pipe is wrong, the frost is right" | the Filter is colour-only and truth-masked (`PIPE:206`); FT-2, FT-22 |
| Class (b), false system state | machine sprite whole or broken, gauge | The mark at the fitting is unchanged (ET-5) | "the floor outranks the machine" | FT-24 (anchored at the fitting, never on the body) |
| Class (a), phantom entity | a monster that is not there | A phantom tears nothing: it leaves no descriptor, so no mark. A **real** hidden entity that ruptures the hull leaves an ordinary BREACH or LEAK descriptor, drawn like any breach. Caveat in the Dependencies and Open Questions: a GOUGE on a non-hull subject surfaces only as the corruptible WORN descriptor, so "never the trail" is guaranteed for the hull, not yet for ducts and cables | "did it leave a mark?" is a check that costs a walk and light | FT-31; the routed Graph/Horror gap |
| Class (c), crewmate as horror | crewmate sprite | Not applicable to trails; the crewmate's lamp is the human channel (Lighting, Controller). Stated so this GDD does not reach for it | none | none (out of scope) |
| Class (d), phantom salvage | crate contents | A module's own fitting on a derelict may carry a dried trail from its authored dead fault: a cheap field read ("this one arced") that exists only where Derelict Authoring placed a dead fault | a candidate field check, never a guarantee | `LSG:226`; opportunity routed to Salvage and Derelict Authoring |

**Two non-negotiables (the guarantee half of the framing).** *The Filter may neither remove, alter nor add a trail* (`LSG:168`): there is no phantom trail, because a false trail would make the floor itself lie and no lie would then be falsifiable by the physical world, the one failure Pillar 4 forbids. And *this system is never a hallucination surface and has no per-player variant*: two crewmates looking at the same floor see the same marks, so co-op chatter moves from "is that scrubber green on your screen?" to "look at the floor" (the Fellowship contribution; the equal-extent promise depends on a routed Graph request, FT-33). **Cost of checking** (Pillar 4: "costs time, risk or trust"): reading a trail is never free. It costs light, a walk to where the lamp can reach, and time. The system must not reduce that cost with highlights, zoom-revealed hints or added dark-visible marks. **The one exception, stated honestly:** the emissive layer of a live scorch, arc or ember edge (FT-26, FT-27) is visible in the dark. That visibility is *paid for in Attention exposure*, not given free: the derived FAULT_GLOW emitter counts toward the web's exposure (Emitter State EM-17), so a player who walks toward a visible arc in a dark room is spending exposure to read it; the overlay itself lights nothing and exposes nothing. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G2)* **The exposure price starts at sev 3, and that is deliberate:** the sev 1-2 `7 OVERLOAD` and `3 OVERHEAT` rims are emissive overlays with no light request (`sev_min` 3, V/A.6), so they are visible in the dark at no exposure cost. The re-review put this as a pillar tension (the early-warning read is free while Pillar 3 says emission is exposure); the rulings sheet's G2 takes the creative-director's stated lean, option (c): the sev 1-2 pre-stage tell is the fairness guarantee (P2), so it stays free to read, and the Pillar 3 trade begins where the emitter does, at sev 3 (Open Questions 6g). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier text said nothing is dark-visible, which emissive faults contradict.)*

**Known gap, closed by user ruling (Open Questions 6a): "follow the mark to the wound" now holds for a coolant leak at every temperature.** A `1 LEAK/COOLANT` descriptor in a COMFORT room (where the home ship spends most of its time) still draws the local frost fan stamp and the drip, and the frost reach there is 0 (D.3); it now also draws a **coolant wet run**: a static, dark, parallel-edged streak with a 2 px Coolant Ice edge, laid along the same chain from the fan out to the extent the trail's age has reached, its width stepping down 8 / 6 / 4 / 2 px with distance from the source (D.2d, FT-11). Following the thickening run ends at the fitting at COMFORT as following the fan does in FROST. **Resolved 2026-10-27 — user ruling (design-review decisions): Option A, a temperature-independent coolant wet run with its own thickness gradient;** Option B ("gradient guaranteed in FROST and colder only") is not adopted. AC-26c asserts it. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

**Reference techniques, taken precisely.**

| Reference | Adopt (exact technique) | Reject (exact technique) |
|---|---|---|
| Pandorum | the chest lamp as the only reader of a dark ship; the ship as the reliable narrator | none |
| Barotrauma | hull damage drawn where it is, as cracks on the wall you go to with a tool; a leak is a medium you see | the Status Monitor ship-overview screen; any per-room damage percentage |
| Dead Space (2008) | information lives on the world, not a HUD | the floor **locator line** pointing at the objective: a trail points to a source only because physics put it there, never to a goal |
| Return of the Obra Dinn | reconstructing cause from residue with no hint system (the verdict grammar, ET-4) | the confirmation ping when a deduction is right: no "correct" feedback |
| Project Zomboid | the room as a record; the base degrades on its own clock and noticing is competence | numeric condition readouts in context menus |
| Batman: Arkham, Witcher senses, any detective vision | none | any highlight, x-ray or scan mode that reveals trails or tints failing pipes: it turns reading into a button press and destroys "light is a decision" |
| Oxygen Not Included | gas and liquid as visible media, propagation shown in the medium | gas, temperature and power **overlays** (forbidden by `LSG:1003`) |
| Alien: Isolation | hissing pipes and leaking steam as the atmosphere of failure; checking costs you | **ambient decorative steam vents** that mean nothing: every mist here is a descriptor (no orphan trail, `ART:669`) |
| The Long Dark | cold read from the world before any number | **screen-space frost** on the view edge: body cues are Suit and HUD's, and this system draws nothing in screen space |
| FTL | causality between doors and air | room fire and breach **icons** (the icon layer the art bible rejected, `ART:44`-`ART:55`) |
| Dwarf Fortress fluids | propagation visible, not announced | per-tile simulation of the spread (the spatial gradient is presentation interpolation, `LSG:974`) |

**Tone and language (for every later document that names a trail).** Use the art bible's five behaviour words and the Graph's names: *mist drifts; frost fan, static, crystalline; scorch, sharp, radial, blooms; puddle, glossy, flat, ember edge (a stain when dried); hairline -> crack -> bent rib*; and *drip, weep, seam, bead, fitting, elbow, collar, lagging, residue*, with *still / live* and *dull / fresh* as the two axes. Avoid "smoke", "fog", "steam" (ambient, or the rejected perception mask); "rime" for coolant (branching rime is the hidden-entity footprint's word, `ART:565`); "burn mark", "explosion", "sparks shower" (combat register); "blood", "bleed", "pool of" anything bodily (flesh is sanity's hue, red is the alarm's); "damage decal", "health crack" (stat language); "leak indicator", "warning", "alert", "HP", "damage %" (meter language); "dead/active effect", "fades out" (a fade is presentation state); and "objective", "marker", "waypoint", "hint", "highlight" (guidance-system drift). Modes are always written `id NAME/MEDIUM` as the Graph does, and the count is always "six hazard groups, eighteen descriptor ids" *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: id 27 PATCH_WEEP is the eighteenth; this GDD's earlier count was seventeen)*.

**What this GDD does not promise (anti-pillar guard rails; each traces to a pillar or anti-pillar and to the rule that enforces it).**
1. **No guidance system.** No marker, outline, ping, highlight, scan mode, objective line, world-space icon or arrow. A trail exists only where a descriptor exists, in the art bible's vocabulary (P1 design test; FT-3, FT-4).
2. **No meter, readout or alarm.** No numeric leak rate, hp tint, health bar, ship-status, schematic or minimap; never the alarm colour; `alarm_eligible` constrains this system only negatively (never first). UI Requirements: none; the three diegetic surfaces are the Graph's, and this system adds no surface (`LSG:1003`; FT-4).
3. **No lie on the floor.** No phantom trail, no "perceived" variant, no per-player trail, no sanity-driven change to any trail property (P4; FT-2).
4. **No repair reward.** No "fixed!" sparkle, completion ping, fade-out flourish or satisfaction effect. After a repair, a cap or a cut the descriptor is gone and the stamp is gone the next frame; the reward is the absence. **A patch leaves a small weep, not a clean floor:** the Graph clears the LEAK mode at the patch and a patched subject (`hp_cap` below `HP_FULL`, `hp` below `HP_OK`) then carries the descriptor-only id `27 PATCH_WEEP` instead of WORN (consequence channel, sev 1: LS-11, LS-14), so the fan, the wet run and the drip are gone the next frame and one small static s mark stays at the fitting for as long as the patch holds. The three-way contrast is the grammar: **a closed valve leaves the full mark, still; a patch leaves a small weep, static; a cap or a cut leaves nothing.** The weep is the patch's own truth (it can fail), never a reward: no sparkle, no completion cue (FT-32). (`LSG:50` "no completion ping"; FT-1.) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*
5. **No combat feedback.** No hit-flash, damage number, impact decal for tool use against horrors, or "blood". A horror's own marks are web grammar, owned by Horror Entities (anti-pillar "not a combat game"; FT-4, FT-31).
6. **No light, and no readability in the dark.** A trail is a lit mark; it casts no light; the emissive layer on scorch, arc and ember is the only glow and it lights nothing; the sev 1-2 OVERLOAD and OVERHEAT rims are dark-visible without an exposure cost, a stated pillar trade (Open Questions 6g) (P3; FT-26, FT-27). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G2)* Whether a fault's glow counts toward the web's exposure is decided by Emitter State's EM-17 (designed): the derived FAULT_GLOW emitter counts as exposure, the overlay does not.
7. **No cleaning, painting or decorating.** No verb, tool or setting erases or places a trail; welding is functional. Derelict authoring may not place loop-vocabulary decals that are not Graph faults (dead faults are Graph faults, `LSG:226`) (anti-pillar "not a freeform builder"; FT-3).
8. **No story beats.** Trails are not authored narrative props; the verdict grammar emerges from authored Graph faults, never from scripted sequences (anti-pillar "no authored story").
9. **No progression or score.** No count of trails fixed and no repair achievement surfaced by this system (anti-pillar "no win state"; `LSG:52`).
10. **Accessibility never removes truth.** Reduced motion holds moving media on a frame (direction and density kept); reduce flashing holds arcs as a steady glow; neither hides a trail (`PIPE:847`; FT-28, FT-29).

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

*Every rule below describes what the player sees and when; node classes appear only in the Engine notes (the closing sub-heading of Visual/Audio Requirements) and the budget table (D.9), tagged as ADR candidates; the registry facts this system introduces are handed to the registry stage in `registry-candidates.yaml`, not duplicated here. Rule ids are `FT-n`; formulas are `D.n` in the Formulas section and are named, never restated, here; knobs are UPPER_SNAKE in Tuning Knobs. The system registers **no block, no step hook and no command**: the words "hook", "block" and "command" appear below only in sentences that say so. Registry constants are cited by their registered name and value (`TICK_RATE` 60, `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6, `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256, `W_MIN_WARNING_S` 10, `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` 3, `TILE_PX` 48, `V_WALK` 2 and `V_RUN` 3 px per tick); Graph-owned constants (`LEAK_HP`, `BREACH_HP`, `ALARM_SEV_MIN`, `A_HOLE_MAX`, `SEV_STEP`, `OPEN_END_RATE_PM`, `FREEZE_WARN_MC` and kin) are registered with source the Graph GDD (2026-10-27) and are cited by registered name and value; they are not this system's to change. A value this system needs from an undesigned neighbour or a Graph field the Graph has not defined is tagged **PROVISIONAL** with its owner; a value this system owns and no neighbour has fixed is tagged **[PLACEHOLDER: NAME]** with its registry candidate. Where the art-director or an advisor ruling changed a row of a table of record, the row was rewritten, not annotated.*

#### 1. Identity and channel

**FT-1 Statelessness.** Every visual property of every trail element is a pure function of `read_view` fields (`failures()`, `compartment()`, `node()` and `edge()`), Layout geometry and `present_time` (animation phase only). The system owns no timer, no fade, no grow counter, no smoothing accumulator, no "last seen" flag, no spawn-time stamp and no `Tween`; the only members that persist across a Graph step are pooled scene-node references, the per-step cache of the last `read_view` poll (which is a copy of truth and not state) and **the diagnostic latch set `diag_latch` (a set of `(log_id, subject_id)` pairs, FT-41), which no draw, reconcile or selection path may read** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the logging flags were previously outlawed by the statelessness lint they also needed)*. The test: the whole trail set can be rebuilt bit-identically from one `ALL` notification and `present_time`. Control-manifest lint line: "this system has no block, no hook, no command, no light node, no `true_value()` call, and no member that survives a Graph step except pooled node references, the last poll and `diag_latch`." *Why:* the Pipeline's PR-8 rejects exactly a presentation-owned fade countdown on a trail: the moment a trail's visibility depends on presentation state it is no longer the truth channel every hallucination is checked against (`PIPE:198`; P1, P4).

**FT-2 Truth channel.** Every node this system creates carries the TRUTH_PX grade of the Filter's PerceptionTag (registry: `perception_tag`). It reads `SIM` only, never `PERCEPTION_OUT`; it never calls `true_value()` (the Perception Filter is the only non-diagnostic caller); it has no "perceived" variant and no per-player trail. The Perception Filter may neither remove, alter nor add a trail, and this system's pixels are bit-identical with the Filter pass on or off. It works with either mask route the Filter chooses (a second viewport silhouette or per-element decomposition) and does nothing different when the mask is unavailable: in that case the Filter disables its own pass (`PIPE:735`). No sanity stage, setting, verb or hallucination class changes any trail property. *Why:* P4 "no lie is unfalsifiable"; the closed consequence-channel list (`LSG:168`); PR-10 (`PIPE:206`).

**FT-3 Descriptor-only drawing; one trail per source.** Every drawn element sits at a descriptor's anchor on a floor, wall face, seam, opening frame or fitting. No descriptor, no element. A source (one descriptor, keyed by the identity tuple `(subject_kind, subject_id, mode, medium, anchor)`) produces **one continuous trail, thickest at the source and never appearing at a node without upstream evidence on the connecting edge**. Two descriptors with different `mode` **or different `medium`** on one anchor draw as two trails in descriptor order (ascending `subject_id`, then `mode`, then `medium`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the tuple omitted `medium`, so a `26 SEAL_STRAIN` GAS and HEAT pair on one hatch would have merged into one trail)*. No orphan trail can occur: ambient decorative mist, steam or decals that are not Graph faults are not drawn by this system and Derelict Authoring may not place them. *Why:* "an orphan trail cannot occur" (`LSG:970`-`LSG:977`; `ART:669`; P1).

**FT-4 What this system never draws.** No meter, schematic, minimap, loop summary, numeric leak rate, health bar, damage number, hit flash, icon layer, beacon or alarm colour; nothing on a crewmate (Suit and HUD own body cues); nothing on a machine body sprite, gauge or inspect card (the module-body channel, FT-24); no web-grammar footprint (Horror Entities); no sound, and no gating on sound (Adaptive Audio leads, `IDX:41`); nothing that shows or hides Attention (P3). *Why:* `LSG:1003`; `ART:44`-`ART:55`; the anti-pillars.

#### 2. Signal-to-visual contract

**FT-5 Signal roles.** The three descriptor fields each drive one visual property, as the Graph documents them: `sev` selects the stamp size and the behaviour band; `intensity_pm` selects **density** (frame count, drip cadence, mist coverage as an ordered dither of 2x2-px cells, never partial alpha); the trail's **age** selects **extent** and **accumulation** (frost reach, scorch darkening, dullness): extent and accumulation read `grow_steps` and dullness reads `mode_steps`, the two per-mode age fields the Graph adopted as GA-T (LS-14; FT-8, FT-30); the Graph's `since_steps` counts from the first sev >= 1 descriptor or from `fail_since_step`, which is not the age of the current trail (the review finding behind GA-T), and is read only for the non-dominant seam weep, whose onset it counts truly (FT-8) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: GA-T is adopted in LS-14)*. A sev 3+ accent at the anchor (a wet collar, a heavy frost collar, a deep scorch pit) is drawn regardless of flow, so a pipe at hp 3999 with a trickle reads "badly damaged, barely flowing" and not "big leak" (exception: a gas leak at zero flow has no pixels at all, FT-18). Because `intensity_pm` is bucketed to 100 permille by the Graph, a density change always coincides with an `F/` key (D.4). *Why:* `LSG:158`-`LSG:159` document these roles; the fairness gate counts `sev` (`LSG:144`), so size tracks the Graph-guaranteed tell (P2).

**FT-6 Size ladder.** `trail_size` (D.1): **s (48 base px) at sev 1-2, m (96) at sev 3, l (144) at sev 4**; sev 0 draws nothing. Inside s there are two sub-tiers, *trace* (sev 1) and *full* (sev 2), so a sev 1 -> 2 step is still visible without a fourth size. For the integrity behaviour the sizes are the art bible's progression: s = **hairline**, m = **crack**, l = **bent rib or hole** (sev 4 = `A_hole` >= 32, `LSG:365`). Size is never produced by scaling a sprite (FT-22); it is a different drawing at the same pixel density. Size is a function of `sev` only (the single exception is the PUDDLE descriptor, which the Graph states at `sev` 1 but whose size follows its volume tier, D.2c), so a size change always coincides with an `F/` or `N/` key and needs no polling. **The sev-0 seam exception is retired** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the Graph now states the seam weep as a `SEAM` descriptor, mode `1 LEAK`, `medium` GAS, `sev` 1 while `A_seam` > 0 (`LSG:170`), so a physically leaking weld seam draws at s-*trace* by the ordinary size table and no special case remains in D.1, D.2 or AC-11. The s/m/l semantics are **defined by this GDD** because the art bible names "the three sizes of 3.3 and 6.3" (`ART:1043`) and neither section defines them; the art-director ratified them with the trace/full amendment. Within one trail the chain tapers: stamp ordinal i from the source uses size index `max(1, size_idx - floor(i / TAPER_STAMPS))` (D.1; integer division), where `TAPER_STAMPS` **[PLACEHOLDER: `TAPER_STAMPS`]** (default 1) is the number of run stamps laid per size tier before the chain steps down one tier, so a larger knob tapers *slower*; the source is the thickest point. *Why:* m first appears at sev 3, which is `ALARM_SEV_MIN` 3 (`LSG:901`, Graph GDD), so the physical channel visibly escalates at or before the alarm ("alarm never first", LS-13 `LSG:144`, R17; P2); large stamps stay rare, which is the draw budget (`PIPE:616`).

**FT-7 Which size cells can be reached (finding, no new rule).** By the Graph's D.4 (`LSG:365`; worked example `LSG:381`, which gives hp 5000 -> sev 2 and a patched hp 6000 -> sev 1) a subject at `hp` just below `LEAK_HP` 4000 (`LSG:127`, Graph GDD) is sev 3 (`SEV_STEP` 1500); SEVERED is forced `sev = max(sev, 3)` (`LSG:367`, `LSG:240`). So a **live `1 LEAK` descriptor (GAS, COOLANT or FUEL), a `2 SHORT` and a forced `8 SEVERED` are always sev >= 3 at birth**: they draw at m or l and never at s. The s cells are reached only by pre-stage and ambient descriptors: `7 OVERLOAD` (sev 1-2), the `4 FREEZE` pre-stage (sev 1), the `5 BREACH` pre-stage hairline (sev 1-2, mode-5 STRUCT, confirmed by the Graph, `LSG:170`; pillar-critical for Pillar 2, see FT-32), `3 OVERHEAT`'s HOT shimmer (sev 2), `20 ATMO_COLD` (sev 2), `26 SEAL_STRAIN` (sev 1), the **seam weep** (`1 LEAK/GAS` on a SEAM, sev 1: the s-mist *trace* cell), `27 PATCH_WEEP` (sev 1 only: the s-*trace* cell of its medium's row, no new asset; *Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review*) and, by volume tier rather than by `sev`, a small `25 PUDDLE` (s-puddle, D.2c). **Consequence for the asset list:** author s-frost, s-scorch, s-hairline and s-strain **and s-mist (trace and full) and s-puddle** for the MVP (the earlier deferral waited on the Graph stating the seam-weep severity, which it now does). A live node `1 LEAK` (GAS, COOLANT or FUEL) is still always sev >= 3 at birth, so s-mist is reached only by the seam weep. *Why:* the pre-stage descriptors are the Graph's fairness tell (`LSG:127`); their trails are the s hairlines the player must learn to read. A wearing LEAK's own pre-stage is the WORN descriptor, which is module-body and draws nothing here (FT-32); a *patched* subject's pre-stage is `27 PATCH_WEEP`, which does. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

**FT-8 Extent is a pure function, bounded twice.** The drawn length of a trail along its edge (D.2 `trail_extent`) is the **minimum of three things, never less than `MIN_EXTENT_PX`** (an m or l head stamp is revealed by D.10 like every other stamp, so at `grow_steps` 0 a sev 4 head shows 48 of its 144 px, threshold 42 of 127, and a sev 3 head 48 of 96, threshold 63; this is the intended "it begins" read and not an undrawn source): the physical edge length; the **age bound** `GROW_PX_PER_STEP[sev] x grow_steps` (clamped by `SINCE_CLAMP`), where **`grow_steps` is the trail's own age: the steps since the descriptor entered its current mode or last changed `sev`, frozen at the isolation step while a closed valve isolates it** (both are descriptor fields the Graph derives from three persisted onset fields, `mode_since_step`, `mode_key` and `isolated_at_step`, adopted as **GA-T** in LS-14, FT-30; they are not the Graph's `since_steps`, which counts from `fail_since_step` for a FAILED node or edge and from `warn_since_step` otherwise, so a LEAK that was a WORN node for half an hour would otherwise arrive fully grown, and every endogenous fault would arrive with at least the 100 fairness-gate steps already counted, which is 600 px at sev 3 and 1500 px at sev 4); and, for `4 FREEZE` only, the **temperature bound** `reach_px` (D.3 `frost_reach`; the whole edge for every other row; **for `1 LEAK/COOLANT` the temperature no longer limits the extent: it limits only how much of the run is drawn as frost, `frost_px`, D.2d**, user ruling D26) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. All arithmetic is integer; the result is floored to base px. A live descriptor therefore always draws the first `MIN_EXTENT_PX` 48 [PLACEHOLDER: `MIN_EXTENT_PX`] base px of its source stamp on its first step. **A severity change restarts the age** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; resolves the "sev rise redraws hundreds of pixels of history in one frame" finding by the onset request; restart-at-source is kept as a default applied 2026-10-27, pending user confirmation, Open Questions 6c: neither the one-frame jump to the old age bound nor an `EXTENT_JUMP_MAX` cap is adopted)*: when `sev` changes, the Graph resets the mode onset (GA-T), so `grow_steps` is 0 on the new size's first frame, the new size's head stamp draws at `MIN_EXTENT_PX` and the extent regrows at the new severity's rate. The one-frame event is therefore a size swap and a restart at the source, never an old long mark inflated in one frame; a sev-4 bent rib that begins at the source and races along the rib at 150 px/s is the breach image (ET-3). The cost is that the longer extent of the previous, smaller mark is replaced by the short head of the new one; the descriptor is never undrawn (FT-19), and the head is the source, the thickest point. **Isolation freezes growth:** while a closed valve isolates a flow-fraction descriptor (`intensity_pm` 0 by the closed-valve rule, `LSG:127`) `grow_steps` stops at the isolation step, so a valve closed at `grow_steps` 50 on a sev 3 coolant leak (6 px per step) leaves the extent at 300 px for ever (a `4 FREEZE` sheath can still be pulled back by the temperature bound, D.3; a coolant leak's temperature pulls back only its frost part, D.2d) until the valve reopens (then `grow_steps` resumes from the live `mode_steps`, a one-time extent step bounded by the edge; Edge Cases). **The onsets are the Graph's, dominant-only and keyed (LS-14, adopted 2026-10-27 per the user ruling D30).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* Phase 9 of the Graph keeps one onset pair per NODE, EDGE or SEAM, for the subject's **dominant** descriptor (the live descriptor of highest `sev`, ties to the lowest `mode` id), under the persisted key `mode_key` = `(mode << 3) | sev` (0 when no descriptor has `sev` >= 1): on each run, if the key computed from that run's state differs from the persisted `mode_key`, the Graph sets `mode_since_step` = `step_no`, `mode_key` = the new key and, when `isolated_at_step` > 0, `isolated_at_step` = `step_no`. **Consequence for seeding:** a seed that sets `mode_since_step` without the matching `mode_key` is overwritten on the first run and the trail is born at `grow_steps` 0 (a 48 px head, fresh colour), which would break the cryo-bay wake read and the ET-4 verdict; every fixture and every authored fault therefore seeds all three persisted fields (`FX-CRYO`: `mode_key` 11, FT-40; Onboarding and Derelict Authoring: FT-30, Open Questions 24 and 25; AC-171, AC-180). **Dormancy:** the Graph's M19 advances `mode_since_step` and a non-zero `isolated_at_step` of every subject in a section by the dormant interval on resume (LS-17), so `grow_steps` and `mode_steps` count only the steps the section ran; this system reads the result and adds nothing. **Non-dominant descriptors** (a seam weep beside a BREACH pre-stage; a FREEZE beside a LEAK): the Graph carries no onset for them and hands them the legacy `since_steps` as both fields (LS-14, Graph Open Question 39), which on a first frame can be hundreds of steps and would draw hundreds of px at once, the inflation this rule forbids. **Interim presentation, in force until the Graph publishes per-descriptor onsets for the RUN pairs that can occur (Open Questions 42); stateless, FT-1:** the resolver computes dominance per subject from the descriptor feed by the Graph's own rule, and a non-dominant descriptor draws as follows. A NODE or EDGE descriptor draws its **head stamp only** (extent `MIN_EXTENT_PX`, `dull_step` 0: a constant, so it neither grows nor inflates). The non-dominant **seam weep** (`1 LEAK/GAS` on a SEAM) reads `since_steps`, which the Graph counts from the SeamRecord's `built_step`, a true onset, so it keeps drawing at its true age and does not jump when a BREACH pre-stage of higher `sev` takes dominance. When a NODE or EDGE descriptor becomes dominant the Graph restamps its onset to that step (the key changes), so it regrows from the head it already shows: no frame shrinks and none inflates; a seam weep that returns to dominance restamps to its head, a rare, accepted shrink (Open Questions 42). A non-dominant descriptor is never undrawn (FT-19). The pair is tested on the hero seam: `FX-SEAM` with a mode-5 sev 1 and then sev 2 pre-stage added (AC-181). **Render-rate smoothing is off by default** (`TRAIL_EXTENT_SMOOTH` false [PLACEHOLDER]); when on, it interpolates only between the extent at `grow_steps - 1` and at `grow_steps`, evaluated from the committed tick and `tick_alpha`, never extrapolating (`KERN:298`); it needs no stored previous value (D.2) and it lags the committed value by up to one Graph step (0.1 s). Because the age fields dirty no key (`LSG:532`), extent is read from the per-step poll cache each frame (FT-15). *Why:* a pure function is restore-safe, MIRROR-safe, reduced-motion-safe and testable; a presentation grow timer is the rejected alternative of PR-8 (FT-1).

**FT-9 Growth invariants.** `GROW_PX_PER_STEP[sev]` is an **integer** (base px per Graph step; the pipeline places by `floor`). It must satisfy: (i) `GROW_PX_PER_STEP[1]` >= 1, so a sev-1 trail crosses a six-tile room (288 base px) in at most 288 steps = 28.8 s, inside the 30 s tell ceiling the Pipeline routed to this system (`PIPE:1081`, item 15); (ii) **`V_WALK` 2 px/tick (12 px per Graph step) < `GROW_PX_PER_STEP[4]` <= `V_RUN` 3 px/tick (18 px per Graph step)**, so a sev-4 single-edge trail is strictly faster than walking and no faster than running; (iii) monotone non-decreasing in `sev`. Placeholder table **[PLACEHOLDER: `GROW_PX_PER_STEP`]**: sev 1 = 2, sev 2 = 3, sev 3 = 6, sev 4 = 15 px per step (20 / 30 / 60 / 150 px/s); a 288 px crossing then takes 144, 96, 48 and 20 steps (14.4, 9.6, 4.8 and 2.0 s). Strict reading flagged for tuning: at the 100-step fairness floor (`W_MIN_WARNING_S` 10; the pre-stage descriptor's own `grow_steps` counts from its onset, so the 100 steps are the trail's steps, GA-T being adopted in LS-14) a pure sev-1 trail at 2 px per step has covered 200 px of 288, so invariant (i) holds but a "complete by 10 s" reading needs `GROW_PX_PER_STEP[1]` = 3 (300 px at 100 steps); the acceptance test will state which reading it checks (Open Questions). *Why:* "a failure trail propagating along a graph edge faster than you can walk" (`ART:224`); P2. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**FT-10 The cascade front owns the other half of the breach image.** The Graph lights successive edges one hop per run (`LSG:129`, LS-12; at most `MAX_SPREAD_PER_STEP` 32 new failures per run), each edge's trail starting at its own `grow_steps` 0. **No propagation animation state exists in this system**; the front's speed is the Graph's. The breach image is therefore true twice: single-edge sev-4 growth (FT-9 ii) and the cascade front. The art-bible carried element is satisfied by both. *Why:* `ART:224`; scope guard against absorbing the Graph. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**FT-11 The coolant frost fan and the coolant wet run are unconditional; the frost reach is not.** A `1 LEAK/COOLANT` descriptor always carries a static crystalline frost fan (a local stamp at the anchor and on the lagging) **at every temperature**, plus the drip; it draws **no drifting mist** (mist is O2's word). The fan's *spread as frost* along the edge and over the room's surfaces is bounded by the temperature (D.2d `frost_px`, FT-8): D.3 `frost_reach` maps the room's true `T` (polled from `compartment()`) to a maximum reach, with knots at the band edges [PLACEHOLDER: `FROST_REACH_PX`] (COMFORT and warmer: local stamp only; COOL/FROST edge: long; FROST/FREEZING edge: longer; FREEZING/DEEP_COLD edge: the whole edge) and **linear interpolation inside a band** [PLACEHOLDER: `FROST_REACH_INTERP` true; false restores a pure per-band step table], so frost stops growing when the band leaves FROST and retreats continuously as the heater warms the bay, with no stored state (the cryo-bay case, `LSG:839`); **what the retreating frost leaves behind is the wet run, not bare floor** (the paragraph below). **Knob constraint (fixture floor):** with interpolation the reach at the cryo-bay fixture band sits near the warm end of FROST, so `FROST_REACH_PX` must satisfy `frost_reach(T = -2000 mC)` >= 336 px (the far wall of `ART:142`, seven tiles); otherwise the far-wall frost of `ART:142` / `LSG:839` is unreachable whatever `mode_since_step` the fixture authors (*Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the fixture authors the mode onset, FT-8, FT-40*). The placeholder knots give 544 px at -2000 mC (D.3). **The coolant wet run (user ruling D26, Option A; Open Questions 6a).** At every temperature the chain that carries the frost fan continues as a **coolant wet run** out to the age-bound extent: the extent of D.2 has no temperature term for this row, `frost_px = min(extent, frost_reach(T))` of it is drawn as frost (the head fan stamp, always, then frost run stamps) and the rest, from the end of the last frost stamp to the extent, is drawn as wet-run pieces on a 48 px pitch whose width steps down with distance from the source (D.2d: 8, 6, 4, 2 px, each width over a quarter of the edge). The wet run is a static stamp, not a moving medium: a Void Steel body with a 2 px Coolant Ice edge line on one long side (no body at 2 px; the *dried* 2 px piece is drawn as the 1 px Hull Grey run plus 1 px Void Steel shadow line of FT-22, still 2 px wide, V/A.3) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, parallel-edged, with no spur, no fork, no blob, no glint speck, no iridescence and no angle. It is told from frost by having no crystalline branching; from a fuel puddle by hue (an Ice edge, not an Ember edge), by running as a strip of parallel edges and by having no blob or glint; from a hairline by a width of at least 2 px with an Ice edge and no fork; and from Horror's wet iridescent smear (`ART:565`) by being non-iridescent and edge-following from a coolant fitting with the frost fan at its source (FT-31). It sits on the band of the part of the polyline it follows (floor marks z +1, or the Y-sorted wall layer for a wall part), under the frost stamps; it dulls like every static stamp (D.5; its dried form is the "dried coolant run" of FT-12), and it freezes with the extent at the isolation step (FT-8). Frost and wet run never double a stamp: one chain, one piece per slot (D.2d, D.9 unchanged). *Why:* the followable gradient "thickest at the source" that FT-3 and Player Fantasy corollary 2 promise must hold at COMFORT, where the home ship spends most of its time, not only in FROST. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* **Room frost with no leak** (`20 ATMO_COLD`, row 20) is anchored at a **room-fixed tile** (the lowest `(row, col)` floor tile of `room_surface`, LY-23, that touches an exposed hull face, else the lowest `(row, col)` floor tile of the room; a function of Layout geometry alone, so a leak appearing or clearing at constant `T` moves no patch; the earlier "grows from the coldest leak" anchor relocated up to 24 patches when a leak toggled and had no tiebreak among several openings, and is withdrawn, Open Questions 6h) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, and its spread is the D.2b coverage ramp of the room's true `T` (0 at the COOL/FROST edge, full at the FROST/FREEZING edge; no age term and not the D.3 reach), *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(orchestrator ruling: the Graph's informative map reads "LEAK/COOLANT -> drip + mist, frost below 0 C" (`LSG:166`), and the Graph itself calls the map Failure & Trail's; shape redundancy outranks an informative table, because the colourblind backbone "O2 = drifting mist, coolant = static crystalline" (`ART:476`) must hold on the home-ship at COMFORT, where the game spends most of its time. Both clauses of the Graph's parenthetical, "+ mist" and "frost below 0 C", are therefore not rendered as written; a one-line informative rewording is routed to the Graph owner, non-blocking. Interpolation inside a band avoids a retreat jump of up to 480 px at a band key; the toggle exists so the literal per-band table can be restored.)* *Why:* P1 greyscale and colourblind redundancy (`ART:47`, `ART:314`, `ART:476`).

**FT-12 Hue from the loop, shape from the medium; the verdict grammar.** Each descriptor's colour is its **loop's hue** and its shape is its **medium's behaviour word**: mist (O2 loop), frost (coolant loop and cold surfaces), scorch (power), puddle (fuel), hairline -> crack -> bent rib (integrity). `medium` HEAT (OVERHEAT and SCRAM) has no behaviour row in the art bible and takes the hue of **the descriptor's `loop` field**, which the Graph now states (`LSG:170`): an OVERHEAT carries loop 3 (COOLANT) on a REACTOR or RESERVOIR and loop 2 (POWER) on a LOAD machine; a SCRAM carries 3 when tripped by OVERHEAT and 4 (FUEL) when tripped by fuel starve. The rim hue is therefore loop 2 Power Amber, loop 3 Coolant Ice, loop 4 Fuel Ember, chosen by a lookup on the field and by nothing else (so a reactor is rimmed Coolant Ice after an overheat trip and Fuel Ember after a starve trip, and an OVERHEAT on a fuel tank does not occur); no heat hue is invented *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the rule was written on the subject kind and disagreed with the Graph's loop provenance; the resolver now keys on `loop`, which makes the hue testable headless)*. **`4 FREEZE` of any medium draws the same frost sheath in Coolant Ice** (frost is the material of cold, as for `20 ATMO_COLD`); a frozen fuel tank (`medium` FUEL, `LSG:170`) is told from a frozen coolant pipe by the absence of a fan and a drip and by its subject (a tank fitting), and no coolant fan is ever drawn on it (Edge Cases); its thaw-burst, if any, is a new `1 LEAK/FUEL` descriptor drawn by that row. Compartment descriptors carry no loop: `20 ATMO_COLD` frost uses the coolant-ice hue because frost is the material of cold (shape, not loop, carries the meaning); `21 ATMO_HOT` soot and `24 AIRFLOW` dust are hue-less. Every behaviour carries **both contrast polarities** (a light element and a dark element) so it reads on lit and mid floor tones; scorch is a dark radial core with a loop-hue rim. The five behaviours stay distinct in greyscale and in a still frame: *mist drifts* (light, round clusters, no straight edge, no angle); *frost is static and crystalline* (lightest, hex-stair spurs branching at 60 degrees, no curve); *scorch is sharp and radial* (dark, straight spokes from one core, uneven lengths, an origin); *a puddle is glossy and flat* (dark, one convex blob, glint specks, no spokes); *a hairline runs along a rib or seam, never from a point* (mid plus dark pair, at most one fork per 16 px). Frost has one dendrite motif in three layouts: a **fan** (radial, at a coolant fitting or lagged elbow: `1 LEAK/COOLANT`), a **crust** (a linear run along an edge: `20 ATMO_COLD`, hatch rim frost, a dried coolant run) and a **sheath** (a continuous thick wrap over a pipe's lagging: `4 FREEZE`), so a fan at a fitting is coolant, a crust with no fitting is cold, and a sheath with no drip and no fan origin is a frozen pipe. **Scorch with a radial origin is a power arc; soot without an origin is heat** (`21 ATMO_HOT` draws a soot wash, not a scorch). Palette, reserved hues and exact values are in Visual/Audio. *Why:* `ART:314` (five behaviours), `ART:697` (verdict grammar), `ART:309` and `ART:399` (scorch dark, in its hue), checklist 13 (`ART:1216`, a trail out-contrasts every floor it crosses).

**FT-13 Wall mark plus floor run: one trail.** A descriptor on a wall-mounted fitting renders one continuous trail with a **wall part** (the Y-sorted wall layer, at the host face's sort origin) and a **floor part** (the floor-marks band) whose start point and direction Layout supplies (Layout `anchor_geometry`, LY-23, designed). Both parts share the single extent of FT-8, measured along the combined polyline, wall part first. Wall cells are 48, 96 and 144 px wide and at most 72 px tall (a wall front face is 72 px, `ART:1033`); floor cells are square. No opaque trail pixel may lie outside its own compartment's floor tiles or its host face: head and source stamps may fill a full cell, body stamps are at most a 48 px lateral strip at m and l, and the Layout clearance read is designed (LY-8 d, FT-37). *Why:* "one trail per source" (FT-3) and the eye line trail -> source -> hatch (`ART:704`).

**FT-14 Seam weep along the bead.** A SEAM subject's descriptor renders the medium's trail **along the raised Hull Grey weld bead** in the Y-sorted layer at the seam's host-face origin: mist along the bead for GAS, rim frost for HEAT, a bowed rim for SEAL_STRAIN on a seam hatch; its extent runs along the seam line per FT-8. The Graph **states the seam weep's descriptor** (`LSG:170`): `subject_kind` SEAM, `mode` `1 LEAK`, `medium` GAS, `acoustic` HISS, `sev` 1 while `A_seam` > 0, `intensity_pm` per LS-14 field provenance (never 0 while the weep physically flows, so never silenced by the closed-valve rule; the fixtures take `A_seam` 1 as `intensity_pm` 15, as given, and restate no expression) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, and counts its `since_steps` from the SeamRecord's `built_step`, a true onset, so the weep grows from the weld at the sev-1 rate with no GA-T dependency. The weep therefore draws at s-*trace* (sev 1) by the ordinary size table; there is no PROVISIONAL tag left on this row *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. **Legibility floor:** at `A_seam` 1 the density table gives `density_level` 1 and `cover16` 4 of 16, which on a 16 px puff lit only by the chest lamp can read as "nothing is wrong" on the first weld the player inspects; the seam mist therefore carries `COVER16_FLOOR` 8 (D.4), a minimum of 8 of 16 cells whenever the seam descriptor is drawn, and the still-image criterion AC-151 asserts that a reviewer identifies "mist along a seam" at `A_seam` 1, `k_auto`, lit. *Why:* the weld is hero shape #3 and "the ship's first failure point"; the carried element "a failure trail spreading along a weld seam while you watch" (`ART:166`); `LSG:228` must-prove (P5, P2).

#### 3. Time, keys and reconcile

**FT-15 Keys wake, polls move.** A notification handler stores the key set and returns; it never reads or draws (`PIPE:190`, PR-8 rule 3). In the next render callback the system (1) re-reads `failures()` and `compartment()` (and, for the provisional rows of FT-30, `node()` and `edge()`, whose continuous `flow` and machine `T` dirty no key) **once per Graph step** into the per-step poll cache (the Graph runs once per `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6 ticks = 0.1 s; `since_steps` dirties no key, `LSG:532`), (2) spawns, despawns and re-selects size and behaviour on `F/` and `N/`, band accents on `B/` and `C/`, and rebuilds anchors on `T` and `SEC/`, and (3) every frame reads extent, dullness and animation phase from the poll cache and `present_time`. The key-family reaction table is below. **Step detection, key union and overflow** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; these were unspecified)*: a Graph step is detected by comparing the read view's `step_no` (the counter `since_steps` is defined against, LS-14) with the `step_no` stored in the poll cache; the poll runs on a difference and on no other condition. Notifications arrive once per tick (P5) and the render callback may run less often than the kernel ticks (30 Hz rendering, a hitch), so the handler **unions** every key set received since the last callback (a set, no duplicates, order irrelevant: a spawn-then-despawn pair within the union is resolved by the poll, which is the truth); if the union grows past `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256, or an `ALL` is among the notifications, the callback treats the union as `ALL` (FT-16). *Why:* LS-20 (`LSG:201`) says keys wake discrete presentation only; PR-8 rule 3 and the kernel's CR-10.

**FT-16 `ALL` reconciles in one frame.** On `ALL` (a restore, a derelict instantiation, or a cascade above `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256, `KERN:466`) the system performs **one full re-read reconciled against its node pool within the same render frame**, never spread over frames. Two logging thresholds apply, **neither a cut-off**, exactly as `STEP_BUDGET_MS` (4 ms) is for the kernel: `TRAIL_RESTORE_BUDGET_MS` [PLACEHOLDER, 8 ms] for a restore or instantiation (a full rebuild) and `TRAIL_RECONCILE_BUDGET_MS` [PLACEHOLDER, 4 ms] for an in-play `ALL`, which is a diff against the pool (D.7) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the single 2 ms threshold fired on every legal worst-case world)*. After a restore the trail set appears already grown, because extent is a function of `grow_steps` (a persisted-field difference, GA-T) and nothing "starts" on load. The consumer-side cost is D.7 `trail_reconcile_cost`, which cites the registry formula `dirty_key_budget` by name and never restates its expression. *Why:* the MVP worst case is about 38 keys and about 17 descriptors (`LSG:532`), so one-frame reconciliation is cheap and keeps "never suppressed" (FT-19) testable. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**FT-17 LIVE / STILL and dullness are two orthogonal axes.** **LIVE vs STILL** is `intensity_pm` > 0 vs = 0 (for the ten ids with no Graph `intensity_pm`, the per-row LIVE predicate of FT-30 and D.4 in its place; a row with no LIVE axis at all is DRAWN_STATIC, States and Transitions): STILL stops the drip and the moving-medium frames **and freezes the extent at the isolation step** (FT-8, GA-T); the static stamps stay and draw at the authored coverage of their size for the current `sev` (the system remembers no earlier density, FT-1, so "held" means "does not animate", not "recalled"). **Dullness** is a quantised, palette-locked ramp of the trail's mode age `mode_steps` (D.5 `dullness`, `DULL_STEPS` [PLACEHOLDER], `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS` [PLACEHOLDER]; *Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: it ramped on `since_steps`, so a pipe that wore for 30 minutes and then leaked was born at the dried stop*) applied to **static stamps only**: a frost fan goes from bright to a greyed fan, a scorch from rimmed to matte, a puddle to a stain. Moving media (drip, mist, debris) are never dulled, so a live leak keeps looking wet. There is **no third "DRIED" state**: a derelict's authored dead fault (`intensity_pm` 0, large back-dated `mode_steps`, `LSG:226`) and a home-ship leak isolated by a closed valve (`intensity_pm` 0, mode kept, `LSG:127`) obey the same STILL rule. With GA-T the extent freezes at the isolation step while dullness keeps counting `mode_steps`, so a leak isolated long ago looks old and one isolated a moment ago looks fresh, which is honest. **A live trail never shows the dried verdict through the Graph's own clocks:** the dried stop is reached only at `dull_pm` 1000 (D.5), that is `mode_steps` >= `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS` >= 7500, and `mode_steps` restarts at every mode entry and every sev change. One residual case remained: a leak isolated early, left, and reopened after `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS` steps is live with a dried-stop age. **Default applied 2026-10-27, pending user confirmation (Open Questions 6d): a LIVE descriptor is capped at `DULL_STEPS` - 2** (D.5 `dull_step_shown`), so no live trail wears the dried look at any age or through any clock; the cap releases on the frame the descriptor stops being LIVE, so the isolation lands the dried verdict in one palette step (intended: the leak is old and the valve is the player's own act; Edge Cases, AC-177) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. **The cap also reaches the ongoing rows with no flow axis** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; the cap's own intent, no default of Open Questions 6d is changed)*: a `7 OVERLOAD` and a `27 PATCH_WEEP` descriptor exists only while the cable is over its rating or the patch holds, so its presence is its liveness. They have no LIVE/STILL axis, so before this amendment they sat in DRAWN_STILL, were never capped and showed the dried verdict after `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS` steps of an unbroken condition, the very look the cap forbids. They are now DRAWN_STATIC and are held at stop `DULL_STEPS` - 2 for as long as the descriptor exists, with no release (when the condition ends the descriptor ends: T5). **A `4 FREEZE` and a `9 SCRAM` are not capped, because each is a latched fault and not an ongoing condition:** a forced-FAILED FREEZE persists until thaw and a SCRAM until `reset_node`, so a frozen coolant run or a scrammed reactor on a derelict is a dead fault and its greyed sheath or matte scorch is its verdict (ET-4). Derelict Authoring's `pa_frozen` is authored as a forced-FREEZE dead fault that must read dried (DA-17, lint `DEAD_FAULT_NOT_DRIED`), and a cap on row 4 would make that lint meaningless; the re-review's wish that a live FREEZE never show the dried look is therefore declined for FREEZE (a pipe frozen for `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS` unbroken steps is a latched failure and its old look is honest) and kept for OVERLOAD and PATCH_WEEP. Rows 20 and 21 carry no age, so there is nothing to cap (D.5, AC-177). Fuel is the exception by medium: the pool follows `fuel_puddle_ml` (row 25), shrinking only as the Graph's puddle does, and the fuel stain's dullness is a **palette swap at the final stop only (`fuel_dried` is true only when `dull_step` is the last stop, D.5), with no intermediate stop and no dither** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: it flipped at half the ramp, so a live fuel leak could read dried at 6000 steps)*, because any mix of the fuel hue with a grey passes through the flesh band the sane world reserves for sanity (Visual/Audio). `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS` must be at least 7500 steps (12.5 min, the longest live MVP clock, `LSG:667` D.15) so no live trail reaches the dried look before its fault finishes. *Why:* three discrete states would need a presentation timer to tell STOPPED from DRIED (FT-1); the art bible chose one shader with state from the mask (`ART:1065`-`ART:1067`).

| Axis state | `intensity_pm` | `mode_steps` (age of the current mode, GA-T) | Draws |
|---|---|---|---|
| LIVE, fresh | > 0 | small | stamp chain growing at `GROW_PX_PER_STEP[sev]` from the source, moving media at `density_level`, full-colour static stamps |
| LIVE, aged | > 0 | large | as above, static stamps dulled by D.5 (never to the dried stop before `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS`); the drip still wet (the moving medium is never dulled) |
| STILL, fresh | 0 | small | stamp chain only at the extent frozen at the isolation step, full colour; the valve was closed moments ago ("the drip stops, the frost stops growing": the extent is capped at `grow_steps` frozen at the isolation step, FT-8) |
| STILL, aged | 0 | large | stamp chain only at the frozen extent, dulled; an old isolated leak |
| STILL, dried (derelict) | 0 | authored very large (back-dated mode onset) | the verdict: greyed fan, matte scorch, ember-dim stain; **GAS rows have no STILL form** (FT-18) |
| No flow axis (DRAWN_STATIC: rows 4, 7, 9, 27) | n/a (reads 0 and means nothing) | any | the static stamp at the authored coverage for the current `sev`, dulled by D.5; rows 7 and 27 are an ongoing condition and are held at stop `DULL_STEPS` - 2, rows 4 and 9 are latched faults and are not *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the table previously promised "the frost stops growing" while D.2 kept growing without an isolation term.)*

**FT-18 A gas leak at zero flow is empty by design.** `1 LEAK/GAS` (and `8 SEVERED/GAS`) at `intensity_pm` 0 draws **zero pixels**: "O2 failure leaves no trail (mist evaporates), read from absence" (`ART:697`), and no residue accent exists for GAS (a stain would suggest a false O2 history on a derelict). A sev-3 trickle therefore reads "wide, sparse plume". This GDD **reads the Graph's lint** (`LSG:1110`, which scans for a consumer that "suppresses a consequence-channel descriptor whose sev >= 1 from its physical channel") as: *no consequence-channel descriptor with `medium` != NONE and sev >= 1 may be hidden from its physical channel while it has flow; an empty STILL GAS plume is not suppression, because the physical flow is zero.* The reading is this GDD's, not the Graph's, and is listed in Open Questions as routed to the Graph owner (non-blocking). **`27 PATCH_WEEP` on a GAS subject (a patched duct or O2 tank) is the second drawn-and-empty case** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G2; creative-director ruling on the specialist disagreement)*: it draws zero pixels, because the Graph states that a PATCH_WEEP carries no flow (LS-11) and a dry-seam residue is rejected (it would suggest a false O2 history on a derelict, the reason this rule exists, and it contradicts "read from absence"). The consequence-channel tell is the **HISS** that Adaptive Audio plays at the descriptor's anchor (FT-35), and it is not suppressed, so R17 (no consumer may suppress a consequence-channel descriptor's acoustic or physical channel) is met acoustically. Recorded as the explicit FT-18 note: the Graph owner is asked to confirm that reading (Open Questions 43); this system never draws a GAS weep mark. *Why:* the verdict grammar (P5); `ART:697`.

**FT-19 Never cull an on-screen consequence-channel element.** Every descriptor with `medium` != NONE and sev >= 1 **whose trail's bounding box lies inside or crosses the camera field at the current ladder step k** has its stamps drawn that frame (subject to FT-18). The test is geometric and is made on the **polyline bounding box padded by one head cell** (the `STAMP_PX` of the trail's head size, 48, 96 or 144 px) intersected with the field, **not on the anchor point** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: an anchor-point test let a trail whose source sits off-screen but whose body crosses the view pop in as the source scrolled on, which is the suppression this rule forbids)*. Cost pressure is relieved first by reducers that change no pixel (FT-41), then by degrading moving media, in this order: frame count, then density, then `TRAIL_ANIM_FPS` halving, then far-from-beam frame hold; **never stamps**. Not drawing is permitted only for trails whose padded bounding box lies wholly outside the field (re-evaluated at every k, so a zoom-out reveals every trail), dormant sections (they emit no descriptors, `LSG:822`) and sev 0. Distance culling and darkness culling are forbidden: they are exactly the suppression the Graph's lint scans for (`LSG:1110`). *Why:* P1 via `LSG:144` R17 (no consumer may suppress a consequence-channel descriptor's physical channel).

**FT-20 Pure variation.** Stamp variant, drip phase offset and fan flip come from `rng_derive(world_seed, "trail.vis", (subject_id << 20) | stamp_index)` (D.6), the pure hash the kernel defines (`KERN:470`, D.10); the kernel's H9 restricts *simulation* from wall-clock and global randomness, and a presentation caller of a pure hash violates nothing. `world_seed` is read as exposed through **the kernel's read view of `kernel.clock`** (`KERN:477`, `KERN:844`; CR-12 makes every `SIM` block readable by presentation; the confirmation that `kernel.clock` is registered class `SIM` is routed to the kernel owner, non-blocking). If that read is not available, the fallback `TRAIL_VIS_SEED_FALLBACK` 0 [PLACEHOLDER] is identical on every client and after every restore, only less varied. Engine global random functions and any random-number-generator instance are forbidden in this system (lint). *Why:* two crewmates must see the same frost fan (P1); a restore redraws the same trail (`LSG:829`).

#### 4. Look: technique, bands, pixels

**FT-21 Static stamps and stepped frames.** Static marks (frost fan, crust and sheath, scorch, glossy streak, puddle, hairline, crack and bent rib, bowed rim, rim frost, soot wash, and any dulled form) are **stamps** from the three-size sheets. Moving media (mist drift, drip, vent debris, wind streaks, AIRFLOW dust, arc pops, shimmer) are **pooled stepped sprite frames** at `TRAIL_ANIM_FPS` 12 [PLACEHOLDER] (the horror-rig precedent of 12 fps stepped, `PIPE:202`), with frame index from `present_time` (D.8), never from wall-clock or the tick counter (PR-8 rule 6). Particle systems are permitted **only** for venting at a BREACH or AIRFLOW opening, and only after a spike proves (a) per-instance positions floor to the base grid and (b) alpha is binary under nearest sampling (both unverified, HIGH risk); until then venting is stepped frames too.
