The "propagation frames" the art bible lists for the trail sheet are not needed and are dropped from the asset list: a growing front is a reveal threshold (FT-23), not extra drawings. *Why:* binary alpha and integer placement are exactly where particles are weakest; stepped frames satisfy both by construction (FT-22).

**FT-22 Pixel integrity.** Nearest sampling; every item at an integer base-px position (placed by `floor`); **no per-node scale animation of any kind** (growth is stamp count, size swap and the reveal threshold of FT-23); `rotation` 0; hairlines, filaments and seams are **sprites, never runtime line draws** (PR-4 rule 10; the engine's 4.7 line-antialiasing change is moot for exactly this reason); alpha is binary (0 or 255) on every drawn sprite, including mist (a partially transparent mist is not pixel art); palette lock; minimum feature 2x2 px, except that a hairline is a **1 px Hull Grey run paired with a 1 px Void Steel shadow line (a 2 px composite, at least 4 px long)**, which the art-director ratified as a permitted 1 px element joining contour and glyph strokes (the *dried* 2 px coolant wet-run piece uses the same composite, V/A.3; *Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review*), an amendment candidate to the art bible's minimum-feature checklist (routed); no single-pixel noise; no violet and no flesh hue in any trail sheet or any dulled render; the alarm colour, the glowstick light colour and human (Tungsten) light appear in no trail. *Why:* `PIPE:110`-`PIPE:123`; `ART:1088`; `ART:422`-`ART:430`.

**FT-23 Chain, reveal by threshold, density by dither.** A trail is an **anchor cap** at the source, **run** pieces chained along the polyline, a **tip** at the leading edge, **corner** pieces where Layout turns the line, and a **terminal** piece when the extent reaches its bound. The last run piece is revealed up to the extent by comparing its flow-phase ramp channel with an integer threshold (so a frost front is ragged and a scorch front radial with no extra art), never by scaling. Density (FT-5) is a **world-anchored dither of 2x2-px cells**, never partial alpha: the pattern keys on world base-px coordinates so it does not swim with the camera, overlapped stamps agree and every client draws the same pixels. Mechanism (runtime discard by coverage rank vs pre-authored dither sets) is an ADR candidate. *Why:* PR-4 rules 4 and 10; `ART:1065`-`ART:1067`.

**FT-24 Never on a machine body, never on a crewmate.** OVERHEAT and SCRAM scorch, shimmer and glow are drawn at the **descriptor's anchor port or fitting** and on the floor and wall marks around the machine, never as an effect on the machine's body sprite (the corruptible module-body channel). Shimmer is a stepped frame sprite at the fitting; this system has no distortion shader of any kind. A trail is anchored to the fitting or edge, never to a machine's body, so a class (b) hallucination that redraws the body cannot move or hide the mark. *Why:* `LSG:168` (a lie can live only on the body channel); P4.

**FT-25 Draw bands.**

| Element | Band (Pipeline name) | z | Y-sorted | Sort origin |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Floor trails and puddles: frost run and crust, floor scorch, glossy streak, puddle, floor-plate cracks, soot edges | Floor marks | +1 | never | none: drawn flat, order among marks = authoring order; a mark never sorts above an actor standing on it and never below the hull it sits on |
| Wall trails and frame sprites: frost fan on the lagging or elbow, wall scorch, crack and rib along the wall face, hatch rim sprites, soot on lower faces | Wall layer (the one Y-sorted layer) | +10 | yes | the host face's sort origin, so the trail sorts with its face |
| Moving media: mist, drip, vent debris, wind streaks, shimmer, AIRFLOW dust | Dust and particles | +30 | no | "venting mist above a trail, below nothing" |
| Emissive layer (scorch, arc, ember) | shares its host stamp's band | as host | as host | as host |
| Not used | Floor (z 0), wall caps (z +20) | | | |

All bands stay inside every light range limit (`PIPE:132`-`PIPE:139`). Placement by `floor`; all marks sit under the hull-floor material, so an unlit trail never renders below the hull it sits on (`ART:1248`). *Why:* PR-5 band table; `PIPE:773` (hard dependency: layer assignment and nearest sampling that keeps a hairline a hairline).

**FT-26 Lit albedo marks.** Trails are lit marks under the hull-floor material (`max(albedo x ambient, floor tint)`); only scorch, arc and ember carry an emissive layer. In a dark room the trail is not seen, and that is correct: the hiss (Audio), the band cues (Suit) and the draught still tell. Acceptance captures are taken **lit**; the dark-room criterion asserts the descriptor and its acoustic class exist while no *non-emissive* trail pixel exceeds the hull-floor tint (a live emissive element may be visible and is the subject of FT-27). *Why:* "darkness is honest; light is a decision" (P1, P3); `ART:1248`.

**FT-27 No light and no emitter registration.** This system writes no light node and registers no emitter and no exposure. It supplies a **descriptor-to-glow data table** (palette hex, radius in base px, flicker class bounded by FT-29) in Visual/Audio; Emitter State registers the emitter from the same descriptor and Lighting renders it (designed; EM-17). The emissive layer on scorch, arc and ember stamps is an unshaded additive layer that **casts no light**. Whether a visible fault glow counts toward the web's exposure is not an art or presentation claim; it is decided by Emitter State's **EM-17**: "The emissive `_msk` trail glow on a sprite is an *unshaded overlay, not an emitter*, and exposes nothing", while the FAULT_GLOW *emitter* that `emitter.place` derives from the same descriptor "counts as exposure". This GDD supplies the ledger values Emitter State's D.11 multiplies (`GLOW_BASE_PM[key]` and `SEV_GLOW_PM[0..4]`, V/A.6; `EM:543`). *(CP-3, a light written here would illuminate a room without being an emitter the Attention system can see, so the narrower contract wins; EM-17 decides that the derived FAULT_GLOW emitter counts as exposure and the overlay does not.)* *Why:* P3 "emission is exposure".

**FT-28 Reduced motion.** Reduced motion holds every moving medium on its **authored hold frame** (frame 0 of each stepped strip; direction and density kept, so a mist that does not drift is still a mist), while extent, size, density and dullness continue because they are Graph state; `present_time` still advances. It never suppresses a trail, a hairline or audio. Each hold frame must read as its behaviour word in a still image (mist = peak-coverage frame with one wisp leaning along `dir`; drip = a hanging bead with a 2 px streak below it; vent debris = a mid-flight cluster; AIRFLOW dust = directional streaks with a tapered tail; arc = a steady glow at 60 percent of peak with the four static spark-tip pixels lit and no pops; shimmer = a static three-line wave). *Why:* `PIPE:232`, `PIPE:847`: reduced motion "never suppresses trails, tremor, hairlines or audio".

**FT-29 Reduce flashing and the arc number.** Arc and ember flicker is a **number**: `ARC_FLICKER_PER_S` (a derived, read-only value, D.8b, never a knob) <= `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` 3, counted per `flash_count` and `PIPE:228` (opposing >= 10 % luminance changes per rolling 1 s window). **One rate mechanism, one hold level** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; the earlier text divided the rate by the arc count and also put every arc on one shared world phase, two contradictory rules, and gave two hold levels, 60 and 88 percent)*: all live arcs flicker on **one shared world phase at one rate** `ARC_FLICKER_PER_S` (D.8b), so the counted transitions in any region equal one source's whatever the arc count; the rate is not divided by anything and cannot flip when the camera pans. The regional luminance clamp is met by **amplitude**, not by rate: each arc's emissive swing is scaled by the world-fixed arc count of its compartment (D.8b `swing_i_pm`), derived at the smallest ladder region (9,216 px at 384 x 216), so the clamp holds at every field and every pan. "Reduce flashing" replaces flicker by a held glow at the single level `ARC_HELD_GLOW_PM` (D.8b; 60 percent of peak), which is also the reduced-motion hold pose of an arc (FT-28, V/A.5). The light verbs are fixed so a decoy, a flare and an arc cannot be confused: **arcs pop**, deployables flicker, the cryo lamp gutters, pipes glow, fuel edges glint (a static highlight). **Counting unit (ratified 2026-10-27, user ruling D91 ii):** a flash is `ceil(n_transitions / 2)` opposing transitions in the rolling window, the registry `flash_count` expression (D.8b derives the rate from it). **Default (user ruling D79, X10):** "reduce flashing" is ON at first launch, so a first-run player sees the held glow at `ARC_HELD_GLOW_PM`, not arc pops; pops are the opt-out look and every pop capture states the setting (AC-64). **The held pose must still read as a fault, not as a fixture lamp or an OVERLOAD rim** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; D79 is not re-opened, its consequence is answered)*: the user's default means the first thing most players ever see of a `2 SHORT` is the hold pose, so the hold frame carries **four static Power Amber spark-tip pixels** (2 x 2 px tips at the uneven spoke ends of the radial scorch, lit at the hold level) on top of the steady glow, which neither a Tungsten fixture lamp (no spokes, no origin) nor the `7 OVERLOAD` rim (a steady warm rim with no tips) has; the pop strip adds motion to the same tips, so the pop capture and the held capture differ only by motion. An art capture compares the three side by side (V/A.11 item 14, AC-63c). The sub-threshold and exemption weighting of D91 (iv) belongs to the HUD marks; the arc class keeps the strict regional ceiling of D.8b. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *Why:* `PIPE:228` PR-13; `ART:979`; `ART` 3.2 and 2.2 reservations.

#### 5. Provisional rows, footprints, co-op, scale

**FT-30 Descriptor provenance (confirmed by the Graph, `LSG:170`); GA-T and the patched-subject pre-stage are adopted, three of the original five requests remain open and three narrow asks are added.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the Graph's provenance paragraph now answers what this GDD asked on 2026-10-26, so the `PROVISIONAL` tags those questions carried are retired and this rule is reduced to what is still open.)* **Confirmed and in force:** `intensity_pm` is a flow fraction only for LEAK, the hole-area BREACH flow, AIRFLOW, SEVERED (`OPEN_END_RATE_PM` 500 permille of port cap, `LSG:772`), the seam weep and SEAL_STRAIN GAS (each per LS-14 field provenance). For `2 SHORT`, `7 OVERLOAD`, `3 OVERHEAT`, `9 SCRAM`, `4 FREEZE`, `20-23` and `27 PATCH_WEEP` the Graph defines none (it reads 0 and means nothing; Graph LS-14 field provenance lists the same ten), so those ten ids drive density from `sev` (`density_level` = `sev` in D.4), age from the trail age (FT-8), and the relevant true value from `node()` or `compartment()` (loading or flow for electric, `T` for heat and cold); LIVE for them is not `intensity_pm` > 0 but a **per-row LIVE predicate on a true value** read through `node()`, `edge()` or `compartment()` (D.4: `2 SHORT` live while the faulted conductor carries current, `3 OVERHEAT` live while the machine's `T` is above `OVERHEAT_OFF_MC`, cited by name; every other of the ten has no moving medium and is always drawn static, in the DRAWN_STATIC state), so a `2 SHORT` pops while current flows and goes still when the current reaches zero with the descriptor still present, whatever the Graph's `intensity_pm` reads; the static stamp always draws (FT-19) and only the moving media follow the predicate. The mode-5 STRUCT pre-stage and the SEAL_STRAIN HEAT/TICK branch are presented the same way and are never silenced by `intensity_pm` 0. Also confirmed: SEAM `since_steps` counts from the SeamRecord's `built_step`; COMPARTMENT and OPENING descriptors (20-26) carry `since_steps` 0 (so they use no age: D.2b); the seam weep is `1 LEAK/GAS`, sev 1, `intensity_pm` per LS-14 field provenance; `25 PUDDLE` is sev 1, `medium` FUEL, `intensity_pm` per LS-14 field provenance (the volume itself is read from `compartment().fuel_puddle_ml`, D.2c); `24 AIRFLOW` is sev 1 below `WELD_DP_WARN` and 2 at or above; an OVERHEAT carries loop 3 (COOLANT) on a REACTOR or RESERVOIR and 2 (POWER) on a LOAD machine, a SCRAM loop 3 (tripped by OVERHEAT) or 4 (FUEL, tripped by starve); a FREEZE on a fuel tank has `medium` FUEL; the BREACH pre-stage is `mode` 5, `medium` STRUCT, `acoustic` TICK at flow zero, consequence channel and never WORN; a patched subject at `hp` < `HP_OK` carries `27 PATCH_WEEP` (sev 1, `medium` by the subject's loop, `intensity_pm` 0 and never silenced by it) instead of WORN. This GDD never states a formula for the Graph's `intensity_pm` (AC-91): wherever this GDD names what `intensity_pm` measures it says "`intensity_pm` per LS-14 field provenance" and states no expression *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

**Open requests, routed as one narrow amendment to the Graph owner (this GDD edits no Graph file):**

1. **GA-T, the time axis: ADOPTED by the Graph (LS-14, 2026-10-27; user ruling D30); closed.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* The Graph's invariant check found no break (LS-14, "GA-T ... Result: no invariant breaks; GA-T is adopted"), so the contingency of the earlier text has lapsed and the legacy fallback is deleted. The Graph persists **three** onset fields per node, edge and seam, `mode_since_step`, `mode_key` and `isolated_at_step` (LS-21), and derives the two descriptor fields from them: `mode_steps = step_no - mode_since_step` (the age of the dominant descriptor's current mode and severity, restamped whenever `mode_key` = `(mode << 3) | sev` changes) and `grow_steps = max(0, (isolated_at_step if isolated_at_step > 0 else step_no) - mode_since_step)` (`mode_steps` frozen at the isolation step); COMPARTMENT and OPENING descriptors read 0 for both. The rule is **dominant-only**: one onset pair per subject, for its highest-`sev` descriptor (ties to the lowest `mode`), and the interim presentation of a non-dominant descriptor is FT-8's. `SHARED_SUMMARY` carries both ages (LS-21, FT-33). What is owed elsewhere: Derelict Authoring back-dates `mode_since_step`, sets `mode_key` to the fault's key and leaves `isolated_at_step` 0, wherever it back-dates `fail_since_step` (its DA-17 and the lint `DEAD_FAULT_NOT_DRIED` do neither yet: Open Questions 25, and ET-4 with AC-43, AC-44 and AC-171 are blocked at integration until they do: Gaps); the Onboarding fixture seeds all three fields, `mode_key` included (`FX-CRYO`, FT-40; Open Questions 24). The resolver also clamps `grow_steps` at `max(0, ...)`, so the formula is total whatever the Graph persists.
2. **Patched-subject pre-stage: ADOPTED by the Graph as `27 PATCH_WEEP` (LS-11, LS-14; Graph OQ 23 closed by creative-director ruling "a patch weeps"); closed.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* Drawn by row 27, FT-32; the earlier interim ruling (a patch clears the trail the next frame and the subject is WORN) is withdrawn.
3. **Footprint kinds CHILL, BURN and STRESS** have no descriptor (FT-31, VS-A).
4. **Which mode a dead, unpowered electrical fault emits** on a derelict (a `2 SHORT` clears on repair or breaker open, `LSG:127`; a dead cable at `hp` < `HP_OK` with no mode live would emit WORN, which draws nothing), so the "matte scorch" leg of the verdict (ET-4) has a descriptor only if the Graph chooses to emit one.
5. **Whether a consequence-channel sev-1 companion exists for WORN** (a "sweat" on a wearing pipe), which would give the competence paragraph's early read a mechanism. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: a Pillar 2 / Pillar 4 decision owed with the Graph owner, Open Questions 45, no longer a non-blocking footnote; the Player Fantasy lists which failures have a floor pre-stage meanwhile.)*
6. **Per-descriptor onsets for non-dominant RUN pairs** *(re-review)*: the seam weep beside a BREACH pre-stage and a FREEZE beside a LEAK are the pairs that occur; the Graph's dominant-only pair (Graph Open Question 39) leaves them the legacy `since_steps`. Ask: widen the pair to the two highest-severity descriptors, or give each RUN descriptor its own onset. Interim presentation: FT-8 (Open Questions 42).
7. **BREACH hole variant key** *(re-review)*: for `5 BREACH` the Graph states `intensity_pm` as the hole-area fraction (`A_hole` over `A_HOLE_MAX`, LS-14), so the rib-versus-hole choice and the three hole variants key on the intensity bucket (row 5, D.6) and no inversion of a Graph formula is needed; the Graph owner is asked to confirm that reading, or to publish a dedicated quantised hole field, which this system would adopt (Open Questions 46).
8. **One combined Graph-level decision on onset restarts** *(re-review)*: a `sev` change restamps the onset for every consumer, the valve-reopen jump is a one-frame extent step, and POINT-row accumulation restarts at each rise; Open Questions 6f puts all three to the user and the Graph owner as one decision.

The earlier MIRROR ask is closed: `SHARED_SUMMARY` now carries each active descriptor's intensity bucket, `since_steps`, `mode_steps` and `grow_steps` (`LSG:210`, LS-21) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. *Why:* inventing a Graph field's semantics in a consumer is owner absorption. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**FT-31 Footprints are Graph descriptors only.** The system draws nothing for a footprint as such. A hull RUPTURE or GOUGE (M17 `deposit_trace`) surfaces as ordinary hp loss and a BREACH or LEAK descriptor and is drawn exactly like any breach, **along the rib or seam** whatever caused it; there is no "exogenous" variant. *(CP-2, the radial EXOGENOUS variant is withdrawn because `ART:565` reserves radiating hairlines to the footprint grammar. Should a radial look ever be wanted for a RUPTURE it carries a Void Steel hole centre and Hull Grey lips, and Horror's footprint stays hue-less and holeless.)* The Graph persists no footprint record (`LSG:196`, `LSG:912`); CHILL and BURN (at most `FOOTPRINT_MAX_Q` 20000 dJ, `LSG:196`, Graph GDD) rarely change a band and STRESS has no descriptor, so those three kinds have no trail (routed to the Graph owner as a VS-A candidate: a read-only footprint mark). Writes above `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP` (16) are dropped by the Graph, so a trail never shows a footprint the Graph did not accept (neither the registry's "per subject" nor the Graph's "in one run" scope wording is restated). **Known truth-channel hole (routed):** a GOUGE on a duct or cable surfaces only as the WORN descriptor (module-body, corruptible) or as a LEAK, contradicting "the hallucination hides the body, never the trail" for non-hull subjects; until the Graph owner closes it, **MVP Horror restricts GOUGE to hull segments; this restriction is binding on the Horror Entities GDD, not advisory** (creative-director ruling, CD-GDD-ALIGN stage 12). **Joint guarantee:** Pillar 4(a), "the hallucination hides the body, never the trail", is guaranteed jointly by this GDD (Graph descriptors, hull subjects) and by Horror Entities (web-grammar footprints: sound, frost, hull stress, light disturbance), so neither document may assume the other covers every footprint kind. Web-grammar marks (wet iridescent smear, branching rime, dust direction, filament twitch) are Horror's and share the floor-marks band; this system never draws coolant-fan-looking rime and tests for contrast collisions with Horror's marks. The coolant wet run (FT-11) is likewise never an iridescent or branching smear: it is non-iridescent, parallel-edged and edge-following from a coolant fitting with the frost fan at its source, and it is part of the collision fixture against Horror's wet smear (V/A.11 item 8, AC-79) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. *Why:* `ART:565`; P4(a).

**FT-32 A patched subject weeps (`27 PATCH_WEEP`); the BREACH pre-stage is confirmed.** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: rewritten. The Graph adopted the patch weep, so the interim "a patch draws nothing" ruling, the switch-aware branch and the routed truth-channel hole are all withdrawn.)* A patch raises `hp` to at most `PATCH_CAP_BP` 6000, which is above `LEAK_HP` 4000, so the Graph clears the LEAK mode. A patched subject (`hp_cap` < `HP_FULL`, `hp` < `HP_OK`, no mode 1-9 live) then carries the descriptor-only id **`27 PATCH_WEEP`** instead of WORN (LS-11, LS-14; Graph OQ 23 closed by creative-director ruling "a patch weeps"): consequence channel, `sev` 1, `medium` by the subject's loop (GAS for a DUCT or O2 tank; COOLANT or FUEL for a PIPE, RESERVOIR or FUEL tank; ELECTRIC for a CABLE, junction or LOAD; STRUCT for the rest), `acoustic` HISS / DRIP / TICK accordingly, `intensity_pm` 0 and never silenced by it. A patched hull or seam already carries the mode-5 STRUCT pre-stage and emits no PATCH_WEEP. WORN (module-body, `medium` NONE) is not emitted for it, so the descriptor sits on the closed consequence-channel list and the Filter can neither hide nor fake it (P1, P4). **What is drawn (table of record row 27):** one small static s-*trace* mark at the fitting, per medium: COOLANT an s-frost spur (no drip), FUEL an s-trace puddle blob at the flange (ember edge, glint, no drip), ELECTRIC an s-scorch trace, STRUCT an s-hairline trace; **GAS draws nothing** (the HISS carries the tell: the FT-18 note). It is always static (no LIVE/STILL axis, DRAWN_STATIC), has no glow row, no extent and no accumulation (geometry class POINT_FIXED), and dulls by `mode_steps` under the ongoing-descriptor cap of FT-17. **The patch is a same-step handover:** the LEAK descriptor ends and the PATCH_WEEP appears on the same Graph step with `mode_steps` 0, so the fan, the wet run and the drip are gone the next frame (T5, a change the player caused) and the weep is there that frame, with no fade and no sparkle (anti-promise 4, AC-114, AC-70). **The three-way contrast** (a closed valve leaves the full mark, still; a patch leaves a small weep, static; a cap or a cut leaves nothing) is the reading grammar the Player Fantasy relies on. After `PATCH_DECAY_S` 240 the patch decays and the re-leak is preceded by the weep, a consequence-channel tell the Filter cannot corrupt, so the former truth-channel hole (the only pre-re-leak tell was on the module-body channel) is closed; the Onboarding line "patch it" (`LSG:839`) now ends the long carried fan but leaves the small weep, and should still be reconciled with the valve-based anchor moment (Onboarding's to do). **The hull case is resolved by the Graph** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the BREACH pre-stage (hull or seam at `HP_OK` > `hp` >= `BREACH_HP`) is `mode` 5, `medium` STRUCT, `acoustic` TICK at flow zero, on the consequence channel and never WORN (`LSG:170`), so the sev 1-2 hairline of row 5 draws, the PROVISIONAL tag and the "blocking" label of the former Open Question 5 are retired, and the TICK is the k = 1 cue (FT-34). No Graph value is restated or changed here. *Why:* `LSG:127`, `LSG:222` are locked; a stale reading would give the consistency check a conflict. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the Graph's LS-11 and LS-23 carry PATCH_WEEP.)*

**FT-33 MIRROR (VS-B).** The co-op summary carries each active descriptor as `(id, mode, sev, alarm_eligible, intensity bucket = intensity_pm / 100, since_steps, mode_steps, grow_steps)`, each age saturating at 2^24 - 1, 13 bytes each (`LSG:210`, LS-21), so a MIRROR client reads the same extent and dullness fields as the HOST and CR-10's identical presentation holds. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the summary now carries the two GA-T ages; the MIRROR legacy branch and its named extent mismatch are deleted, and Open Questions 10 is closed.)* The summary carries no `mode_key`, so a MIRROR client computes dominance from the `(mode, sev)` pairs of the summary by the same rule as FT-8. MVP is single-player; the path is exercised against a mocked summary through the descriptor-feed seam (AC-72) and is not an MVP gate. *Why:* the kernel's CR-10 requires identical presentation in HOST and MIRROR; P1.

**FT-34 Zoom invariance and budgets at every ladder step.** Layout is in base px and identical at every integer zoom step k in [1, `k_auto` + 2] (`PIPE:161`, C6). Budgets (D.9 `trail_budget_at_k`) are stated **at every k and at 1080p, 1440p and 2160p, not at `k_auto` alone**: at k = 1 the camera field is up to 9x (1080p), 16x (1440p) or 36x (2160p) the area of the 640x360 default, so far more trails are on screen at once. The Pipeline's draw-call and particle-emitter caps are restated by name in Visual/Audio and Section D: floor marks `D_marks` 4-20 draws, at most 12 live particle emitters (mist at most 64 per trail, venting at most 128 per vent; "emitter" alone is Emitter State's record vocabulary, EM-3 and EM-4) re-expressed as frame-sprite counts for the stepped path, one Y-sorted layer of 50-300 draws, whole-frame 1000 draws (`PIPE:616`, `ART:1173`); if the sorted layer exceeds ~300 draws the remedy is per-row z-index bands for wall faces, never fewer props. Legibility gate: full legibility at `k_auto` and the first zoom-out; at k = 1 only polarity, hue blob and form class are guaranteed for m and l, and a sev 1-2 hairline (a 2 px composite) may not resolve, which is accepted and logged (not a licence to cull, FT-19, nor to add markers). **The non-visual cue that carries the flow-zero hull pre-stage at k = 1 (CD-GDD-ALIGN, stage 12):** the row 5 pre-stage has `intensity_pm` 0, so HISS and WIND (driven by flow) are silent; the cue is the **TICK** acoustic class, the hull groan the Graph's LS-22 Hull row names (`LSG:215`), played by Adaptive Audio at the descriptor's anchor on the consequence channel (FT-35); the Graph now assigns TICK to the flow-zero BREACH pre-stage (`LSG:170`), which closes the former FT-30 item (c) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. While the hairline is unresolved at k = 1 that TICK must exist for the descriptor; no marker, layer, icon or extra overlay is added, and the no-added-layer rule stands. *Why:* reflexion-log pattern 6 (an invariant asserted at the default must be re-checked when later decisions add reachable states).

**FT-35 Audio independence.** This system plays no audio. The `acoustic` classes (HISS, DRIP, HUM_DROP, CLANK, WIND, TICK, SIZZLE, CREAK) are Adaptive Audio's, played at the same anchor from the same descriptor; a hiss with no visible mist (a dark room) is expected, not a bug; this system never waits for audio, and audio may lead visuals. Both systems take the anchor position from Layout (one source). Adaptive Audio is designed (`adaptive-audio-and-tells.md`): AA-24 plays the eight witness classes at the descriptor's anchor from its true fields, and AC-AA-117 discharges AC-144. *Why:* `IDX:41`.

**FT-36 Accessibility toggles are read, not owned.** The system reads "reduced motion" and "reduce flashing" from the `SettingsStore` seam that the Chart Table & Menus layer owns (CT-18, CT-19; designed; the later Settings & Input Rebinding system, undesigned, replaces the page and keeps the seam) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* and applies FT-28 and FT-29 only; it owns no setting. **Defaults (user ruling D79, X10, 2026-10-27): "reduce flashing" is ON at first launch;** "reduced motion" keeps the pipeline default; the Chart Table & Settings owner sets both, this system only reads them. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* *Why:* `PIPE:847`.

**FT-37 Containment (no orphan pixels).** No opaque trail pixel lies outside its own compartment's floor tiles or its host face. Run (body) stamps are authored so their lateral content never exceeds the corridor clearance (a lateral strip of at most 48 px at m and l); head and source stamps may fill a full cell. Clipping to a Layout floor mask and clamping the head stamp to clearance are rejected alternatives (the second would make `trail_size` depend on Layout, breaking FT-6). The Layout clearance read is designed (LY-8 d). **Corridor narrower than a head cell (closing-stage ruling, decided here):** because clipping and clamping are rejected, a corridor whose clearance is below the head cell of any size its anchor can reach (an anchor that can reach sev 4 needs 144 px of clearance at the head) is a **Layout content error** (a lint to Layout and Derelict Authoring, routed in Open Questions), never a runtime fallback; the acceptance fixture uses a corridor at least as wide as the head cell. **Interval reading (confirmed for Layout LY-8 d, 2026-10-27):** the 144 px is a free interval normal to the final run segment at each sample point of the floor run, not a square centred on the run line; the cryo-bay run 24 px from the north wall passes under it. *Why:* an orphan trail on the neighbouring room's floor would break FT-3 (`LSG:970`-`LSG:977`).

**FT-38 MVP slice (what ships first, what waits).** **MVP:** every drawing row of the table of record for the ids the Graph's MVP slice emits (`LSG:228`: `1 LEAK` GAS/COOLANT/FUEL, `2 SHORT`, `3 OVERHEAT`, `4 FREEZE`, `5 BREACH`, `7 OVERLOAD`, `8 SEVERED`, `9 SCRAM`, `20`-`26` as emitted, `27 PATCH_WEEP` (every medium but GAS draws; GAS draws nothing, FT-18); `6`, `10`, `22`, `23` draw nothing) on the home-ship (5 compartments), derelict A (4) and derelict B (5); the stepped-frame path only (no particle node, FT-21); single-player (no MIRROR path, FT-33); the authored s cells s-frost, s-scorch, s-hairline, s-strain, **s-mist (trace and full) with `vfx_o2_mist_drift_s`, and s-puddle** (the seam weep is the ET-2 anchor moment and the Graph states its severity, FT-7, FT-14; *Amended 2026-10-27 — design review*); `27 PATCH_WEEP` reuses the s-trace cells of s-frost, s-puddle, s-scorch and s-hairline and adds no asset *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; one port shape (the round Foundry frame) for row 26 and the seam rows; variants s 2 / m 1 / l 1 (V/A.10 MVP cut); `TRAIL_EXTENT_SMOOTH` false; the six glow rows of V/A.6; the five drawing hazard groups of the eight MVP faults of `LSG:228`, each with a retained lit capture (AC-75). **Later:** particles for venting after spike S1; MIRROR parity (VS-B, FT-33; the summary already carries the two ages) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; the frozen-puddle presentation hint (VS-A, `LSG:838`); a read-only footprint mark for CHILL/BURN/STRESS (VS-A, FT-31); further port shapes and the full variant counts; the `DULL_STEPS` 8 scan matrix if the user chooses it (OQ 1). *Why:* the index names this system MVP for MVP item 2 (`game-concept.md`, MVP Definition); a slice that is only implied cannot be scoped into epics.

**FT-39 The pure resolver *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; was only an "ADR candidate")*.** Every decision this system makes (state NOT_DRAWN / DRAWN_LIVE / DRAWN_STILL, `size_idx` and `sub_tier`, the chain, `trail_extent`, the reveal threshold, `density_level` and `cover16`, `dull_step`, `glow_step`, `fuel_dried`, hue token, band, patch tiles, puddle stamps, animation frame, the in-field test) is computed by one **pure `RefCounted` resolver**: `resolve(descriptor, ctx) -> TrailSpec` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the earlier argument list could not compute the in-field test, the LIVE predicates of SHORT and OVERHEAT, `n_arcs_room`, dominance or the smoothing)*, where `ctx` is one injected context object carrying: the node and edge views (`node(id)`, `edge(id)`), the compartment view, the geometry provider, the camera field and ladder step k, `tick_alpha`, `step_no`, `present_time`, settings, the knob table, the seed, and a **`hash_fn` seam** (default: the kernel's `rng_derive`; tests inject a stub, so the resolver itself references no random function, AC-164). A per-feed **pre-pass** `prepare_feed(descriptors, ctx)` runs once per poll and computes, from the feed alone, the per-subject dominance (FT-8), the arc counts over the world-fixed neighbourhood (D.8b) and the stable draw order; its result lives in the poll cache (a copy of truth, FT-1) and is passed in `ctx`. It reads no scene node, no autoload and no clock; every input is an argument (dependency injection over singletons: coding standards). The scene layer is a thin applier that writes a `TrailSpec` into pooled nodes and decides nothing. The resolver is the oracle for every `(auto)` criterion that follows and runs headless.

**FT-40 Test seams and fixtures *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.** The resolver's inputs are the test seams: (1) the **descriptor feed** (an array of descriptor records with every `FailureDescriptor` field of LS-14, which already includes the GA-T fields `mode_steps` and `grow_steps`, and the feed's `step_no`; a Graph-integration fixture additionally seeds the persisted triple `mode_since_step`, `mode_key`, `isolated_at_step`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; (2) the **geometry provider** (`anchor_geometry`, `room_surface`, clearance: a stub returning a fixed polyline and tile set); (3) `present_time` (a float argument; no wall clock); (4) **settings** (`reduced_motion`, `reduce_flashing`); (5) the **knob table** (a data file, never constants in code); (6) the **seed** (`world_seed`); (7) the **logger sink**. Test tags: **(auto)** runs on the resolver or the poll logic headless, in the mandated CI command, under `tests/unit/failure_and_trail/`; **(auto-render)** needs rendered pixels (captures, bit-identity, dither, containment) and runs under `tests/integration/failure_and_trail/` on the named render job `ft-render` (a windowed or offscreen-capable Godot run on the reference machine of AC-83, never the headless job); **(lint)**, **(spike)** and **(manual)** as before. Fixtures are data files in `tests/fixtures/failure_and_trail/`: `fx_cryo.json`, `fx_tell.json`, `fx_row_oracle.json`, `fx_steady.json`, `fx_mvp_worst.json`, `fx_design_max.json`, `fx_seam.json`. **`FX-CRYO`** (the self-contained MVP cryo-bay fixture, owned by this GDD; Onboarding and the Graph's `LSG:839` fixture must match it): one compartment of 42 tiles at `T` -2000 mC (FROST), warming 8 mC per Graph step once the heater is on; one `1 LEAK/COOLANT` descriptor on a PIPE edge, `sev` 3, `intensity_pm` 600, `loop` 3, `acoustic` DRIP, anchor `anc_cryo_coolant` (geometry stub: wall part 0 px, floor part 336 px, a straight run along the north wall 24 px from it); `mode_steps` 56 and `grow_steps` 56 at the wake frame (`mode_since_step` = `step_no` - 56, **`mode_key` 11** = `(1 << 3) | 3`, the dominant descriptor's mode 1 LEAK at sev 3, `isolated_at_step` 0; seeded together, because the Graph's phase 9 overwrites a `mode_since_step` that has no matching `mode_key`, FT-8) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. **`FX-SEAM`**: one SEAM descriptor, `1 LEAK/GAS`, sev 1, `A_seam` 1 (`intensity_pm` 15), `since_steps` 0 at the weld, edge 288 px. **`FX-TELL`**: synthetic descriptors on a 288 px edge, `T` -12000 mC (below `FREEZE_WARN_MC` -10000 so the `4 FREEZE` pre-stage can exist, `LSG:127`; `frost_reach` = 480 + floor(12000 x 480 / 15000) = 864, at least 288), `4 FREEZE` sev 1, `5 BREACH` sev 2, `1 LEAK/COOLANT` sev 3, `5 BREACH` sev 4, each with `grow_steps` 0 at its first frame. **`fx_row_oracle.json`** is the **independent oracle**: one row per descriptor id and medium with its expected drawing/non-drawing, hue token and size reach, written from the table of record by hand and never generated from the resolver. The three budget fixtures (`fx_steady`, `fx_mvp_worst`, `fx_design_max`) are tabulated in D.9.

**FT-41 Cost, idle frames, warm-up, pool and one logging rule *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.** (1) **Idle frame:** a render frame with no new Graph `step_no`, no notification keys, no `anim_frame` index change and no camera-field change performs **zero scene writes and zero resolver calls**; it is the frame class `TRAIL_FRAME_MS_P99` budgets (D.7, D.9). (2) **Warm-up:** at load, before the first draw, one off-screen draw instantiates every material and shader variant the table of record uses, so the first leak of a session carries no compile hitch (spike S4, AC-84). (3) **Pool:** the node pool is pre-sized to `TRAIL_MAX_STAMPS` at load; growth past it is allowed, logged and never a cull. (4) **Pixel-neutral reducers come first:** write only the properties that changed, keep a retained per-trail draw list rebuilt on a key rather than per frame, and batch static stamps per room; each is verified bit-identical to the unreduced render (AC-163). Only then does FT-19's moving-media degradation order apply. (5) **One logging rule:** every diagnostic line (unknown descriptor id, missing geometry, each D.9 threshold, `ALL` over budget) is **edge-triggered**: a `(log_id, subject_id)` pair is latched in `diag_latch` on the rising edge of its condition, writes exactly one line, and re-arms when the condition clears (thresholds use `subject_id` 0; an unknown id or missing geometry latches per descriptor). No draw, reconcile or selection path reads `diag_latch` (FT-1).

#### 6. Descriptor-to-visual table of record

*This table is the system's table of record and the input to `/asset-spec system:failure-and-trail-system` (`LSG:979`); Visual/Audio refers to it and does not duplicate it. All eighteen descriptor ids have a row (ids 1-10, 20-26 and 27); mode 1 is three rows by medium; mode 8 inherits mode 1's row for its medium; id 27 is one row with a draw per medium. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* Behaviour words are the art bible's (`ART:314`). Band: **F** = floor marks (z +1), **W** = Y-sorted wall layer (z +10, host-face origin), **D** = dust and particles (z +30). "TRUTH" rows are consequence-channel and exact under the Filter; the four "draws nothing" rows say who renders that id's channel instead. `intensity_pm` and age columns say what each field drives (the age column is `grow_steps` for extent and accumulation and `mode_steps` for dullness, GA-T, FT-8); the PROVISIONAL tags on cells the Graph now states (`LSG:170`) are retired *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, and a cell is still PROVISIONAL only where it depends on Layout geometry detail or on the open requests of FT-30. Sheets: `trail_shared` stamps (albedo and mask pages, plus normal and specular only on frost and puddle), stepped media strips `vfx_*`.*

**Seam weeps (FT-14)** use the same rows along the bead (stem `trail_hull_seamweep_<s|m|l>`: seam straight, corner and T pieces as run and corner variants).

**Geometry class per row** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: only the RUN geometry had extent semantics, so radial scorch, soot and frame rows were undefined)*. Every row has one class, and `trail_extent` (D.2) is defined only for RUN:

| Class | Rows | What "extent" and age mean |
|---|---|---|
| RUN | 1 (GAS, COOLANT, FUEL), 4, 5, 8 (by its medium), the seam weep | a chain of stamps along the Layout polyline; `trail_extent_px` per D.2 (age `grow_steps`, bounded by the edge, and for `4 FREEZE` by the temperature bound; for `1 LEAK/COOLANT` the temperature sets only the frost part, D.2d) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| POINT | 2, 3, 7, 9 | an anchor cap only (one stamp of the D.1 size at the anchor, wall part and the floor scorch around it); no chain and no extent; the age drives **accumulation**: `acc_step = min(ACC_STEPS - 1, floor(grow_steps / ACC_STEP_LEN))` picks the char-fleck variant of the cap (`ACC_STEPS` 3, `ACC_STEP_LEN` 300 steps = 30 s **[PLACEHOLDER: `ACC_STEP_LEN`]**); the radial reach is the authored cap size |
| POINT_FIXED | 27 | one s-*trace* stamp at the anchor (wall part or floor cap); no chain, no extent and no accumulation (`acc_step` does not exist for it); the age drives dullness only *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| PATCH_SET | 20, 21, 25 | a set of tile patches (D.2b) or puddle stamps (D.2c); no chain; no age term |
| STREAK | 24 | moving media at the opening; no extent, no age |
| FRAME | 26 | one frame-attached sprite per port shape; no extent, no age |
| NONE | 6, 10, 22, 23 | draws nothing |

**Five behaviours by three sizes (what 48, 96 and 144 mean as drawings, never as scaled copies).** "Ink" is the opaque area inside the atlas gutter. Density (coverage) is separate from size (FT-5, FT-23).

| Behaviour | s (48): sev 1 *trace* / sev 2 *full* | m (96): sev 3 | l (144): sev 4 | Sev 3+ anchor accent (drawn regardless of flow) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Mist** (`1 LEAK/GAS`) | trace: one nozzle puff, one cluster <= 16 px wide; full: one plume <= 24 px wide | a plume of 2 lobes plus a secondary wisp, <= 56 px wide | a full jet: 3 lobes, a spreading cone <= 100 px wide, a detached wisp row | **none** (FT-18): a wider, sparser plume instead |
| **Frost** | trace: 1 spur, <= 12 px; full: a 3-spur fan, spur <= 20 px, 2 px arms | a 5-6-spur fan, one fork level, radius <= 44 px, an under-shadow row | a full 6-spur hex fan, two fork levels, a crust patch, radius <= 68 px | a heavy frost collar: a 2 px crust ring around the fitting or lagging with a Void Steel under-shadow |
| **Scorch** | trace: 4 char flecks around a 4x4 core; full: 4 spokes <= 20 px, core <= 8x8 | 6-8 spokes <= 40 px, core <= 16x16, a dashed rim ring | 10+ uneven spokes <= 64 px, a 24x24 burn pit with char flecks outward | a deep pit: the core grows and its rim becomes continuous |
| **Puddle / drip** | blob <= 24x16 | blob <= 64x40 | blob <= 120x72 (about 2.5 x 1.5 tiles), a 2 px ember edge, 2-3 glint specks | a wet collar: a dark glossy ring with an ember lip around the flange |
| **Hairline -> crack -> bent rib** | s = **hairline**: a 1 px Hull Grey run + 1 px Void Steel shadow (trace: a single run <= 16 px; full: <= 48 px, 1-2 forks) | m = **crack**: 2 px Hull Grey lips with a 2 px Void Steel gap, 2-3 forks | l = **bent rib or hole**: a Graphite rib segment jogged 2-4 px with a buckled plate edge; for `A_hole` >= 32 a Void Steel hole 16-48 px with a Hull Grey lip <= 4 px (three authored hole variants: 32-40, 41-52, 53-64) | a buckle jog at the rib |

The hole shows Void Steel only: no web, no filament, no iridescence at a breach (the web is seen only in the player's light and is Horror's, `ART:565`).

#### 7. Key family -> reaction

| Key (`LSG:201`) | Meaning | Reaction in the next render callback |
|---|---|---|
| `F/<id>` | a descriptor's intensity bucket or appearance changed | spawn or despawn the trail; re-select size, density and LIVE / STILL |
| `N/<id>` | a node or edge's state, forced mode or severity transitioned | re-select size and behaviour; the sev 3+ accent on or off |
| `C/<cid>` | a compartment changed (one key per compartment per step) | re-read `T` (frost reach) and `fuel_puddle_ml`; update room frost and soot patches |
| `B/<cid>` | a band changed | band accents; the `ATMO_*` patch set |
| `L/<loop>/<anchor>` | a loop component summary changed | ignored (loop on/off is not this system's) |
| `S/<seam_id>` | a seam changed | re-resolve seam-bead trails |
| `T`, `SEC/<id>` | a structural change (a weld, a discard, a salvage) | rebuild anchors from Layout |
| `ALL` | collapse: restore, derelict instantiation, or more than `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256 keys | one full re-read within the same frame (FT-16) |
| (no key) | the age fields (`since_steps` and GA-T's `mode_steps` and `grow_steps`) never dirty a key (a `sev` change does: `N/`, and it resets the mode onset); neither do the continuous values behind the FT-30 LIVE predicates (`flow`, machine `T`) | read from the per-step poll cache every frame (FT-15) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

### States and Transitions

*These are **derived render states**, recomputed from the poll cache and the camera field every frame and never stored (FT-1): a trail's state is a function of the descriptor and the camera, and nothing remembers what the previous state was. The system has no state machine of its own to persist, snapshot or restore; the table exists so the reaction to every Graph change is unambiguous and testable.*

| State | Entry condition (a pure predicate) | Exit condition | Behaviour | Who may cause entry or exit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| NOT_DRAWN | no descriptor for the subject; or `sev` 0; or `medium` NONE (WORN); or the id's row is a "draws nothing" row (STARVE 6, WORN 10, ATMO_THIN 22, ATMO_DECOMP 23); or the trail's bounding box padded by one head cell lies wholly outside the camera field at the current k (FT-19; *Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: it was the anchor point*); or the section is dormant (it emits no descriptors) | a descriptor appears with `medium` != NONE and sev >= 1 and the padded bounding box intersects the field | draws nothing | the **Graph** (descriptor appears or clears), the **camera** (field and ladder step k). Never Perception, sanity, a setting or a verb directly |
| DRAWN_LIVE | descriptor present, sev >= 1, `medium` != NONE, padded bounding box meets the field, `intensity_pm` > 0 (for the rows with a LIVE axis but no Graph `intensity_pm`, `2 SHORT` and `3 OVERHEAT`, the per-row LIVE predicate of FT-30) | `intensity_pm` falls to 0 (-> DRAWN_STILL), or the descriptor clears, or the padded bounding box leaves the field (-> NOT_DRAWN) | stamp chain at the extent of FT-8, moving media at `density_level`, static stamps dulled by D.5 | the Graph; the camera *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| DRAWN_STILL | as DRAWN_LIVE with `intensity_pm` = 0 (for `2 SHORT` and `3 OVERHEAT`, the LIVE predicate of FT-30 false) (a valve-isolated leak, a derelict dead fault; for `1 LEAK/GAS` and `8 SEVERED/GAS` the plume is empty by design, FT-18, and this is a drawn-and-empty state, not suppression) | `intensity_pm` rises above 0 (-> DRAWN_LIVE), or the descriptor clears or leaves the field (-> NOT_DRAWN) | stamp chain only, no moving media, dulled by D.5; static stamps at their authored coverage (nothing is recalled) | the Graph (a valve opening, a re-pressurised pipe); the camera *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| DRAWN_STATIC *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | descriptor present, sev >= 1, `medium` != NONE, padded bounding box meets the field, and the row has **no LIVE/STILL axis** (`has_flow_state` false): rows 4 FREEZE, 7 OVERLOAD, 9 SCRAM and 27 PATCH_WEEP (rows 20 and 21 are compartment patch sets with no age and use it too) | the descriptor clears or the padded bounding box leaves the field (-> NOT_DRAWN) | the static stamp at the authored coverage for the current `sev`, never a moving medium, dulled by D.5; rows 7 and 27 are an ongoing condition and are held at stop `DULL_STEPS` - 2, rows 4 and 9 (latched faults) are not (FT-17); a `27 PATCH_WEEP/GAS` is drawn and empty | the Graph; the camera |

Attributes inside DRAWN_*: size index (D.1) from `sev`; `density_level` (D.4); dull step (D.5); extent (D.2). **Dullness, glow decay (SCRAM) and extent are attributes, not states**: they change continuously with `grow_steps`, `mode_steps` and the room's true temperature and need no transition. Reduced motion and reduce flashing change an attribute (the hold frame, the held glow) and never a state.

| # | From -> To | Trigger | Condition | System action |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | NOT_DRAWN -> DRAWN_LIVE | `F/` or `N/` key; or the per-step poll on field entry | a descriptor appears with `intensity_pm` > 0 | spawn the chain at the age-derived extent (at least `MIN_EXTENT_PX`; at `grow_steps` 0 exactly `MIN_EXTENT_PX`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| T2 | NOT_DRAWN -> DRAWN_STILL | `F/`, `N/` or `ALL` | a descriptor appears with `intensity_pm` 0 (derelict dead fault; isolated leak) | spawn the chain, no moving media, already grown (to its frozen or back-dated `grow_steps`) and dulled by its `mode_steps` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| T3 | DRAWN_LIVE -> DRAWN_STILL | `F/` (or the per-step poll, for the FT-30 predicates) | `intensity_pm` falls to 0 (a closed upstream valve), or the FT-30 LIVE predicate turns false (a breaker opens) | stop the drip and mist frames; the stamps stay, **the extent stops growing (it is capped at the frozen `grow_steps`, FT-8, GA-T)** and the frost does not retreat on its own (it retreats only with the room's temperature, FT-11) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| T4 | DRAWN_STILL -> DRAWN_LIVE | `F/` (or the per-step poll, for the FT-30 predicates) | `intensity_pm` > 0 again, or the FT-30 LIVE predicate turns true | start the moving media |
| T5 | DRAWN_* -> NOT_DRAWN (descriptor gone) | `F/`, `N/`, `T` or `SEC/` | the descriptor is gone (a repair, a patch ending its LEAK trail, a cap, a weld rebuild, a salvage, or the section went dormant) or `sev` fell to 0 | despawn on the **next frame**; **no fade** (FT-1) |
| T6 | DRAWN_* size change | `F/` or `N/` | `sev` crosses 1-2 / 3 / 4 | swap stamp sizes; the Graph resets the mode onset, so `grow_steps` is 0 and the extent restarts at `MIN_EXTENT_PX` and regrows at the new `GROW_PX_PER_STEP[sev]` (FT-8; *Amended 2026-10-27 — design review*) |
| T7 | DRAWN_* density change | `F/` | the 100-permille bucket crosses a `density_level` edge (D.4) | change frame count, cadence and dither cover |
| T8 | DRAWN_* extent change | per-step poll | `grow_steps` grows (age bound; frozen while isolated), or the room's `T` changes `reach_px` (temperature bound: the extent of a `4 FREEZE`, the `frost_px` split of a coolant leak, D.2d) | grow, or retreat (frost turns into wet run); no state is stored *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; user rulings)* |
| T9 | NOT_DRAWN <-> DRAWN_* (field) | per-step poll; camera or zoom change | the padded trail bounding box enters or leaves the field at the current k | spawn or despawn; a zoom-out reveals every trail (FT-19) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| T10 | any -> any | `ALL` | restore, derelict instantiation, or more than `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256 keys | one full re-read reconciled against the pool within the same frame (FT-16) |
| T11 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | NOT_DRAWN <-> DRAWN_STATIC | `F/`, `N/`, field entry or `ALL` | a row with no flow axis (4, 7, 9, 20, 21, 27) gains or loses its descriptor, or enters or leaves the field | spawn the stamp at its age-derived size and dullness, or despawn on the next frame with no fade; a patch is a same-step handover: the LEAK trail despawns (T5) and the row-27 weep spawns on the same frame |

**Causes that do not exist.** No Perception Filter stage, sanity level, hallucination class, accessibility setting or player verb is a cause of any transition above. Verbs act on the **Graph** (a valve closes, a patch lands, a breaker opens, a section is welded or cut), the Graph changes the descriptor, and the transition follows from the descriptor. In particular a trail is never removed by a presentation-side effect after a repair.

### Interactions with Other Systems

*Direction: "in" is data this system takes; "out" is data it gives. Status: **designed** = the neighbour's GDD exists with Status Designed and is cited by rule id and by line (lines as read on 2026-10-26; the rule id is the stable citation, because a sibling file still moves); **provisional** = no GDD exists and the contract is this GDD's proposal, to be repeated or rejected by the neighbour (reflexion-log pattern 4: only what this system must take or give is listed, never a neighbour's internals). Where a designed neighbour has its own row for this system, it is quoted.*

| # | System | Status | Data in | Data out | Interface owner | Exact names fixed elsewhere |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Life-Support Graph** | designed | Through `read_view` (LS-19), pure and side-effect free: `failures(filter)` -> sorted `FailureDescriptor[]` (fields per LS-14: `subject_kind`, `subject_id`, `compartment_id`, `anchor`, `loop`, `mode`, `medium`, `sev`, `intensity_pm`, `since_steps`, `warn_elapsed`, `alarm_eligible`, `cause`, `acoustic`, `dir`; plus the adopted **GA-T** fields `mode_steps` and `grow_steps`, and descriptor id 27, FT-30); `compartment(cid)` -> `T`, `band_t`, `fuel_puddle_ml`; `node(id)` / `edge(id)` -> `sev`, `hp`, `flow`, `served_pm` (for the provisional rows of FT-30); dirty keys `F/`, `N/`, `B/`, `C/`, `S/`, `T`, `SEC/`, and `ALL` (LS-20); the closed consequence-channel list (LS-14) | nothing (pure reader) | **Graph owns semantics; this system owns rendering** | `graph.state` is the Graph's only block; this system reads it, never writes it; M17 `deposit_trace` is named only as the source of footprints that surface through descriptors (FT-31); `W_MIN_WARNING_S` 10, `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256, `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6, `HATCH_DT_FROST_MC` 20000, `WELD_DP_WARN` 300. **The Graph's own row (`LSG:263`):** "Failure & Trail \| none (pure reader) \| `failures()`, `compartment()` bands, `AIRFLOW`, `since_steps`, `intensity_pm`, `alarm_eligible`; dirty keys `F/`, `N/`, `B/`, `C/` \| Graph owns semantics; F&T owns rendering \| presentation; polls `read_view` at render rate, keys wake spawn/despawn \| designed; R17 limits routed". **The Graph's downstream row (`LSG:857`):** "`failures()` sorted `FailureDescriptor[]` ...; dirty keys `F/`, `C/`, `B/`, `T`; true compartment values for frost/mist spatial presentation \| nothing (pure reader) \| PRESENTATION subscriber". Bidirectional, with one wording gap (the Graph's two rows list slightly different key sets, `F/ N/ B/ C/` vs `F/ C/ B/ T`; this GDD consumes the union in FT-15 and the reaction table); the Graph's 'polls `read_view` at render rate' is satisfied by reading the per-step poll cache every frame (FT-15): the Graph runs once per step, so no value changes between polls *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

Bidirectional. Two Pipeline wordings to reconcile are routed (Open Questions): the reduced-motion test at `PIPE:1010` names a state block that does not exist (this system registers none), and the hairline composite (FT-22) |

The kernel names this system twice (`KERN:110`, and `KERN:466`: "a hull breach cascading through 300 nodes -> `ALL`; Failure & Trail does one full re-read") but its **Interactions table (`KERN:187`) has no row for this system** (rows concern the registered blocks and the subscriber classes in general): a bidirectional-dependency gap, routed |

Save/Load **does** name this system, three times, and agrees: `SL:286` lists "Save/Load; Failure & Trail; Item & Module Database" among the systems that "register no block"; `SL:853` "Failure & Trail System \| None \| - \| Pure reader and renderer; registers no block; the Graph persists no footprint record \| In flight (draft); no edge"; `SL:1027` "Failure & Trail System \| No block, no edge \| SL-25 \| `failure-and-trail-system.md` Overview (draft)". Status **designed, bidirectional, consistent** (SL-17 and SL-25). No data crosses |

**Bidirectionality summary.** Rows 1, 2, 7 and 18 are bidirectional with designed neighbours (Emitter State, `EM:333` and `EM:714`; Save/Load, `SL:286`, `SL:853`, `SL:1027`; both neighbours name this system). Row 6 needs no Interactions row (no data crosses) and the Item DB's Cross-References row (`IDB:1133`) names this system. Rows 3 and 4 are one-way gaps (the neighbour mentions this system in prose or not at all but has no Interactions row): routed in Open Questions to the consistency check, not fixed in place. Rows 16 (Onboarding), 17 (Co-op) and 22 (Settings & Input Rebinding) cannot be bidirectional until those GDDs exist (row 20, the Chart Table, exists and needs its own row updated); each must repeat or reject its row when written. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: rows 5, 8-15 and 19 now cite designed neighbours; the Sanity (`sanity-and-hallucination-rules.md`, C1 stage range), Boarding & Salvage (`boarding-and-salvage.md`, the dried-trail field check) and HUD & Suit rows are added to Dependencies.)* *(Refreshed 2026-10-27, consistency pass: rows 5, 8, 10, 12, 15 and the new row 21 now cite designed neighbours whose own rows name this system.)*

## Formulas

*Conventions. Every formula here is a pure function of Graph fields, Layout geometry and the render clock (FT-1); none has state. All arithmetic is integer, with `floor` (integer division) stated wherever pixels or counts are produced, so everything that selects what is drawn is bit-identical on every client and after every restore (the kernel's determinism rule applies to anything hashed or replicated; nothing here is hashed, but FT-2 and FT-33 make equality of the drawn set a requirement). The only float is `present_time` in D.8 (the Pipeline's render-rate clock, PR-9; presentation only, never hashed, never fed back into the Graph) and `tick_alpha` in D.2's optional smoothing. Graph formulas (D.2 hole geometry `LSG:308`, D.4 efficiency and severity `LSG:361`, D.10 `dirty_key_budget` `LSG:532`, D.11 `fairness_gate`) are **cited by name and never re-derived**; where a worked example needs a Graph value it is taken as given and labelled so. Values tagged **[PLACEHOLDER: NAME]** are this system's, have a registry candidate of that name, and are tuned in Tuning Knobs. Rule ids `FT-n` are in Detailed Design; formula ids `D.n` here (registered names: `trail_size`, `trail_extent`, `frost_reach`, `dullness`, `arc_flash_share`, `trail_budget_at_k`, `trail_reconcile_cost`; unregistered section-local names: `trail_density`, `trail_stamp_variant`, `anim_frame`, `trail_reveal`).*

### D.1 `trail_size`

The `trail_size` formula is defined as:

`size_idx = 0 if sev = 0 else min(3, max(1, sev - 1))` ; `sub_tier = trace if sev <= 1 else full` (meaningful only when `size_idx` = 1; *Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the sev-0 seam exception is retired, FT-6, because the Graph states the seam weep at sev 1*) ; chain: `idx_i = max(1, size_idx - floor(i / TAPER_STAMPS))`, `start(0) = 0`, `start(i + 1) = start(i) + STAMP_PX[idx_i]`

Derived: `n_cover(E)` = the smallest `n` with `start(n)` >= `E` (the stamps needed to cover an extent `E` from D.2); the chain tapers so the source is the thickest point, and a larger `TAPER_STAMPS` tapers *slower*.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sev` | int | 0-4 | descriptor `sev` (Graph D.4, `LSG:365`) | Severity; 0 draws nothing *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `size_idx` | int | {0, 1, 2, 3} | calculated | 0 none, 1 s (48 base px), 2 m (96), 3 l (144) |
| `sub_tier` | enum | {trace, full} | calculated | The two sub-tiers inside s: sev 1 trace, sev 2 full (FT-6) |
| `STAMP_PX` | int[3] | 48 / 96 / 144 | `ART:1043`, defined by FT-6 | Stamp length along the chain for `size_idx` 1 / 2 / 3 |
| `TAPER_STAMPS` | int | 1-3 (default 1) **[PLACEHOLDER: `TAPER_STAMPS`]** | tuning | Run stamps laid per size tier before the chain steps down one tier |
| `i` | int | 0 - 2^20 - 1 | calculated | Stamp ordinal from the source (0 = anchor cap) |
| `idx_i` | int | 1-3 | calculated | Size index of stamp `i` |
| `start(i)` | int px | 0-1152 | calculated | Offset of stamp `i` from the source along the polyline |

**Output Range:** `size_idx` 0..3: sev 1 and 2 -> 1; sev 3 -> 2; sev 4 -> 3. `idx_i` never rises along the chain and never falls below 1, so no stamp is larger than the source stamp. With `TAPER_STAMPS` 1 the start offsets are: sev 4: 0, 144, 240, 288, 336, ...; sev 3: 0, 96, 144, 192, ...; sev 1-2: 0, 48, 96, ... . Not clamped further: every input is bounded by the Graph. Wall cells are 48 / 96 / 144 px wide and at most 72 px tall (FT-13); `STAMP_PX` is the along-run length.
**Example:** a hull plate at `hp` 1500, which the Graph's D.4 maps to sev 4 (taken as given), gives `size_idx` 3. With `TAPER_STAMPS` 1 the stamps are l (144) at 0, m (96) at 144, s (48) at 240, 288, 336 ...; `n_cover(336)` = 4 (`start(3)` = 288 < 336, `start(4)` = 336 >= 336). With `TAPER_STAMPS` 2 the same plate lays l, l, m, m, s, s ...: starts 0, 144, 288, 384, 480, 528 (a fatter, slower taper). A coolant pipe at sev 3 gives `size_idx` 2: m at 0, then s at 96, 144, 192 ....

### D.2 `trail_extent`

The `trail_extent` formula is defined as:

`age_steps = min(grow_steps, SINCE_CLAMP)` ; `age_px = GROW_PX_PER_STEP[sev] x age_steps` ; `reach_px = frost_reach(T)` (D.3) when the row is `4 FREEZE`, else `edge_len_px` (**`1 LEAK/COOLANT` has no temperature term in its extent: its `reach_px` here is `edge_len_px`, and `frost_reach(T)` bounds only `frost_px`, D.2d**) ; `trail_extent_px = min(edge_len_px, max(MIN_EXTENT_PX, min(age_px, reach_px)))` ; optional render smoothing (`TRAIL_EXTENT_SMOOTH` true only): `E_disp = E(n - 1) + floor((E(n) - E(n - 1)) x alpha_pm / 1000)` with `alpha_pm = floor(tick_alpha x 1000)`, `E(n)` the value above at `grow_steps`, `E(n - 1)` the same expression at `max(0, grow_steps - 1)` with the same `sev` and `T`; otherwise `E_disp = E(n)`. **`grow_steps` is the trail's own age (FT-8, GA-T, adopted in LS-14): `max(0, (isolated_at_step if isolated_at_step > 0 else step_no) - mode_since_step)`, where the Graph restamps `mode_since_step` whenever the subject's dominant descriptor changes its `mode_key` = `(mode << 3) | sev`; the resolver reads the descriptor's `grow_steps` field and never recomputes it, except that a non-dominant descriptor takes the interim rule of FT-8 (head only; the seam weep reads `since_steps`).** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* D.2 applies to the RUN class only (geometry-class table, Detailed Design section 6); `20 ATMO_COLD` and `21 ATMO_HOT` use D.2b and read no age. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the age was the Graph's `since_steps`, `sev_g` existed only for the retired seam exception, and `20 ATMO_COLD` was listed as a cold RUN row.)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: the coolant leak is no longer a temperature-bounded row, Open Questions 6a, D.2d.)*

Derived (the tell-window invariants, testable): `crossing_steps(L, sev) = ceil(L / GROW_PX_PER_STEP[sev])` ; (i) `crossing_steps(288, 1) <= 288` (28.8 s, inside the 10-30 s tell ceiling of `PIPE:1081` item 15); (ii) `V_WALK` x `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` = 12 < `GROW_PX_PER_STEP[4]` <= `V_RUN` x `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` = 18; (iii) `GROW_PX_PER_STEP[sev]` monotone non-decreasing in `sev`; (iv) in the cryo-bay fixture (sev 3) `GROW_PX_PER_STEP[3]` x 10 <= `DRIP_FALL_PX_PER_FRAME` x `TRAIL_ANIM_FPS` ("nothing moves faster than the drip", `ART:142`).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sev` | int | 1-4 | descriptor | Severity (a change of `sev` resets `grow_steps`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `grow_steps` | int | 0 - unbounded | descriptor, **GA-T (adopted, LS-14)**: `max(0, (isolated_at_step if isolated_at_step > 0 else step_no) - mode_since_step)`, dominant descriptor only; a non-dominant descriptor takes FT-8's interim rule (`since_steps`, which the Graph counts from `built_step` for a SEAM, is read only for the seam weep) | Steps of this trail's own life: 0 at the first frame of the current mode and severity; frozen at the isolation step; for a dormant section it counts steps the section ran *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `SINCE_CLAMP` | int | 1000-65535 (default 20000) **[PLACEHOLDER: `SINCE_CLAMP`]** | tuning | Keeps `age_px` inside 32-bit shader uniforms; 20000 steps = 33 min (clamps `grow_steps` only; dullness reads `mode_steps` unclamped) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `GROW_PX_PER_STEP[sev]` | int px/step | sev 1: 1-4; sev 2: 2-8; sev 3: 3-7; sev 4: 13-18; default 2 / 3 / 6 / 15 **[PLACEHOLDER: `GROW_PX_PER_STEP`]** | tuning (FT-9) | Growth per Graph step (20 / 30 / 60 / 150 px/s) |
| `edge_len_px` | int px | 48 - `TRAIL_EDGE_MAX_PX` 1152 | Layout `anchor_geometry` (LY-23, designed) | Polyline length of the combined wall + floor run (FT-13) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `MIN_EXTENT_PX` | int px | 24-96 (default 48) **[PLACEHOLDER: `MIN_EXTENT_PX`]** | tuning | The source stamp is present from step 0 |
| `DRIP_FALL_PX_PER_FRAME` | int px | 4-8 (default 6) **[PLACEHOLDER: `DRIP_FALL_PX_PER_FRAME`]** | art-director (drip strip: hang 2, fall 2, land 4 frames) | Fall speed of the drip, 72 px/s at 12 fps; the benchmark for "nothing moves faster than the drip" in the cryo bay |
| `reach_px` | int px | 0-1152 | D.3 for `4 FREEZE`; `edge_len_px` for every other row, `1 LEAK/COOLANT` included | Temperature bound of the extent (`4 FREEZE` only) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| `tick_alpha` | float | [0, 1] | Pipeline PR-9 | Interpolation fraction inside the current Graph interval; never extrapolated (`KERN:298`) |
| `trail_extent_px` | int px | `min(edge_len_px, 48)` - `edge_len_px` | calculated | Length to draw; floored base px |

**Output Range:** `min(edge_len_px, MIN_EXTENT_PX)` at `grow_steps` 0 (or when the reach bound is 0), up to `edge_len_px`. Monotone non-decreasing in `grow_steps` for a fixed `sev`; non-increasing in room `T` while the reach bound binds (a `4 FREEZE` sheath retreats as the room warms; for `1 LEAK/COOLANT` the extent does not retreat, only `frost_px` does and the wet run takes the stamps, D.2d), with no stored state *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. **A severity change restarts `grow_steps` at 0** (the extent restarts at `MIN_EXTENT_PX` and regrows at the new rate: the one-frame event is a size swap, not an inflation of old history; FT-8; default applied 2026-10-27, pending user confirmation, Open Questions 6c) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. **Isolation freezes the age bound:** while `isolated_at_step` > 0, `grow_steps` is constant, so a valve closed at `grow_steps` 50 on a sev 3 coolant leak leaves `age_px` at 6 x 50 = 300 for ever (a `4 FREEZE` sheath can still be pulled back by the temperature bound; a coolant leak's temperature pulls back only its frost part, D.2d). The frozen extent is a property of `trail_extent_px`, which exists even where FT-18 draws nothing: a `1 LEAK/GAS` plume at `intensity_pm` 0 has the same frozen `trail_extent_px` and zero visible pixels, so the GAS variant is tested on the resolver output (AC-146), not on pixels. `edge_len_px` above 1152 is a content error (lint to Layout and Derelict Authoring). `GROW_PX_PER_STEP` x `SINCE_CLAMP` is at most 18 x 65535, which fits int32.
**Example (cryo-bay coolant leak, `T` -2000 mC, `LSG:839`):** the pipe at `hp` 3800 carries sev 3 (taken as given from the Graph's D.4), so the rate is 6 px/step; `edge_len_px` 336 (7 tiles; Layout's `anc_cryo_coolant` polyline, wall part 0 px and floor part 336 px, LY-23 and Layout D.8); `reach_px` at -2000 mC is 544 (D.3). `grow_steps` 0: extent 48. 10: 60. 48: 288. 56: `min(336, max(48, min(336, 544)))` = 336, the far wall, 5.6 s after the leak began (so the fixture `FX-CRYO` authors `mode_since_step` at least 56 steps in the past, with `mode_key` 11, `grow_steps` >= 56, for the frost to be at the far wall at wake). **Worked cases added by the design review:** (a) *wear then leak*: a pipe that wore (WORN) for 30 minutes and then crosses `LEAK_HP` enters mode `1 LEAK` with `mode_since_step` = that step, so its first LEAK frame has `grow_steps` 0, extent 48 (`MIN_EXTENT_PX`), `dull_step` 0 and `fuel_dried` false, whatever `since_steps` says; (b) *isolation*: a valve closing at `grow_steps` 50 on a sev 3 coolant leak (6 px/step, edge 336, `T` -2000 mC) freezes `grow_steps` at 50, extent `min(336, 6 x 50)` = 300 px, and 100 steps later the extent is still 300 (a `1 LEAK/GAS` plume isolated the same way has the same frozen `trail_extent_px` and no visible pixel, FT-18); (c) *severity rise on an aged subject*: a hull plate at sev 3 with `mode_steps` 300 (extent capped by its edge) rises to sev 4, the Graph resets the mode onset, and on the `N/` frame `grow_steps` is 0, the l head stamp draws at 48 of 144 px, then the extent is 48, 60, 75, ... at 15 px/step (`max(48, 15 x grow_steps)` = 48 until `grow_steps` 4, then 60, 75), reaching a 288 px crossing at `grow_steps` 20 (2.0 s). The heater warms the bay at 8 mC per Graph step (`LSG:839`): `T` leaves FROST at step 250 after power-on with reach 480 > 336 (extent unchanged); reach falls below 336 when `T` passes 3000 mC (step 625, 62.5 s), from which `frost_px` = reach; at `T` 6000 mC (step 1000) reach is 192, so `frost_px` has retreated from 336 to 192 and the extent stays 336 (the 144 px beyond the frost are wet run, D.2d); at `T` 10000 mC (step 1500, 2.5 min, COMFORT) reach is 0 and `frost_px` is 0, so the frost is the head fan stamp alone and the extent stays 336, all of it wet run beyond the head. The age bound saturated at `grow_steps` 56 and, for a coolant leak, governs the extent for ever; the temperature bound governs only `frost_px` from step 625 after power-on *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. **Tell-window check:** at sev 1 a 288 px crossing takes `ceil(288 / 2)` = 144 steps = 14.4 s; at sev 4, `ceil(288 / 15)` = 20 steps = 2.0 s, and 15 px/step = 150 px/s is faster than the 120 px/s walk and slower than the 180 px/s run. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

#### D.2b `surface_patch_count` (ATMO_COLD, ATMO_HOT)

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: rewritten. The earlier formula grew with `since_steps`, which the Graph states as 0 for COMPARTMENT descriptors (`LSG:170`), so room frost and soot never spread and the 21-patch example was unreachable; the first fix considered, a radius equal to the whole reach, would have popped up to 24 patches in one step at the 0 mC edge. Coverage is now a function of the room's true temperature alone, starts at 0, and is a prefix of a fixed ordering.)*

The `surface_patch_count` formula is defined as:

`cover_pm = 0` unless the row draws (`20 ATMO_COLD` at sev >= 2, `21 ATMO_HOT` at sev 4) ; `20 ATMO_COLD`: `cover_pm = clamp(floor((T_COOL_FROST - T) x 1000 / (T_COOL_FROST - T_FULL_COLD)), 0, 1000)` with `T_COOL_FROST` the COOL/FROST edge (0 mC) and `T_FULL_COLD` the FROST/FREEZING edge (-15000 mC, the Graph's `COLD_DMG_ONSET_MC`), both cited by position from LS-7 and never redefined here ; `21 ATMO_HOT` (sev 4): `cover_pm = clamp(floor((T - T_SCORCH) x 1000 / SOOT_RAMP_MC), 0, 1000)` with `T_SCORCH` the HOT/SCORCH edge (70000 mC, LS-7) ; candidate tiles `c_0 ... c_(N-1)` from Layout `room_surface`, sorted ascending by **`(dist_t, row, col)`** where `dist_t` is the **Manhattan distance in tiles** from the anchor tile (the **room-fixed anchor tile** of FT-11: the lowest `(row, col)` floor tile that touches an exposed hull face, else the lowest `(row, col)` floor tile of the room; a function of Layout geometry alone and never of any descriptor, so a leak appearing or clearing at constant `T` moves no patch) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* ; `m = 0` if the row does not draw, else `min(N, max(1, floor(N x cover_pm / 1000)))` ; tile `c_k` carries a patch iff `k < m` and `k mod PATCH_STRIDE = 0`, and only the first `PATCH_MAX_PER_ROOM` such tiles are kept ; `n_patches = min(PATCH_MAX_PER_ROOM, ceil(m / PATCH_STRIDE))` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `T` | int mC | about -40000 - 90000 | `compartment(cid).T` (true value, polled once per Graph step) | Room temperature; never the perceived value (FT-2) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `T_COOL_FROST`, `T_FULL_COLD`, `T_SCORCH` | int mC | 0 / -15000 / 70000 | Graph LS-7 edges, cited | Ramp knots; if LS-7 moves they move with it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `SOOT_RAMP_MC` | int mC | 5000-40000 (default 20000) **[PLACEHOLDER: `SOOT_RAMP_MC`]** | tuning | Temperature above the SCORCH edge at which the soot wash covers the whole room *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `cover_pm` | int | 0-1000 | calculated | Permille of the room's candidate tiles the set reaches; 0 at the COOL/FROST edge, so the first FROST step adds only the anchor patch (the `max(1, ...)` term) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `N` | int | 1 - room tiles | Layout `room_surface` (LY-23, designed) | Candidate floor tiles of the room *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `m` | int | 0 - `N` | calculated | Length of the covered prefix of the ordering; a prefix, so the set only grows as the room cools (or heats) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `dist_t` | int tiles | 0 - room extent | calculated | Manhattan distance in tiles from the anchor tile; integer, so the order is bit-identical on every client *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `PATCH_STRIDE` | int | 1-4 (default 2) **[PLACEHOLDER: `PATCH_STRIDE`]** | tuning | Every n-th candidate in the covered prefix gets a patch *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `PATCH_MAX_PER_ROOM` | int | 4-48 (default 24) **[PLACEHOLDER: `PATCH_MAX_PER_ROOM`]** | tuning | Content bound, not a cull (FT-19) |

**Output Range:** 0..`PATCH_MAX_PER_ROOM`. `20 ATMO_COLD` sev 1 (COOL) and `21 ATMO_HOT` sev < 4 give 0 (row 20, row 21). Monotone non-decreasing as `T` falls (cold) or rises (soot). `ceil` is used so the count equals the number of selected tiles for an odd `m` (indices 0, 2, ..., m - 1). **Pop bound:** `m` rises by at most `ceil(N x delta_cover_pm / 1000)` per step, and at the cryo-bay heater's 8 mC per step `delta_cover_pm` is 0.53, so for a room of at most 1875 tiles `m` rises by at most 1 and the patch count by at most 1 (never more than `PATCH_STRIDE`) per Graph step, which is the "room is slowly getting cold" read. Placement and variants are deterministic (D.6). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
**Example (cryo bay, no leak anchor, `FX-CRYO` geometry stub):** 42 tiles (registry `room_volume` fixture V 42), `T` -2000 mC (FROST, sev 2): `cover_pm` = floor(2000 x 1000 / 15000) = 133, `m` = floor(42 x 133 / 1000) = 5, `n_patches` = `min(24, ceil(5 / 2))` = **3** patches at size s (the anchor patch plus two). At `T` -1 mC (the first FROST step): `cover_pm` 0, `m` = max(1, 0) = 1, 1 patch. At `T` -15000 mC and colder: `cover_pm` 1000, `m` 42, `n_patches` = `ceil(42 / 2)` = **21** (the earlier 21 is now the full-coverage value, reached at the FROST/FREEZING edge, not at the FROST entry). An odd room of 41 tiles at full coverage: `m` 41, indices 0, 2, ..., 40 = `ceil(41 / 2)` = **21** patches (the former `floor` gave 20, one fewer than the tiles selected). A 60-tile room at full coverage: `min(24, ceil(60 / 2))` = 24. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

#### D.2c `puddle_stamps` (25 PUDDLE)

The `puddle_stamps` formula is defined as:

`puddle_size_idx = 1 if ml < PUDDLE_ML_M ; 2 if ml < PUDDLE_ML_L ; else 3` ; `n_puddle = clamp(ceil(ml / ML_PER_PUDDLE_STAMP), 1, PUDDLE_MAX_STAMPS)`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ml` | int mL | 1 - unbounded | `compartment().fuel_puddle_ml` (Graph, mL: `LSG:316`) | True puddle volume; size follows volume, not `sev` (the Graph states `sev` 1 for `25 PUDDLE`, which would make every puddle s, `LSG:170`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `PUDDLE_ML_M`, `PUDDLE_ML_L` | int mL | 500 / 2000 **[PLACEHOLDER: `PUDDLE_ML_TIERS`]** | tuning | Size tier thresholds |
| `ML_PER_PUDDLE_STAMP` | int mL | 250 **[PLACEHOLDER: `ML_PER_PUDDLE_STAMP`]** | tuning | Volume per stamp |
| `PUDDLE_MAX_STAMPS` | int | 1-16 (default 8) | tuning | Content bound |

**Output Range:** 1..`PUDDLE_MAX_STAMPS` while `ml` > 0; the trail is gone when the descriptor is gone. The puddle shrinks only as the Graph's `fuel_puddle_ml` does (FT-17).
**Example:** `ml` 900: size m (`500 <= 900 < 2000`), `n_puddle` = `ceil(900 / 250)` = 4 stamps. `ml` 100: size s, 1 stamp.

#### D.2d `coolant_wet_run` (`1 LEAK/COOLANT`; user ruling D26, Option A) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

*Why a formula of its own:* the followable gradient "thickest at the source" (FT-3, Player Fantasy corollary 2) has to hold at COMFORT, where D.3 gives a frost reach of 0. The extent of D.2 is age-bound only for this row; this formula splits that one chain into a frost part and a wet-run part and gives the wet part its own width law. It adds no second chain (FT-3: one source, one continuous trail).

The `coolant_wet_run` formula is defined as:

`frost_px = min(trail_extent_px, frost_reach(T))` ; `n_frost = max(1, n_cover(frost_px))` (D.1; the head fan stamp is always drawn, and stamps `i < n_frost` are frost kind at their D.1 sizes) ; `frost_end_px = start(n_frost)` ; `n_wet = ceil(max(0, trail_extent_px - frost_end_px) / WET_PITCH_PX)` ; wet piece `j` (0 to `n_wet` - 1) at offset `o_j = frost_end_px + WET_PITCH_PX x j` ; `L = (WET_SRC_PX - WET_MIN_PX) / 2 + 1` ; `lvl_j = min(L - 1, floor(o_j x L / edge_len_px))` ; `wet_w_j = WET_SRC_PX - 2 x lvl_j`

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `trail_extent_px` | int px | 48 - `edge_len_px` | D.2 (age bound only for this row) | Length of the whole chain, frost plus wet run |
| `frost_reach(T)` | int px | 0-1152 | D.3 | The temperature bound, now an appearance bound |
| `frost_px` | int px | 0 - `trail_extent_px` | calculated | How much of the chain is drawn as frost |
| `n_cover`, `start` | int | - | D.1 | Stamp count and offsets of the frost stamps |
| `n_frost` | int | 1 - `n_cover` | calculated | Frost stamps; at least the head fan |
| `frost_end_px` | int px | 48 - 1152 | calculated | End of the last frost stamp; wet pieces begin here |
| `WET_PITCH_PX` | int px | 48 (fixed, = `STAMP_PX[1]`) | technical-artist | Wet pieces tile at the s pitch whatever the frost stamp sizes |
| `WET_SRC_PX` | int px | 6-12, even (default 8) **[PLACEHOLDER: `WET_SRC_PX`]** | tuning | Width of the widest wet piece |
| `WET_MIN_PX` | int px | 2 (fixed) | FT-22 minimum feature | Width of the narrowest wet piece |
| `L` | int | 3-6 | calculated | Number of width levels |
| `edge_len_px` | int px | 48 - `TRAIL_EDGE_MAX_PX` | Layout (D.2) | Polyline length; the width law is keyed to the whole edge, not to the extent, so a piece's width never changes as the trail grows |
| `wet_w_j` | int px | `WET_MIN_PX` - `WET_SRC_PX`, even | calculated | Width of piece `j` |

**Derivation of the constants.** *Constraint 1 (followable):* a six-tile (288 px) edge must show at least three distinct widths, so a player can order any two points that lie in different thirds of the run: `L` >= 3, hence `WET_SRC_PX` >= `WET_MIN_PX` + 4 = 6. *Constraint 2 (the pixel rules):* a width step is a whole 2 x 2 cell (FT-22), so widths go down in 2 px steps and `WET_MIN_PX` is 2. *Choice:* `WET_SRC_PX` 8 gives `L` 4, one 2 px step per quarter of the edge, the widths 8 / 6 / 4 / 2, and a lateral strip far inside FT-37's 48 px. *Constraint 3 (budget):* frost stamps are at least 48 px long, so `n_frost` <= `frost_end_px` / 48; `frost_end_px` is a multiple of 48 (sums of 48, 96 and 144), so `n_frost + n_wet` <= `frost_end_px` / 48 + ceil((`trail_extent_px` - `frost_end_px`) / 48) = ceil(`trail_extent_px` / 48), which is the 12-cell worst case that D.9 already budgets (a 576 px edge at 48 px stride, plus the accent): the wet run replaces frost slots one for one and `stamps_world` is unchanged.

**Output Range:** `wet_w_j` in {2, 4, 6, 8} at the defaults, monotone non-increasing in `o_j`; any two pieces in different width levels differ by at least 2 px in the right order, so any two points more than one level apart (72 px on a 288 px edge) are ordered by width. `n_wet` 0 while the extent is within the head frost stamp (the first `grow_steps` of a fresh fault). The wet run is static and freezes with the extent at the isolation step (FT-8); it dulls by D.5.
**Example (COMFORT, the home-ship default):** a sev 3 `1 LEAK/COOLANT` on a 288 px edge, `T` 15000 mC, `grow_steps` 100. D.2: `age_px` = 6 x 100 = 600, `reach_px` = `edge_len_px` 288, extent = `min(288, max(48, min(600, 288)))` = **288**. `frost_reach(15000)` = 0, so `frost_px` 0, `n_frost` = `max(1, n_cover(0))` = 1 (the m head, 96 px), `frost_end_px` = `start(1)` = 96. `n_wet` = ceil((288 - 96) / 48) = **4**, at offsets 96, 144, 192, 240. `L` 4: `lvl` = floor(o x 4 / 288) = 1, 2, 2, 3, so widths **6, 4, 4, 2** px. The chain has 1 + 4 = 5 pieces = `n_cover(288)` (D.1 starts 0, 96, 144, 192, 240, 288), the same count as before the ruling.
