At `grow_steps` 0 the extent is 48, `n_wet` 0 and only the head fan shows (revealed to 48 of 96 px); the first wet piece appears at `grow_steps` 17 (extent 102, revealed 6 of 48 px, D.10). **Cryo bay warming** (336 px edge, extent 336, `L` 4, level boundaries every 84 px): at wake (`T` -2000, reach 544) `frost_px` 336, `n_frost` 6, `n_wet` 0, all frost. At `T` 6000 (reach 192): `frost_px` 192, `n_frost` = `n_cover(192)` = 3, `frost_end_px` 192, `n_wet` = ceil(144 / 48) = 3 at 192, 240, 288, `lvl` = floor(o x 4 / 336) = 2, 2, 3, widths **4, 4, 2**; 3 + 3 = 6 = `n_cover(336)`. At `T` 10000 (reach 0): `n_frost` 1, `frost_end_px` 96, `n_wet` 5 at 96, 144, 192, 240, 288, `lvl` 1, 1, 2, 2, 3, widths **6, 6, 4, 4, 2**; 1 + 5 = 6. The extent is 336 in all three. A derelict dead coolant fault back-dated past `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS` at COMFORT draws the head fan and the whole run, greyed (D.5): the coolant verdict now reads at any temperature.

### D.3 `frost_reach`

The `frost_reach` formula is defined as:

`reach_px = 0` for `T >= T_EDGE[COMFORT/COOL]` ; `reach_px = R_k + floor((T_k - T) x (R_(k+1) - R_k) / (T_k - T_(k+1)))` for `T` between knot `k` (warmer) and knot `k + 1` (colder), when `FROST_REACH_INTERP` is true ; `reach_px = 1152` for `T <= T_EDGE[FREEZING/DEEP_COLD]` ; when `FROST_REACH_INTERP` is false, `reach_px = R_STEP[band(T)]` (a pure per-band step table)

The knots `T_k` are the Graph's temperature band edges (LS-7, `LSG:76`-`LSG:102`), **cited by position and never redefined here**: the COMFORT/COOL edge, the COOL/FROST edge, the FROST/FREEZING edge (the Graph's `COLD_DMG_ONSET_MC`) and the FREEZING/DEEP_COLD edge. This GDD supplies only the reach `R_k` at each edge.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `T` | int mC | about -40000 - 70000 | `compartment(cid).T` (true value, polled once per Graph step) | Room temperature; never the perceived value (FT-2) |
| `T_k` | int mC | the four LS-7 edges, warm to cold | Graph LS-7 (`LSG:76`-`LSG:102`), cited | Interpolation knots; if LS-7 moves, the knots move with it |
| `R_k` | int px | 0 / 480 / 960 / 1152 at the four edges, warm to cold **[PLACEHOLDER: `FROST_REACH_PX`]**, monotone, range 0-1152 | tuning | Reach at each band edge |
| `R_STEP[band]` | int px | COMFORT and warmer 0; COOL 480; FROST 960; FREEZING 1152; DEEP_COLD 1152 **[PLACEHOLDER: `FROST_REACH_PX`]** | tuning | Used only when `FROST_REACH_INTERP` is false (value at the band's colder edge) |
| `FROST_REACH_INTERP` | bool | default true **[PLACEHOLDER: `FROST_REACH_INTERP`]** | tuning (CP-7) | False restores the literal per-band table; retreat then jumps up to 480 px at a band key |
| `reach_px` | int px | 0-1152 | calculated | Maximum frost spread: the temperature bound of D.2 for `4 FREEZE`, and the bound of `frost_px` for `1 LEAK/COOLANT` (D.2d) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |

**Output Range:** 0..1152, non-increasing in `T` (monotone; a test sweeps `T` and asserts it). The largest intermediate product is 15000 x 480 = 7,200,000 (the widest segment, COOL/FROST to FROST/FREEZING), which fits int32. **Fixture floor (FT-11, knob constraint):** `frost_reach(T = -2000 mC)` >= 336 px (a function of `T` alone; the age bound is separate, D.2). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
**Example:** `T` -2000 mC lies between the COOL/FROST edge (`T_k` 0, `R_k` 480) and the FROST/FREEZING edge (-15000, 960): `480 + floor(2000 x 480 / 15000)` = 480 + 64 = **544** (>= 336, so the fixture floor holds). `T` 6000 mC, between the COMFORT/COOL edge (10000, 0) and the COOL/FROST edge (0, 480): `floor(4000 x 480 / 10000)` = **192**. `T` -20000 mC, between the FROST/FREEZING edge (-15000, 960) and the FREEZING/DEEP_COLD edge (-40000, 1152): `960 + floor(5000 x 192 / 25000)` = 960 + 38 = **998**. With `FROST_REACH_INTERP` false the same three temperatures give 960, 480 and 1152. (The edge values quoted are LS-7's as of this writing and are the Graph's to move.)

### D.4 `trail_density`

The `trail_density` formula is defined as:

`bucket = floor(intensity_pm / 100)` (0..10) ; `density_level = 0 if intensity_pm = 0 else 1 + min(3, floor(bucket / DENSITY_BUCKETS_PER_LEVEL))` ; for the ten ids with no Graph `intensity_pm` (FT-30), `density_level = sev` (static cover only; `27 PATCH_WEEP` is sev 1 only) and the moving media run iff the row's LIVE predicate holds: `2 SHORT` iff the faulted conductor's `flow` > 0 (arc pops), `3 OVERHEAT` iff the machine's `T` is above the Graph's OVERHEAT_OFF edge (`OVERHEAT_OFF_MC`, cited, Graph-owned; shimmer), and no other of the ten has a moving medium (`7 OVERLOAD`, `9 SCRAM`, `4 FREEZE`, `27 PATCH_WEEP` and `20 ATMO_COLD` / `21 ATMO_HOT` draw static stamps only; `22` and `23` draw nothing) ; `cover16 = 0 if density_level = 0 else max(DITHER_MIN_CELLS, COVER16_FLOOR[row], 4 x density_level)` with `COVER16_FLOOR[row]` = 8 for the **seam-weep mist** (`1 LEAK/GAS` on a SEAM subject) and 0 for every other row *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: at `A_seam` 1 the weep is about 15 permille, level 1, 4 of 16 cells on a 16 px puff lit only by the lamp, which can read as "nothing is wrong")* ; the sev 3+ anchor accent (FT-5) is drawn at `cover16 = 16` whatever the flow ; `drip_per_s = DRIP_PER_S[density_level]` ; `frames_active = FRAMES_ACTIVE[density_level]`

This system never states a formula for the Graph's `intensity_pm` (leak rate, hole area, AIRFLOW `|dP|`: `LSG:158`); it only buckets the value the descriptor carries, on the Graph's own 100-permille quantum so a level change always coincides with an `F/` key (`LSG:201`, D.10 `dirty_key_budget`).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `intensity_pm` | int | 0-1000 | descriptor (`LSG:158`) | Flow fraction in permille; 0 = STILL (FT-17) |
| `DENSITY_BUCKETS_PER_LEVEL` | int | **2-3** (default 3) **[PLACEHOLDER: `DENSITY_BUCKETS_PER_LEVEL`]** | tuning | Level width in 100-permille buckets. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the range was 2-5, but `bucket` tops out at 10 and level 4 needs `bucket` >= 3 x the width, so a width of 4 or 5 made level 4 unreachable inside the legal range; the range is now 2-3 and AC-167 sweeps it.)* |
| `density_level` | int | 0-4 | calculated | 0 = STILL (no moving media), 1-4 otherwise |
| `DITHER_MIN_CELLS` | int | 2 (fixed floor) | technical-artist | A non-zero density never draws fewer than 2 of the 16 cells of the 4 x 4 ordered dither (a lone 2 x 2 cell per 8 x 8 reads as noise, so 1 of 16 is never used); it is the absolute guard under every `COVER16_FLOOR` (a floor below 2 but above 0 is a lint error) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `COVER16_FLOOR[row]` | int | 0 or 2-16 (default 8 for the seam-weep mist, 0 otherwise) **[PLACEHOLDER: `COVER16_FLOOR`]** | tuning (design review) | Per-row legibility floor on `cover16`; the seam mist is the first row that exceeds the dither minimum *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `cover16` | int | 0, 4, 8, 12, 16 | calculated | Lit cells of a world-anchored 4 x 4 ordered dither of 2 x 2-px cells (binary alpha, never partial alpha: FT-23) |
| `DRIP_PER_S[level]` | int/s | {0, 1, 2, 4, 6} **[PLACEHOLDER: `DRIP_PER_S`]** | tuning | Drops per second; 6/s is one drop per 2 frames at 12 fps |
| `FRAMES_ACTIVE[level]` | int | {0, 2, 4, 6, 6} **[PLACEHOLDER: `FRAMES_ACTIVE`]** | tuning | Active frames of vent, dust and debris strips |

**Output Range:** `density_level` is 0 only when STILL; otherwise 1..4. Boundaries at the default width 3: `intensity_pm` 1-299 -> 1; 300-599 -> 2; 600-899 -> 3; 900-1000 -> 4; at the minimum width 2: 1-199 -> 1; 200-399 -> 2; 400-599 -> 3; 600-1000 -> 4 (level 4 is reachable at both legal widths). (orchestrator ruling: the technical-artist proposed a ten-tier `ceil(intensity_pm / 100)` ladder; the written FT-30 and transition T7 already assume a `density_level` scheme, so the four-level scheme is kept and the technical-artist's finding survives as the `DITHER_MIN_CELLS` floor.) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
**Example:** a hull breach whose descriptor carries `intensity_pm` 625 (taken as given from the Graph's hole-area scaling): `bucket` 6, `density_level` = 1 + min(3, floor(6 / 3)) = 3, `cover16` 12 of 16, and the debris and wind strips run `FRAMES_ACTIVE[3]` = 6 frames. A trickle at `intensity_pm` 80: `bucket` 0, level 1, `cover16` 4, 1 drop per second, 2 active frames. The seam weep at `A_seam` 1 (`intensity_pm` 15): level 1, `cover16` = max(2, 8, 4) = **8**, 2 active frames. The same plate at its pre-stage (`intensity_pm` 0): level 0, hairline only. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

### D.5 `dullness`

The `dullness` formula is defined as:

`dull_pm = min(1000, floor(mode_steps x 1000 / DRIED_AFTER_STEPS))` ; `dull_step = DULL_STEPS - 1 if mode_steps >= DRIED_AFTER_STEPS else floor(mode_steps x (DULL_STEPS - 1) / DRIED_AFTER_STEPS)` ; `glow_step = max(0, GLOW_STEPS - floor(dull_pm x GLOW_STEPS / EMISSIVE_OFF_DULL_PM))` ; `dull_step_shown = min(dull_step, DULL_STEPS - 2)` while `cap_applies` and `dull_step` otherwise, with `cap_applies` = the descriptor is LIVE (D.4: `intensity_pm` > 0, or the row's LIVE predicate true) or its row is an ongoing static row (7 OVERLOAD, 27 PATCH_WEEP; FT-17; 4 FREEZE and 9 SCRAM are latched faults and are not capped) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* ; fuel only: `fuel_dried = (dull_step_shown = DULL_STEPS - 1)`, a palette swap with no intermediate stop and no dither *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: (1) the age is `mode_steps`, the trail's own mode age, GA-T, so a pipe that wore for 30 minutes and then leaks starts at `dull_step` 0 and `fuel_dried` false; (2) the final, dried stop is reached only at `mode_steps` >= `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS` (the ramp's end, `dull_pm` 1000), so it can never start before the 7500-step floor, and the steps below it divide the ramp evenly; (3) `fuel_dried` flips at the final stop only, not at half the ramp; (4) the integer forms are explicit, so an odd `DULL_STEPS` is defined)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings: `dull_step_shown`, the LIVE cap, Open Questions 6d.)*

Dullness is the visual consequence of age, with **one LIVE cap** (default applied 2026-10-27, pending user confirmation, Open Questions 6d): below the dried stop there is no LIVE term in the ramp (CP-8), so a live leak and an isolated one of the same `mode_steps` carry the same `dull_step`, but a LIVE descriptor is shown at most at stop `DULL_STEPS` - 2, so no live trail ever wears the dried verdict. `mode_steps` restarts at every mode entry and every `sev` change, so a live trail is young by construction; the one exception, a leak isolated early and reopened after `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS` steps (live with a dried-stop age), is what the cap catches: it is shown at stop `DULL_STEPS` - 2, aged but not dried. The cap releases on the frame the descriptor stops being LIVE, so an isolation lands the dried stop in one palette step (intended: the leak is old and the valve is the player's own act; AC-36 therefore holds for every `mode_steps` below `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS`, and AC-177 covers the exception). **The cap's reach** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: a row with no LIVE axis whose descriptor is an ongoing condition (7 OVERLOAD, 27 PATCH_WEEP) is held at the same stop for as long as the descriptor exists (state DRAWN_STATIC, no release), and a latched fault (4 FREEZE, 9 SCRAM) is not capped (FT-17, AC-177). `glow_step` stays on the uncapped `dull_pm`: the emissive rows with a LIVE predicate (rows 2 and 3) are moving media (arc pops, shimmer) that are never dulled, and the always-static emissive rows (7, 9) have no LIVE state to cap. It applies to **static stamps only**; moving media (drip, mist, debris, arc pops) are never dulled (FT-17). The emissive overlay of a static scorch, arc box or ember edge follows `glow_step`, one function of `dull_pm` (so SCRAM's decaying rim and every other overlay share one rule); the arc *pops* (a moving medium) are never dulled and continue while the descriptor lives. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `mode_steps` | int | 0 - unbounded | descriptor, **GA-T (adopted, LS-14)**: `step_no - mode_since_step`, unfrozen by isolation, dominant descriptor only (a non-dominant NODE or EDGE descriptor takes `dull_step` 0, FT-8) | Age of the current mode and severity; derelict dead faults are authored large by back-dating `mode_since_step` (`LSG:226`, Derelict Authoring D.11) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS` | int | 7500-36000 (default 12000 = 20 min) **[PLACEHOLDER: `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS`]** | tuning (CP-8) | `mode_steps` at which the ramp ends and the dried stop is reached. Lower bound 7500 = 12.5 min, the longest live MVP clock (`LSG:667` D.15), so no live fault reaches the dried look before it finishes; upper bound 36000 (1 h) so authored derelict faults can reach it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `dull_pm` | int | 0-1000 | calculated | Permille of the ramp |
| `DULL_STEPS` | int | 4-8, any integer (default 4) **[PLACEHOLDER: `DULL_STEPS`]** | tuning (CP-8) | Number of palette steps, the last of which is the dried stop (the art-director's scan-matrix alternative is 8) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `dull_step` | int | 0..`DULL_STEPS` - 1 | calculated | Index into the per-medium stop table; at 4 steps the fresh-hue cells of the 16-cell dither are `HUE_CELLS` = {16, 11, 5, 0} (Ice, Amber, Cyan only; the table is authored per `DULL_STEPS` in the asset spec) |
| `dull_step_shown` | int | 0..`DULL_STEPS` - 1 | calculated | The stop the palette actually draws: `dull_step`, capped at `DULL_STEPS` - 2 while `cap_applies` (the descriptor is LIVE, or its row is an ongoing static row 7 or 27). Constraint: the live stop must sit strictly below the dried stop, `DULL_STEPS` - 2 < `DULL_STEPS` - 1, and be at least 0, which holds for every legal `DULL_STEPS` (4-8 gives a cap of 2-6) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| `GLOW_STEPS` | int | 4 (fixed) | technical-artist | Quantisation of the emissive overlay level |
| `EMISSIVE_OFF_DULL_PM` | int | 250-750 (default 500) **[PLACEHOLDER: `EMISSIVE_OFF_DULL_PM`]** | tuning (CP-8) | `dull_pm` at which the overlay is fully off; expressed in permille so it is independent of `DULL_STEPS` |
| `glow_step` | int | 0..`GLOW_STEPS` | calculated | 4 = peak overlay, 0 = off |
| `fuel_dried` | bool | - | calculated | Fuel Ember draws for every step below the last, the dried stop at the last step only; never a mix (any blend of Fuel Ember with a grey passes through the flesh band: technical-artist consult) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** `dull_step` 0..`DULL_STEPS` - 1, monotone non-decreasing in `mode_steps`, 0 at `mode_steps` 0, equal to `DULL_STEPS` - 1 only at `mode_steps` >= `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS`; `glow_step` monotone non-increasing, 0 for `dull_pm` >= `EMISSIVE_OFF_DULL_PM`. Every dried stop is a palette hex and the floor of every ramp is Hull Grey (contrast 1.90:1 against Graphite lit, `TRAIL_MIN_CONTRAST_X100` 190); Hull Grey is a thin-element colour, so dried interiors are drawn as edges and stains, never as a plane. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
**Example (defaults 12000 and 4: the stops change at `mode_steps` 4000, 8000 and 12000, 6.7 minutes apart):** the cryo-bay leak at `mode_steps` 168: `dull_pm` = floor(168000 / 12000) = 14, `dull_step` 0, `glow_step` 4 (no overlay on coolant anyway). The same leak at 4000 (6.7 min): `dull_pm` 333, `dull_step` = floor(4000 x 3 / 12000) = 1 (11 of 16 cells Ice, 5 Hull Grey), still dripping: slightly aged; at 3999 it is still 0. A SCRAM scorch at 3000: `dull_pm` 250, `glow_step` = max(0, 4 - floor(250 x 4 / 500)) = 2, a half-strength rim. A derelict dead frost fan back-dated to 18000: `dull_pm` 1000 (clamped), `dull_step` 3 (all Hull Grey). A derelict fuel stain at 40000: `dull_step` 3 = `DULL_STEPS` - 1, so `fuel_dried` (the dried stop, an ember-dim stain with value descending first: the art-director's rule, Visual/Audio). **A live fuel leak at `mode_steps` 7500 (the longest live MVP clock): `dull_step` 1, `fuel_dried` false** (the former half-ramp rule gave `fuel_dried` true from 6000). **Wear then leak:** a fuel pipe that was WORN for 30 minutes and then crosses `LEAK_HP` has `mode_steps` 0 at its first LEAK frame: `dull_step` 0, `fuel_dried` false, even though its `since_steps` is 18000. **Odd width:** `DULL_STEPS` 5 gives stops at 3000, 6000, 9000 and 12000. **Live cap (default applied 2026-10-27):** at the defaults a coolant leak isolated at `mode_steps` 3000, left, and reopened at `mode_steps` 15000 is live; `dull_step` there is 3 (all Hull Grey, the dried stop), and `dull_step_shown` = min(3, 4 - 2) = **2** (5 of 16 Ice cells: aged, not dried); the same history on a fuel leak keeps `fuel_dried` false; the valve closes one step later, the descriptor is STILL, the cap releases and the stop is 3 (and `fuel_dried` true) in that one frame. A derelict dead fault (`intensity_pm` 0, never LIVE) is unaffected. **Ongoing static rows (re-review):** a `7 OVERLOAD` scorch or a `27 PATCH_WEEP` weep present at `mode_steps` 15000 shows `dull_step_shown` 2, not 3; a derelict `4 FREEZE` sheath or `9 SCRAM` scorch at 15000 shows 3 (the dried verdict), uncapped. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings; re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

### D.6 `trail_stamp_variant`

The `trail_stamp_variant` formula is defined as:

`h = rng_derive(seed, "trail.vis", (subject_id << 20) | stamp_index)` ; `h63 = h & 0x7FFFFFFFFFFFFFFF` ; `variant = h63 mod N_VARIANTS[behaviour][size_idx]` ; `phase_offset = (h63 >> 8) mod N_FRAMES[medium]` ; `flip = (h63 >> 16) & 1` ; `seed = world_seed` as exposed through the kernel's read view of `kernel.clock` (`KERN:477`), else `TRAIL_VIS_SEED_FALLBACK` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Value-keyed choices are not hashed** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the BREACH rib-versus-hole choice and `hole_variant` (intensity bucket 5-6, 7-8, 9-10 select the three authored holes, row 5) are chosen by the descriptor's value, so the same value gives the same stamp on every client with no hash; the hash chooses only among interchangeable variants. The hash is reached only through the injected `hash_fn` seam (FT-39). `rng_derive` is the kernel's pure hash (D.10, `KERN:470`), cited by registry name and not restated. **Integer semantics under GDScript** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: GDScript's `int` is a signed 64-bit value, so a 64-bit hash can arrive negative (a negative `mod` result is a negative variant index) and `>>` is an arithmetic shift; the hash is therefore **masked to 63 bits before any use** (`h63`, always >= 0), and every integer division in this GDD that is not already non-negative by construction uses `floori` and `posmod`. The bit pattern `0x8000000000000000` (the signed value -2^63) and `-1` (all ones) are both legal hash outputs and both mask to non-negative values. Here the counter argument is a **key** (subject and stamp), not a draw index: this system has no block, so no counter ever advances and no draw order exists.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `subject_id` | int | < 2^20 | descriptor (the Graph asserts the bound, LS-9) | Stable subject id |
| `stamp_index` | int | 0 - 2^20 - 1 | calculated (chain ordinal; patch ordinal for D.2b; puddle ordinal for D.2c) | Position of the stamp |
| `seed` | int (signed 64-bit bit pattern of a u64) | any 64-bit pattern | `world_seed` from the kernel's read view of `kernel.clock` (confirmation routed) else `TRAIL_VIS_SEED_FALLBACK` 0 **[PLACEHOLDER: `TRAIL_VIS_SEED_FALLBACK`]** | Identical on every client and after every restore *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `N_VARIANTS` | int | 2-4 per sheet (default 3 for s, 2 for m and l) **[PLACEHOLDER: `N_VARIANTS`]** | asset spec | Variants per behaviour x size |
| `N_FRAMES[medium]` | int | mist 6, drip 8, debris 6, dust 4, arc 6, shimmer 4 **[PLACEHOLDER: `N_FRAMES`]** | asset spec | Frames per loop (the art-director's strip lengths) |
| `variant`, `phase_offset`, `flip` | int | by modulus | calculated | Per-stamp choices |

**Output Range:** `variant` in 0..`N_VARIANTS` - 1; `phase_offset` in 0..`N_FRAMES` - 1; `flip` 0 or 1. Subjects of different kinds that share an id collide on identical variants, which is harmless (visual only). The modulo bias is below 2^-60 and is irrelevant to a sprite choice. Arc pops do not use `phase_offset`: they share one world phase (D.8b). Engine global random functions are forbidden (lint).
**Example (the hash value is assumed; the real one is opaque until the kernel ADR picks `mix64`):** with `h` = 0x2C9 (713), `N_VARIANTS` 3 and mist `N_FRAMES` 6: `variant` = 713 mod 3 = 2, `phase_offset` = (713 >> 8) mod 6 = 2, `flip` = (713 >> 16) & 1 = 0. **Signed edge cases (AC-134):** `h` = -1 (all ones) masks to 2^63 - 1: `variant` = (2^63 - 1) mod 3 = 1, `phase_offset` = (2^55 - 1) mod 6 = 1, `flip` = 1; `h` = 0x8000000000000000 (signed -2^63) masks to 0: variant 0, phase 0, flip 0; no case yields a negative index. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

### D.7 `trail_reconcile_cost`

The `trail_reconcile_cost` formula is defined as:

keyed step: `cost_us = n_poll x C_POLL_US + n_F x C_SPAWN_US + n_BC x C_BAND_US + n_stamp_changed x C_STAMP_US` ; `ALL`: `cost_all_us = n_desc x C_DESC_US + n_stamps x C_STAMP_US + n_pool_ops x C_POOL_US` ; check: `cost_all_us <= TRAIL_RECONCILE_BUDGET_MS x 1000` (a logging threshold, never a cut-off)

The key counts come from the Graph's `dirty_key_budget` (`LSG:532`, cited by registry name, never restated) and are bounded by `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256; above that the kernel collapses the notification to `ALL` (`KERN:466`). The unit costs are placeholders pending spike S5; this is a consumer-side cost model, and the Graph's own budget is not this system's.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `n_F`, `n_BC` | int | 0-256 | per-family counts of `dirty_key_budget` (`F/` keys; `B/` + `C/` keys) | Keys this step |
| `n_poll`, `n_desc` | int | 0 - `N_DESC_DESIGN_MAX` | `failures()` size | Descriptors polled (once per Graph step) or re-read (`ALL`) |
| `n_stamps`, `n_stamp_changed` | int | 0 - `TRAIL_MAX_STAMPS` | D.1, D.2 | Stamps in the pool; stamps changed this step |
| `n_pool_ops` | int | 0-256 | pool | Pool acquire and release operations |
| `C_POLL_US`, `C_SPAWN_US`, `C_BAND_US`, `C_STAMP_US`, `C_DESC_US`, `C_POOL_US`, `C_FIELD_US`, `C_FRAME_US` | int us | 2 / 30 / 10 / 3 / 15 / 5 / 1 / 4 **[PLACEHOLDER: unit costs]** | spike S5 | Unit costs (the last two price a pan frame and an animation flip, below) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `N_DESC_DESIGN_MAX` | int | 32-128 (default 64) **[PLACEHOLDER: `N_DESC_DESIGN_MAX`]** | tuning | Design maximum of live descriptors |
| `TRAIL_RECONCILE_BUDGET_MS` | float ms | 0.5-6 (default 4) **[PLACEHOLDER: `TRAIL_RECONCILE_BUDGET_MS`]** | tuning | Logging threshold for an in-play `ALL` (a diff); `STEP_BUDGET_MS` 4 is the kernel's, not this system's *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `TRAIL_RESTORE_BUDGET_MS` | float ms | 4-16 (default 8) **[PLACEHOLDER: `TRAIL_RESTORE_BUDGET_MS`]** | tuning | Logging threshold for a restore or instantiation `ALL` (a full rebuild) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |

**Output Range:** steady (0-3 keys): about 130 us; the MVP worst cascade (about 38 keys): about 766 us (640 us of key work plus the poll term of the 63 descriptors of `fx_mvp_worst`, 126 us; 946 us at the technical-artist's `C_STAMP_US` 6) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the poll term was omitted)*; `ALL` at the MVP: about 760 us; `ALL` at the **three published fixtures** (D.9 table; *Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the earlier design-maximum line assumed 384 stamps, but a 49-edge cascade at 6 stamps plus a full set of room frost in 12 rooms is 618*): `fx_steady` (3 descriptors, 17 stamps, 10 pool ops) = 3 x 15 + 17 x 3 + 10 x 5 = **146 us**; `fx_mvp_worst` (63 descriptors, 661 stamps, 150 pool ops; 12 puddle descriptors added by the re-review) = 63 x 15 + 661 x 3 + 150 x 5 = **3,678 us**; `fx_design_max` (64 descriptors, 618 stamps, 150 pool ops) = 64 x 15 + 618 x 3 + 150 x 5 = **3,564 us**. The unit cost `C_STAMP_US` 3 is the best case: the technical-artist's own estimate is about 6 writes per stamp, which with `C_STAMP_US` 6 gives 5,661 us and 5,418 us for the last two. **Two thresholds, not one** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the single 2 ms threshold fired on every legal worst-case world, and a gameplay-time `ALL` was costed as a full rebuild)*: a **restore or instantiation `ALL`** is a full rebuild, once, and is logged above `TRAIL_RESTORE_BUDGET_MS` 8 **[PLACEHOLDER]** (the modelled worst is 5.7 ms; the measured pass thresholds of spike S5 are the same 8 ms and 12 ms); an **in-play `ALL`** (a cascade above `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` 256 while the game runs) is modelled as a **diff against the pool**, not a rebuild: `cost_all_play_us = n_desc x C_DESC_US + n_stamp_changed x C_STAMP_US + n_pool_ops x C_POOL_US`, with `n_stamp_changed` at most the stamps of the newly failed descriptors (221 at `fx_mvp_worst`, 294 at `fx_design_max`), which gives 2,358 us and 2,592 us at `C_STAMP_US` 3 and 3,021 us and 3,474 us at 6, logged above `TRAIL_RECONCILE_BUDGET_MS` 4 **[PLACEHOLDER]** (raised from 2). Both are logging thresholds and never cut anything (FT-16, FT-19). Both estimates are `[UNV]` until measured; the **spike S5 pass thresholds** are [PLACEHOLDER] measured `ALL` <= 8 ms at `fx_mvp_worst` and <= 12 ms at `fx_design_max` on the reference machine of AC-83 (half a 60 Hz frame and three quarters of one).
**Example:** steady, 17 descriptors and 3 `F/` keys: 17 x 2 + 3 x 30 = **124 us**. MVP worst cascade, 63 descriptors polled, 12 `F/`, 10 `B/` or `C/`, 60 stamps changed: 63 x 2 + 12 x 30 + 10 x 10 + 60 x 3 = **766 us** (640 us without the poll term; 946 us with `C_STAMP_US` 6). `ALL` after a restore, 17 descriptors, 102 stamps, 40 pool ops: 17 x 15 + 102 x 3 + 40 x 5 = 255 + 306 + 200 = **761 us** = 0.76 ms <= 2 ms.

**Frame classes and the p99 target** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the 0.25 ms p99 was stated per render frame, but a keyed cascade costs 640 us by this very model)*. A Graph step is one tick in six, so at 60 Hz one render frame in six is a **step frame** (the frame that polls and applies keys) and five are **idle frames**; a p99 over all frames therefore lands on step frames. **Four frame classes, four budgets** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: only idle and step frames were budgeted, but a walking player makes almost every frame a pan frame and every fifth an animation-flip frame; the step budget's "2x headroom" was false (1.0 ms against a modelled 0.64 ms that also omitted the poll term, 0.77 ms with it, 0.95 ms at `C_STAMP_US` 6))*: a frame is classed by the most expensive event it carries. **Idle** (no step, no key, no frame-index change, no camera-field change): `TRAIL_FRAME_MS_P99` 0.25 ms (FT-41 rule 1: they write nothing, so the cost is scheduler overhead only). **Pan** (the camera field moved; the in-field test of FT-19 is re-evaluated for every trail): `cost_pan_us = n_trails x C_FIELD_US + n_enter_leave x C_SPAWN_US`, with `C_FIELD_US` 1 **[PLACEHOLDER]**, `n_trails` at most `N_DESC_DESIGN_MAX` 64 and `n_enter_leave` at most 8 per frame, which is 304 us, budget `TRAIL_PAN_FRAME_MS_P99` 0.5 ms **[PLACEHOLDER]**. **Animation flip** (the stepped-strip frame index changed, one render frame in five at 12 fps; no step, no key): `cost_anim_us = n_moving x C_FRAME_US`, with `C_FRAME_US` 4 **[PLACEHOLDER]** and `n_moving` at most 128 moving-media sprites, which is 512 us, budget `TRAIL_ANIM_FRAME_MS_P99` 0.75 ms **[PLACEHOLDER]** if the index is written to scene nodes; if spike S2 shows the frame index can be shader-driven from `present_time` (a shared uniform, V/A.13), an animation flip writes nothing and joins the idle class at 0.25 ms (S2 decides). **Step** (the frame that polls and applies keys, excluding `ALL`): `TRAIL_STEP_FRAME_MS_P99` **1.5 ms [PLACEHOLDER]** (raised from 1.0: 1.5 ms is 1.96 times the modelled 0.766 ms with the poll term and 1.6 times the 0.946 ms of the technical-artist's own unit cost, so the headroom is about twice). The cost of a step frame is O(trails currently growing, keyed or changed, plus the poll), not O(stamps). The profiling criterion measures a walking player so all four classes occur in their real mix (AC-83, AC-174, AC-183).

### D.8 `anim_frame`

The `anim_frame` formula is defined as:

`frame = 0 if reduced_motion else (floor(present_time x TRAIL_ANIM_FPS) + phase_offset) mod N_FRAMES[medium]` ; `drip_phase`, mist, debris, dust and shimmer use this form; the growth tip uses `tip_frame = floor(trail_extent_px / STAMP_STRIDE_PX) mod 3` (a function of extent, not of time)

`present_time` is the one float in this system: the Pipeline's render-rate clock (PR-9), frozen while the kernel is PAUSED, used for animation phase only, never hashed and never read by the Graph. Using a float here is allowed because the result selects a sprite frame and cannot affect any simulation state or replicated value; a stepped (10-12 Hz) result is not required to be bit-identical between clients, only equal in kind (FT-28's hold frame is).

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `present_time` | float s | >= 0, frozen while PAUSED | Pipeline PR-9 | The only animation clock; never wall-clock, never the tick counter (PR-8 rule 6) |
| `TRAIL_ANIM_FPS` | int | 6-24 (default 12; safe 8-12) **[PLACEHOLDER: `TRAIL_ANIM_FPS`]** | tuning | Stepped media rate (the 12 fps horror-rig precedent, `PIPE:202`) |
| `phase_offset` | int | 0 - `N_FRAMES` - 1 | D.6 | Per-trail phase |
| `N_FRAMES[medium]` | int | 2-12 | asset spec | Frames per loop |
| `reduced_motion` | bool | - | Settings (FT-36, provisional) | Holds frame 0 (FT-28) |
| `STAMP_STRIDE_PX` | int px | 48 | D.1 (`STAMP_PX[1]`) | Run stride for the growth-tip frame |

**Output Range:** `frame` in 0..`N_FRAMES` - 1; `tip_frame` in 0..2. Under reduced motion every moving medium is on frame 0, its authored hold frame (FT-28).
**Example:** `present_time` 5.40 s at 12 fps: floor(64.8) = 64; phase 2; mist `N_FRAMES` 6: (64 + 2) mod 6 = **0**. Reduced motion: 0. A trail of extent 336: `tip_frame` = floor(336 / 48) mod 3 = 7 mod 3 = 1.

### D.8b `arc_flash_share`

*(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: rewritten around one rate mechanism and one hold level. The earlier text divided the rate by the arc count (`floor(3 / n)`, a held glow from four arcs) while also putting every arc on one shared world phase, which makes the division moot; it defined two hold levels (60 percent and 88 percent of peak); it claimed "the region only grows" with the field, which is false at the smallest ladder step (384 x 216 gives a region of 9,216 px, where two l arcs reached about 11 percent, over the 10 percent counted threshold); it let a swing of 240 permille through for three arcs (13.5 percent, over the 9 percent ceiling); and it made `n_arcs_region` depend on the camera, so panning flipped the rate.)*

The `arc_flash_share` formula is defined as:

`ARC_FLICKER_PER_S = ceil(floor(TRAIL_ANIM_FPS / ARC_HOLD_FRAMES) / 2)` ; **every live arc flickers on one shared world phase at that one rate** (the rate is not divided by anything; the counted transitions in any region equal one source's whatever the arc count, and the rate cannot change when the camera pans) ; `arc_rate_eff = 0` (a held glow at `ARC_HELD_GLOW_PM`) when reduce-flashing is on, else `ARC_FLICKER_PER_S` ; amplitude: `swing_i_pm = min(ARC_SWING_PM, floor(FLASH_REGION_FRACTION_PM x REGION_MIN_PX x 1000 / (n_arcs_room x A_emis_i x Y_PEAK_PM)))` ; the held glow level is `ARC_HELD_GLOW_PM` of peak, the one hold level for reduce-flashing and for the reduced-motion hold pose *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

Counted per the Pipeline's `flash_count` (PR-13, `PIPE:228`): an opposing luminance change of at least 10 percent in a rolling 1 s window, per light, per region (3 x 3 grid, each region a ninth of the displayed picture) and on the frame mean. **Counting unit (ratified 2026-10-27, user ruling D91 ii): a flash is `ceil(n_transitions / 2)` opposing transitions in the window.** *Derivation:* an arc changes state every `ARC_HOLD_FRAMES` frames, so it makes `floor(TRAIL_ANIM_FPS / ARC_HOLD_FRAMES)` transitions per second and `F = ceil(n / 2)` flashes. At the defaults (12 fps, hold 3) n = 4 and F = **2**; a window that catches one extra edge has n = 5 and F = ceil(5 / 2) = **3**, which equals `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` 3 and still passes; n = 7 gives F = 4 and fails. The earlier `floor(fps / (2 x hold))` rounded an odd transition count down (9 fps, hold 3: 3 transitions, 1 flash by the floor form, 2 by the ruled unit), so the rate is now derived with the ceiling; for every even n the two forms agree and no default changes. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* **The region the amplitude is derived at is the smallest one the ladder can present**: `REGION_MIN_PX` = 9,216 px, the region of the smallest reference field (1080p at `k_auto` + 2 = 5: 384 x 216, so 128 x 72); the larger regions (11,360 px at 1440p `k_auto` + 2, 14,400 px at 2160p `k_auto` + 2, 25,560 px at the 640 x 360 default) only dilute the same swing, so the clamp holds at every field and every pan. **`n_arcs_room` is world-fixed and counted over a neighbourhood, not a compartment** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the compartment count let arcs in adjacent compartments share one 128 x 72 region and exceed the 9 percent ceiling)*: for arc `i` it is the count (itself included) of live arcs (`2 SHORT` descriptors whose LIVE predicate is true) whose anchors lie within `|dx| <= NB_DX_PX` 272 and `|dy| <= NB_DY_PX` 216 base px of arc `i`'s anchor, across compartment boundaries (the smallest region's 128 x 72 plus one l cell of 144, so any two arcs whose emissive cells can fall in one region count each other); every term of the regional sum then satisfies `n_arcs_room >= |R|` for any region `R` it lies in, so the sum is at most the ceiling. It is computed from world coordinates in the per-feed pre-pass (FT-39), independent of the camera and the compartment ids; assuming that they could all share one screen region is the conservative case. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ARC_FLICKER_PER_S` | int | 0-3 (default 2) | **derived, read-only; not a knob and not a placeholder** (`ceil(floor(TRAIL_ANIM_FPS / ARC_HOLD_FRAMES) / 2)` from the next two) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | Nominal flashes per second per arc; the rolling-window worst case (lint below) must be <= `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` |
| `TRAIL_ANIM_FPS` | int | 6-24 (default 12) | D.8 | Stepped-strip rate *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `ARC_HOLD_FRAMES` | int | **3-6** (default 3) **[PLACEHOLDER: `ARC_HOLD_FRAMES`]** | tuning | Frames each flicker state is held; 1 and 2 are forbidden: hold 1 is 12 transitions/s (6 flashes/s) and hold 2 at 12 fps derives exactly 3 flashes/s, which a rolling window that catches one extra edge counts as 4 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: the minimum was 2)* |
| `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` | int | 3 | registry constant (`REG:657`), locked | The clamp |
| `n_arcs_room` | int | 1-8 | calculated from the Graph's `compartment_id` of the live `2 SHORT` descriptors | World-fixed arc count that scales the amplitude *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `A_emis_i` | int px | 922 / 3686 / 8294 (s / m / l cell at 40 percent emissive) | technical-artist consult | Emissive area of arc i *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `Y_PEAK_PM` | int | 577 (Power Amber, 0.577) | technical-artist consult | Peak relative luminance *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `REGION_MIN_PX` | int px | 9,216 | derived from the Pipeline's zoom ladder (D.5 `zoom_ladder`, cited): smallest field 384 x 216 | Pixels in the smallest region any display presents; re-derive if the ladder changes *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `FLASH_REGION_FRACTION_PM` | int | 90 | technical-artist (below the 100 permille counted threshold) | Regional swing ceiling *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `ARC_SWING_PM` | int | 0-240 (default 240) **[PLACEHOLDER: `ARC_SWING_PM`]** | tuning | Artistic maximum depth; the formula only ever lowers it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `swing_i_pm` | int | 0-240 | calculated | The depth arc i actually dips *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `ARC_HELD_GLOW_PM` | int | 400-800 (default 600) **[PLACEHOLDER: `ARC_HELD_GLOW_PM`]** | tuning | The single held level, permille of peak: reduce-flashing glow and the reduced-motion arc hold pose *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |

**Output Range:** `arc_rate_eff` 0..3 and the same for every arc; `swing_i_pm` 0..240 and never above the bound that keeps `sum_i(A_emis_i x Y_peak x swing_i) / REGION_MIN_PX` below 0.09. Worked amplitudes (`ARC_SWING_PM` 240): one l arc 173, two l arcs 86 each, three l arcs 57 each; one m arc 240 (bound 389), two m arcs 194, three m arcs 129; s arcs 240 up to three. The common phase makes overlapping arcs transition together, so even at the shared rate the counted transitions equal one source's rather than the sum; this narrows D.6 for the arc class only (no per-trail phase offset). **Knob-pair lint (closing-stage ruling, decided here):** `ARC_FLICKER_PER_S` is derived, and inside the permitted knob ranges (`TRAIL_ANIM_FPS` 6-24, `ARC_HOLD_FRAMES` 3-6) the pair (24 fps, hold 3) would derive 4 flashes per second nominal and 5 in a rolling window; the build lint therefore **rejects** any (`TRAIL_ANIM_FPS`, `ARC_HOLD_FRAMES`) pair whose **rolling-window** flash count `F_win = ceil((floor(TRAIL_ANIM_FPS / ARC_HOLD_FRAMES) + 1) / 2)` exceeds `FLASH_MAX_PER_S` 3 (the `+ 1` is the one extra edge a rolling 1 s window can catch: at the defaults 12 fps and hold 3 the nominal 4 transitions become 5 and `F_win` = 3, at the limit and passing; (12, 2) gives 7 and `F_win` 4 and fails, where the nominal form `ceil(floor(fps / hold) / 2)` passed it at exactly 3; (24, 3) gives 9 and 5 and fails), and never clamps it silently *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review: a photosensitivity safety item)* (a silent clamp would change the hold frames the art-director authored). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
**Example:** one bus-overload l arc (`n_arcs_room` 1): rate 2 flashes/s (`ARC_HOLD_FRAMES` 3 at 12 fps: 12 / 3 = 4 transitions/s = 2 flashes/s), amplitude 173, regional swing = 8294 x 0.577 x 0.173 / 9216 = **8.98 percent** < 9. Two l arcs: 86 each, 2 x 8294 x 0.577 x 0.086 / 9216 = **8.93 percent**. Three l arcs: 57 each, **8.88 percent**; the earlier flat 240 would have given 3 x 8294 x 0.577 x 0.240 / 9216 = 37 percent for three. The same three arcs at 2160p `k_auto` + 2 (region 14,400 px) change the regional mean by 5.7 percent, and at 1440p (11,360 px) by 7.2 percent. Rate is 2 per second in every one of these cases, because it does not depend on the count. Reduce flashing: every arc holds at 600 permille of peak with no transitions. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

### D.9 `trail_budget_at_k`

The `trail_budget_at_k` formula is defined as:

`D_FT = L_sets x P_trail x M_floor + sum over wall rows of M_wall(row) + I_wall + P_media`, with `M_wall(row)` = 1 + (1 if the row carries an emissive stamp, else 0), read from the table of record, and **`D_FT <= D_FT_MAX` conditional on spike S2** proving the per-instance data route (V/A.13) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the model had no 2D light-set term and no Y-sort interleave term; re-review: `M_wall` is data-driven, no longer charged as 2 per row unconditionally, and the conditionality is stated here)* ; `D_FT <= D_FT_MAX` ; `stamps_world = sum_desc(n_cover(E_desc) + 1) + n_patches_total + n_puddle_total` ; `stamps_world <= TRAIL_MAX_STAMPS` (logging threshold) ; `stamps_in_field(k) <= stamps_world` ; `layers_per_px <= TRAIL_OVERDRAW_MAX` ; mist mass per trail <= `MIST_MASS_PX_PER_TRAIL`, venting mass per vent <= `VENT_MASS_PX_PER_VENT` ; live particle emitters <= 12 only if the particle spike re-enables particles (otherwise 0)

The field at ladder step k is the Pipeline's (D.5 `zoom_ladder`, cited, not restated); the table below lists the values read from it at the three reference displays under `expand`. **The worst case is world-wide, not per field:** at the low steps the field is a multiple of the whole MVP world, so the binding cap is every active descriptor in the loaded world being on screen, and k only changes the on-screen pixel size and which cap is reachable. Because nothing on screen is culled (FT-19), the caps below are **logging thresholds and content-lint bounds, never runtime drops**. **Logging frequency (closing-stage ruling, decided here):** every threshold in this system is edge-triggered, one log line per crossing from below to above the threshold per session (re-armed when the value falls back below it), never per frame and never per step, matching the one-diagnostic-line-per-session precedent of the unknown-id and missing-geometry cases.

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `k` | int | 1 - `k_auto` + 2 | Pipeline C6 (`PIPE:161`) | Ladder step; `k_auto` is 3 / 4 / 6 at 1080p / 1440p / 2160p |
| `P_trail`, `M_floor` | int | 1-2; 2 | asset spec; technical-artist | Floor-band atlas pages; materials per page (lit mark, additive emissive) |
| `R_wall`, `M_wall(row)` | int | 0 - `WALL_ROWS_MAX` (16) ; 1-2 | Layout (loaded world); the table of record | Host-face rows carrying a wall trail (one batch per row because wall trails sort with their face); materials per row (2 only where the row carries an emissive stamp) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `P_media` | int | 1-2 | asset spec | Stepped-media pages in the dust band |
| `L_sets` | int | 1-3 (default 1) **[PLACEHOLDER: `L_SETS`]** | Lighting (2D light masks, LO-8, LO-14) and spike S2 | Distinct 2D light-mask or shadow-mask sets the floor-mark materials are drawn under; each set splits the floor-band batch, so floor draws scale with it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `I_wall` | int | 0-4 (budgeted 2) **[PLACEHOLDER: `I_WALL`]** | spike S2 | Batch breaks in the Y-sorted layer where a non-trail sprite (an actor, a prop) sorts between two wall-trail rows; measured, not derived *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| `D_FT_MAX` | int | 38 (about 3.8 percent of `D_BUDGET` 1000) **[PLACEHOLDER: `D_FT_MAX`], conditional on spike S2** | technical-artist, drawing on the Pipeline's D.16 | This system's share; independent of k and resolution because batches follow pages and rows, not stamp counts, **provided** per-instance data reaches `CanvasItem` shaders (unverified, `[UNV]`); if S2 fails, batching collapses toward one draw per stamp and this value is re-derived at the ADR *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| `TRAIL_MAX_STAMPS` | int | 128-1024 (default 704) **[PLACEHOLDER: `TRAIL_MAX_STAMPS`]**, **derived from the class caps, not from the fixtures** | tuning | World-wide stamp count that triggers a log line; also the node pool's pre-size (FT-41). `TRAIL_MAX_STAMPS = 64 x ceil(stamps_at_caps / 64)` with `stamps_at_caps` = every class at its cap in the MVP world: hazard descriptors 17 x 13 = 221, room frost 12 x `PATCH_MAX_PER_ROOM` 24 = 288, AIRFLOW 12 x 2 = 24, SEAL_STRAIN 8, seam weeps 2 x 12 = 24, puddles 12 x `PUDDLE_MAX_STAMPS` 8 = 96, total **661**, so 704 (the puddle term was missing and a legal world reached 661 against the former 640). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: 384 sat below the MVP worst case once room frost is counted at its real maximum; re-review: now derived from the caps.)* |
| `TRAIL_OVERDRAW_MAX` | int | 4 (fixed) | technical-artist | Lit mark + emissive + mist + accent on one pixel |
| `MIST_MASS_PX_PER_TRAIL`, `VENT_MASS_PX_PER_VENT` | int px | 4096 / 8192 **[PLACEHOLDER: stepped-path equivalents of `ART:1173`'s 64 and 128 particles]** | technical-artist | Opaque mist pixels per trail and per vent (64 and 128 particles of 8 x 8) |

Fields under `expand` (floor division; tile-areas = field area / 48^2; the placeholder loaded world is `TILES_WORLD_MVP` 600 tile-areas, Layout's to confirm):

| Display | `k_auto` | Ladder | Field and tile-areas at each k (k = 1 first) | Whole MVP world fits one field at |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1080p | 3 | 1-5 | 1920 x 1080 (900); 960 x 540 (225); **640 x 360 (100)**; 480 x 270 (56); 384 x 216 (36) | k = 1 only |
| 1440p | 4 | 1-6 | 2560 x 1440 (1600); 1280 x 720 (400); 853 x 480 (177); **640 x 360 (100)**; 512 x 288 (64); 426 x 240 (44) | k = 1 only |
| 2160p | 6 | 1-8 | 3840 x 2160 (3600); 1920 x 1080 (900); 1280 x 720 (400); 960 x 540 (225); 768 x 432 (144); **640 x 360 (100)**; 548 x 308 (73); 480 x 270 (56) | k = 1 and k = 2 |

**Output Range:** pass or fail per row; the draw-call term is the same at every k and resolution (at most 38, or at most 60 if the Perception Filter's mask route renders trails a second time as silhouettes, a Filter ADR item); **both figures are conditional on spike S2** (the per-instance data route is `[UNV]`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Legibility by step (FT-34): full legibility at `k_auto` and the first zoom-out; at k = 1 only polarity, hue blob and form class are guaranteed for m and l, and a sev 1-2 hairline may not resolve, which is accepted and logged and is not a licence to cull.
**Example (MVP worst cascade, any k)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: room frost was budgeted at 4 stamps per room while D.2b allows 24, so the earlier 325 understated the worst case by 240)*: about 17 hazard descriptors x 13 stamps (a worst 12-tile edge = 576 px at 48 px stride = 12 cells + 1 accent) = 221; + room frost **12 compartments x `PATCH_MAX_PER_ROOM` 24 = 288** (the all-cold case: every room at or below the FROST/FREEZING edge with full coverage); + AIRFLOW 12 openings x 2 = 24; + SEAL_STRAIN 8; + seam weeps 2 seams x 12 = 24; + puddles 12 x `PUDDLE_MAX_STAMPS` 8 = 96 (the term the earlier 565 omitted); `stamps_world` = **661** <= 704 (about 6 percent margin). Draws: `L_sets` 1 x `P_trail` 1 x 2 + 16 wall rows x `M_wall` (1, or 2 for a row with an emissive stamp: 32 if every row is emissive, the worst case) + `I_wall` 2 (budgeted) + `P_media` 2 = **38** <= 38 at that worst case (28 with six emissive rows), with no margin at the worst: spike S2 measures `I_wall` and `L_sets` and may move `D_FT_MAX`; the term is reported, never silently absorbed. If wall rows reach 17 the draw term is 40 > 38; `WALL_ROWS_MAX` 16 logs first, so the overflow is reached only through a second floor atlas page (`P_trail` 2: 4 + 32 + 2 + 2 = 40, 17 rows: 4 + 34 + 2 + 2 = 42). It is logged, and the remedy is the Pipeline's per-row z bands, never fewer trails. A cascade of about 49 descriptors x 6 stamps = 294 stamps is also under the world cap; with the 80 wall stamps the Y-sorted layer can spare as a share (`TRAIL_WALL_STAMP_MAX` placeholder, Tuning Knobs) about half of them, 147, are wall stamps, which exceeds that share: logged, and the remedy is the Pipeline's per-row z bands (`PIPE:731`), never fewer stamps. At 2160p k = 1 the in-field count is the whole world. **Coolant wet run (user ruling D26):** the wet run replaces frost slots one for one (D.2d: `n_frost + n_wet` <= `ceil(extent / 48)`), so the 13-stamp worst case per descriptor, the 661 above and the three fixtures below are unchanged by it. **Zoom-step withholding (default applied 2026-10-27, pending user confirmation, D5):** if a ladder step fails its caps on the reference machine, the Pipeline withholds the lowest failing step(s) rather than cutting content; this system never culls a trail to fit (FT-19). The reference machine itself stays open (X14): none is named here. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

**The three budget fixtures** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; shipped as the data files `fx_steady.json`, `fx_mvp_worst.json` and `fx_design_max.json`, FT-40, so every budget criterion names its fixture)*:

| Fixture | Descriptors (`n_desc`) | Stamps (`stamps_world`) | Composition | Pool ops | `ALL` model, `C_STAMP_US` 3 / 6 | Draws |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `fx_steady` | 3 | 17 | `FX-CRYO`: the coolant leak (336 px sev 3 chain: `n_cover` 6 + 1 = 7), 3 room-frost patches at -2000 mC, one seam weep (288 px sev 1: 6 + 1 = 7) | 10 | 146 / 197 us | 38 or fewer, same term as below |
| `fx_mvp_worst` | 63 | 661 | 17 hazard x 13 = 221, room frost 12 x 24 = 288, AIRFLOW 24, SEAL_STRAIN 8, seam weeps 24, puddles 12 x 8 = 96 (all rooms cold and puddled) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | 150 | 3,678 / 5,661 us | 38 (`I_wall` 2) |
| `fx_design_max` | 64 | 618 | a 49-edge cascade x 6 = 294, room frost 12 x 24 = 288, 3 seam weeps x 12 = 36 | 150 | 3,564 / 5,418 us | 38 (`I_wall` 2) |

`fx_design_max` is 618 stamps and `fx_mvp_worst` 661 against a logging threshold of 704, so neither logs; a world above 704 stamps logs once per crossing and never culls (FT-19, FT-41). `fx_design_max` stays at the 64-descriptor design maximum, so puddle descriptors trade against its room-frost descriptors there. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

### D.10 `trail_reveal`

The `trail_reveal` formula is defined as:

`rem_px = trail_extent_px - start(i_last)` ; `thr = clamp(floor(rem_px x RAMP_MAX / STAMP_PX[idx_(i_last)]), 0, RAMP_MAX)` ; an opaque pixel of the last run stamp is drawn iff its flow-phase ramp value `G` <= `thr`

The last stamp of a chain is revealed up to the extent by comparing its flow-phase ramp channel (the `_msk` G channel, `RAMP_MAX` 127, 7 bits: technical-artist channel contract) with an integer threshold, so a frost front is ragged and a scorch front radial with no extra art and no scaling (FT-22, FT-23). All earlier stamps (`i < i_last`) are fully revealed. For a coolant leak the last piece of the chain may be a wet piece (D.2d), and its length is `WET_PITCH_PX` 48 in place of `STAMP_PX[idx_(i_last)]`. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

**Variables:**

| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `trail_extent_px` | int px | 48-1152 | D.2 | Drawn length |
| `start(i_last)` | int px | 0-1152 | D.1 | Offset of the last stamp |
| `i_last` | int | `n_cover(E)` - 1 | D.1 | Ordinal of the last stamp |
| `RAMP_MAX` | int | 127 (fixed) | technical-artist | Top of the 7-bit ramp |
| `thr` | int | 0-127 | calculated | Reveal threshold |

**Output Range:** 0..127; monotone non-decreasing in `trail_extent_px`; 127 when the extent reaches the end of the last stamp (then the terminal piece replaces the tip). Integer-only. The runtime-discard versus pre-authored-dither mechanism is an ADR candidate (FT-23).
**Example:** a sev 3 chain, extent 336, `TAPER_STAMPS` 1: starts 0, 96, 144, 192, 240, 288, 336; `n_cover(336)` = 6 stamps, `i_last` 5 (an s stamp, `start(5)` = 288, length 48); `rem_px` = 336 - 288 = 48, `thr` = floor(48 x 127 / 48) = 127: fully revealed. At extent 312: `rem_px` 24, `thr` = floor(24 x 127 / 48) = 63, so the half of the stamp with the lower ramp values is drawn.

## Edge Cases

*Rule ids are this GDD's `FT-n`; formulas are `D.n` in Formulas. Every outcome is a pure consequence of the descriptor, Layout geometry and the camera (FT-1): where an edge case seems to need memory, the outcome below says what the stateless reading is. "The Graph" owns every state change named here; this system only redraws.*

### Zero, maximum and out-of-range values

- **If a descriptor carries `sev` 0** (the Graph emits no mode 1-26 descriptor for a subject at `hp` >= `HP_OK`): nothing is drawn. `sev` 0 is the no-trail state (FT-6). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the PROVISIONAL sev-0 seam exception is retired; the Graph states the seam weep at sev 1, `LSG:170`, so no sev-0 descriptor remains to draw.)*
- **If a subject carries the WORN descriptor** (`medium` NONE; the Graph's D.4 gives a subject at `hp` just below `HP_OK` sev 1): nothing is drawn, because WORN is the module-body channel and has no consequence-channel medium, not because its `sev` is 0 (FT-32). The sprite wear, gauge and inspect card are the module presenter's. A *patched* subject at its `PATCH_CAP_BP` 6000 cap does not carry WORN: it carries `27 PATCH_WEEP` ("a patch is never silent", `LSG:381`), which draws by row 27 (FT-32). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*
- **If a descriptor has `medium` != NONE and `sev` >= 1 but no row of the table of record matches its id** (the Graph's ids are append-only forever, so a later Graph version can add one): the unknown id draws nothing and writes one diagnostic line per session; the build-time lint "every descriptor id has a row" (`LSG:1110`'s consumer scan) fails first. A silent fallback to a generic mark is rejected because a generic mark is a trail with no vocabulary (FT-3, `ART:669`).
- **If `grow_steps` is 0** (the mode or the severity began this step: a LEAK that has just crossed `LEAK_HP`, a sev change, a seam welded this step): the source stamp draws at `MIN_EXTENT_PX` 48 (D.2), already thickest at the source, and moving media start on the next `present_time` frame. Never an empty first frame: a live descriptor draws on its first step (FT-8). `dull_step` is 0 and `fuel_dried` false on that frame, whatever `since_steps` says (a pipe that wore for 30 minutes and then leaks is born fresh). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)*
- **If `grow_steps` exceeds `SINCE_CLAMP` 20000**: the age bound stops growing at `GROW_PX_PER_STEP[sev] x 20000` (always above any legal `edge_len_px`), so extent equals the edge; `dull_pm` clamps at 1000 (D.5); no overflow is possible because the product is at most 18 x 65535. At the default `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS` 12000, a derelict dead fault back-dated to 40000 steps therefore reads exactly like one back-dated to 20000: the verdict does not age further (the clamp bounds the age term of D.2 only; D.5 reads `mode_steps` unclamped, so dullness is unaffected by `SINCE_CLAMP` at any `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS`). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: field names.)*
- **If `edge_len_px` is below 48** (a one-tile stub): the source stamp is clipped to the edge by the reveal threshold (D.10), never scaled; no pixel leaves the edge (FT-37).
- **If `edge_len_px` exceeds `TRAIL_EDGE_MAX_PX` 1152**: it is a Layout content error (lint to Layout and Derelict Authoring); the trail still draws to the length Layout gave, never hidden and never silently shortened, because shortening would hide a consequence-channel element (FT-19).
- **If the room's `T` is below the DEEP_COLD edge or above the SCORCH edge** (outside the knots of D.3): `frost_reach` clamps at 1152 or 0 (D.3); frost never extends beyond the edge, and a SCORCH room shows no frost reach at all (only the local stamp); a coolant leak's wet run still runs to the age-bound extent at any temperature (D.2d) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.
- **If `intensity_pm` is 299 on one step and 300 on the next** (a density-level boundary at the default width): the level steps 1 -> 2 on the same step as the Graph's `F/` key for that 100-permille bucket (D.4), so cover, drip cadence and frame count change once, on the key, and never between keys.
- **If a coolant leak is isolated the instant it starts** (`intensity_pm` 0, `isolated_at_step` = `mode_since_step`, so `grow_steps` 0 for ever): the descriptor is STILL at once; the system draws the source stamp (extent `MIN_EXTENT_PX`, frozen) and the sev 3+ anchor accent in full colour, no drip, no moving media (FT-17). Static stamps carry no remembered density: they draw at the authored coverage for the current `sev`, because "held" density would be state (FT-1). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)*
- **If an edge's `sev` is 3 and its `GROW_PX_PER_STEP[3]` of 6 px/step runs in the cryo bay**: the front moves at 60 px/s, below the drip's 72 px/s (D.2 invariant iv), so "nothing in the cryo bay moves faster than the drip" (`ART:142`) holds. Raising `GROW_PX_PER_STEP[3]` above 7 breaks it and is refused by the knob range.

### Simultaneous rules and handovers

- **If two descriptors of different `mode` share one anchor** (a `2 SHORT` and a `3 OVERHEAT` at one junction box): two trails draw, in ascending (`subject_id`, `mode`) order, each with its own extent, size and rows (FT-3). Their emissive overlays overlap additively and count once per pixel toward `TRAIL_OVERDRAW_MAX` 4 (D.9).
- **If a severity change and a band change arrive in one Graph step** (a plate drops to sev 4 while the room crosses a band): the `N/` and `B/` keys are delivered together; the next render callback applies both, so there is no frame with the new size and the old frost reach, and no intermediate state exists to draw (FT-15).
- **If a coolant pipe's FREEZE descriptor clears and a `1 LEAK/COOLANT` descriptor appears on the same step** (thaw-burst): the frost sheath despawns on the next frame and a new fan spawns at `grow_steps` 0 (extent 48, then growing at the new `sev`'s rate); the room's `20 ATMO_COLD` crust is a separate descriptor and stays while its band stays (row 4, row 20). The handover is two Graph facts, not an animation.
- **If a valve closes on a live coolant or fuel leak**: `intensity_pm` falls to 0 and the descriptor stays: DRAWN_LIVE -> DRAWN_STILL (T3). The drip stops and the fan, collar or streak **stays, still, not gone**; **its extent stops growing at the isolation step** (`grow_steps` is frozen, GA-T: a valve closing at `grow_steps` 50 on a sev 3 coolant leak leaves the extent at 300 px 100 steps later); it dulls with `mode_steps` (D.5), and frost recedes only as the room's temperature rises (FT-11). An old isolated leak looks old, which is honest (FT-17). **If the valve is reopened**, `grow_steps` rejoins `mode_steps` and the extent steps once to the age bound of the full mode age, capped by the edge (and, for a `4 FREEZE`, the temperature bound): the leak resumes at its true age; the one-time step is intended and flagged for the Graph owner, who may instead shift the onset by the isolated interval (the resolver renders whatever `grow_steps` is). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)*
- **If a patch lands on a frosted coolant pipe** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the fan, the wet run and the drip vanish the next frame and, on that same frame, the pipe's `27 PATCH_WEEP/COOLANT` appears as one small static s-frost spur at the fitting (a same-step handover: the LEAK descriptor ends, T5, and the weep begins at `mode_steps` 0, T11); the room's `ATMO_COLD` crust (row 20, sev >= 2) stays while the band is FROST or colder. The vanish is a Graph change the player caused, so the art-director's "no stamp disappears in one frame without a player-caused Graph change" holds. The onboarding sentence "patch it" (`LSG:839`) therefore replaces the long carried frost with a small weep, not an empty floor; reconciling it with the valve-based anchor moment is Onboarding's. For a patched duct (`27 PATCH_WEEP/GAS`) the plume ends and nothing new is drawn: the HISS is the tell (FT-18). When the patch later decays and the pipe re-leaks, the weep ends and the new LEAK descriptor begins at `grow_steps` 0 on the same step.
- **If a SEAM descriptor is the weep of an imperfect weld** (`1 LEAK/GAS`, sev 1, `intensity_pm` per LS-14 field provenance, counted from `built_step`, `LSG:170`): it draws at s-*trace* along the bead (FT-14) with the mist legibility floor `COVER16_FLOOR` 8, so a physically leaking seam is never undrawn and the first weld the player inspects reads as mist along a seam (P1). When wear-in takes `leak_bp` to 0 the descriptor ends and the trace vanishes the next frame (a Graph change). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review: the sev-0 exception that stood here is retired.)*
- **If a SEAL_STRAIN hatch has both media in play** (GAS pressure strain and a HEAT cold face): two descriptors, two frame sprites in ascending order, bowed rim for GAS and rim frost for HEAT (row 26); `dir` picks the side of each.
- **If a fuel puddle sits in a room below the freezing point** (`LSG:838`): nothing changes in the MVP; the `25 PUDDLE` descriptor stays and the puddle draws unfrozen, with no frozen hint (the Graph reserves that for VS-A). Coolant's frost fan is never drawn on a fuel stain (FT-30's routed `medium` question for a frozen non-coolant tank).
- **If a `1 LEAK/GAS` descriptor's `intensity_pm` is 0** (an isolated duct): zero pixels draw, by design (FT-18); the descriptor and its acoustic class still exist, and no cyan glow is requested while `intensity_pm` is 0 (row 1). The absence is the verdict: "O2 failure leaves no trail" (`ART:697`).
- **If an exposed DUCT leaks to EXTERIOR**: the stream draws out through the hull-run anchor as `1 LEAK/GAS` (FT-3, row 1); the compartment's own `AIRFLOW` and `ATMO_*` descriptors carry the draught and the thinning, each by its own row.
- **If a SEVERED fluid open end remains after salvage**: it draws its medium's leak row at the open end until `cap_port` ends the descriptor; an ELECTRIC cable end draws nothing unless a `2 SHORT` is live there (the `8 SEVERED` row of the table of record).
- **If a BREACH pre-stage descriptor were WORN rather than the mode-5 STRUCT the Graph now states** (`LSG:170`; a later Graph version regressing it): the hull hairline would not be drawn (FT-32) and the hull case would lose its consequence-channel Pillar 2 tell; the TICK acoustic class would be absent too. This cannot happen under the present Graph and is kept as the regression the build-time lint "every id has a row" and AC-71 guard. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)*
- **If a descriptor's `sev` rises on an aged subject** (a hull plate at sev 3 with `mode_steps` 300 rises to sev 4): the Graph resets the mode onset, so on the `N/` frame `grow_steps` is 0, the l head draws at `MIN_EXTENT_PX` and the extent regrows at 15 px per step (48, 48, 48, 48, 60, 75, ...); the previous, shorter-lived mark of the smaller size is replaced by the new head, never inflated (FT-8, T6). If `sev` falls, the same reset applies at the lower rate. This is the resolution adopted for the "a sev rise redraws hundreds of pixels of history in one frame" finding; it is a default applied 2026-10-27, pending user confirmation (Open Questions 6c), and neither the one-frame jump to the old age bound nor an `EXTENT_JUMP_MAX` cap is adopted. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)*
- **If a subject carries two live RUN descriptors** (a seam weep and a BREACH pre-stage; a FREEZE and a LEAK) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the Graph keeps one onset pair, for the dominant one (highest `sev`, ties to the lowest `mode`), and hands the other the legacy `since_steps`. The resolver draws the non-dominant NODE or EDGE descriptor as its head stamp only (`MIN_EXTENT_PX`, `dull_step` 0) and the non-dominant seam weep at its true `built_step` age, so no frame inflates (FT-8, AC-181). It is never undrawn.
- **If a leak appears or clears in a room that already shows `20 ATMO_COLD` frost at a constant `T`** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: no patch moves, appears or disappears, because the room-frost anchor tile is fixed by Layout geometry alone (FT-11, D.2b, AC-182).
- **If a derelict dead fault is back-dated** (Derelict Authoring DA-17, which must seed all three GA-T fields, Open Questions 25): the section is instantiated with `mode_since_step` (and `fail_since_step`) in the past, `mode_key` set to the fault's key and `isolated_at_step` 0, so `grow_steps` and `mode_steps` are large, the extent is the whole edge and `dull_step` is the dried stop once `mode_steps` >= `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS`; a `mode_since_step` seeded without its `mode_key` is overwritten by the Graph's phase 9 on the first run and the fault is born fresh (FT-8); a back-date that sets only `fail_since_step` leaves `mode_steps` at 0, so every dead fault would be born `dull_step` 0 with extent 48 (the authoring lint `DEAD_FAULT_NOT_DRIED` must read `mode_steps`; cross-GDD note). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)*
- **If a tank of fuel freezes** (`4 FREEZE`, `medium` FUEL, `LSG:170`): the frost sheath draws on the tank's lagging in Coolant Ice with no fan and no drip; no coolant fan is drawn on it (row 4, FT-12). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)*
- **If a trail's source is off-screen but its body crosses the view** (an anchor behind the camera edge, a 600 px run entering the field): the trail is drawn that frame, because the test is the polyline bounding box padded by one head cell, not the anchor (FT-19, AC-154). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)*
- **If the room warms or cools across the FROST edge at 8 mC per step** (room frost, row 20): the patch count rises by at most one patch per Graph step (D.2b pop bound, AC-148); at the first FROST step exactly one patch (the anchor tile's) appears, so a sev-2 descriptor is never undrawn (FT-19) and never pops across the room. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)*
- **If a coolant leak sits in a COMFORT room** (the home-ship default, frost reach 0): the chain is the head fan stamp followed by the wet run to the age-bound extent (D.2d); `frost_px` is 0, the drip runs, and the width steps 8 / 6 / 4 / 2 px with distance from the source, so the wound is followable at every temperature; a sev 3 six-tile run is complete at `grow_steps` 48 (4.8 s), and a valve closing freezes it with the extent (FT-8). Nothing changes with temperature except which pieces are frost. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*
- **If a derelict dead coolant fault sits in a COMFORT room**: it draws the head fan stamp and the whole run greyed, with no drip (STILL, back-dated, dulled by `mode_steps`, D.5, D.2d); the coolant verdict reads at any temperature, not only in a cold room. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*
- **If a LIVE descriptor's `mode_steps` is at or above `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS`** (a leak isolated early and reopened; default applied 2026-10-27, pending user confirmation, Open Questions 6d): it is shown at stop `DULL_STEPS` - 2 and `fuel_dried` stays false; when the valve next closes the descriptor is STILL, the cap releases and the stop steps to the dried stop in one frame (one palette step, intended: the pipe is old and the step follows the player's own valve; AC-177). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*
- **If a closed door lets an unheated room cool** (Graph ruling D12, default applied 2026-10-27, pending user confirmation): rooms behind closed doors cool to COOL and FROST in 20-30 minutes; the Graph rules this the intended consequence and not a warning, so this system adds nothing for it: the `20 ATMO_COLD` crust draws from the room's true `T` by D.2b exactly as for any other cold room. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*

### Degenerate strategies

- **If the player zooms out to hide nothing, or in to hide something**: neither works. A trail is drawn whenever its padded polyline bounding box meets the field at the current ladder step k (FT-19; the anchor point is not the test) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; off-field trails are re-evaluated at every k, so a zoom-out reveals every one and a zoom-in leaves no stale state (there is none to go stale).
- **If the player opens and closes a valve every Graph step**: the `F/` key fires each step, the descriptor toggles DRAWN_LIVE and DRAWN_STILL, and only the moving media (drip, mist) start and stop; the stamps never blink and no cost accumulates (T3, T4; one re-select per key per step, D.7). The physical effect (the leak runs on alternate steps) is the Graph's.
- **If the player cuts and re-welds a section to "reset" a mature fan**: the Graph discards and rebuilds the nodes (`T`, `SEC/` keys), the descriptor's mode onset restarts (`grow_steps` and `mode_steps` 0; the legacy `since_steps` also restarts), and the trail regrows from 48 px at the sev's rate. This is honest: a re-welded joint is a new joint. No trail is hidden, because the Graph, not this system, ended the old one. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If the player stands a carried or placed object, a body or a crewmate on a floor trail**: the mark is drawn beneath actors by the band rule (FT-25) and is occluded by the object's sprite; the descriptor, its hiss and its draught still exist. Occlusion by world geometry is physical and is not suppression; this system never moves, hides or fades a stamp for readability.
- **If the room is dark** (no lamp, no fixture, no glow reaches the trail): no *non-emissive* trail pixel is brighter than the hull-floor tint (FT-26); the acoustic class, the band cues and the draught still tell; a live emissive element (scorch rim, ember edge) may be visible and is the subject of FT-27, not a leak. Darkness is honest; light is a decision.
- **If the player tries to read all trails from a glance at a gauge, map or schematic**: none exists. The only way to see a trail is to spend light and walk to where the lamp reaches (Player Fantasy, "cost of checking"; FT-4).

### Save, restore, dormancy and co-op

- **If a restore happens mid-cascade**: the Graph emits one `ALL` after `post_restore`; this system performs one full re-read reconciled against its pool within the same render frame (FT-16, T10). Extents are already grown (a pure function of `grow_steps`), dullness is already final, and moving media take their phase from `present_time`, so a mist does not "start" on load. Nothing was saved by this system (`SL:853`, `SL:1027`). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If a derelict section is instantiated** (`ALL`): its authored dead faults draw STILL, dulled to their back-dated `mode_steps` (T2); no live moving media appear unless the authored descriptor has `intensity_pm` > 0 (a wet derelict fault is allowed and must be authored as such). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If a section is dormant**: it emits no descriptors (`LSG:822`), so no trail draws; on wake the Graph's M19 has advanced every subject's `mode_since_step` and non-zero `isolated_at_step` by the dormant interval (LS-17), so the trails reappear at the `grow_steps` and `mode_steps` the section ran, with no catch-up animation. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If the kernel is PAUSED**: `present_time`, `tick_alpha` and the committed tick freeze together; extents and animation stop; nothing advances or fades (FT-1).
- **If the player dies and the game restarts from the last autosave** (the MVP death handling, owned by Save/Load): the load is a restore; the trail set rebuilds from one `ALL` and carries no memory of what was on screen at death. Anything that depended on that memory (a "seen" flag, a first-sight flourish) does not exist here.
- **If a MIRROR client receives descriptors** (VS-B): the co-op summary carries the intensity bucket, `since_steps`, `mode_steps` and `grow_steps` per active descriptor (`LSG:210`, LS-21), so the client draws the same extent and dullness as the HOST by the same resolver; there is no legacy branch (FT-33). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; re-review.)*
- **If two co-op clients are on different ladder steps k or different displays**: each draws the same descriptors at the same base-px positions and extents (layout is in base px, FT-34); only the field and the on-screen pixel size differ. A trail never depends on any one client's camera except for which anchors are inside its field.
- **If a client reconnects or a late joiner arrives (VS-B)**: it receives the Graph's state as a restore and therefore a single `ALL`; no trail history is transmitted or needed.

### Hallucination and the truth channel

- **If the Perception Filter runs at its maximum stage** (violet bleed on pipe silhouettes, a distorted cone): every trail pixel is bit-identical with the Filter on or off (FT-2, `PIPE:989`); a violet pipe beside an unviolet frost fan is itself a tell (Player Fantasy, drift stage 3).
- **If the Filter's mask route is unavailable**: the Filter disables its own pass (`PIPE:735`) and this system does nothing different; it never branches on the Filter's state.
- **If a class (b) hallucination shows a machine whole and green while its outlet fitting leaks**: the body sprite and gauge are the module-body channel and may lie; the mist, fan or scorch at the fitting is anchored to the fitting, not the body, and is unchanged (FT-24, ET-5). The floor outranks the machine.
- **If a class (a) phantom entity appears to tear a duct**: it leaves no descriptor, so no mark; a **real** hidden entity that ruptures a hull plate leaves an ordinary BREACH or LEAK descriptor, drawn along the rib or seam like any breach with no variant by `cause` (FT-31). "Did it leave a mark?" costs a walk and light.
- **If a hidden entity's GOUGE lands on a duct or cable** (not a hull segment): the Graph surfaces it only as the WORN descriptor (module-body, corruptible) or as a LEAK; this system draws the LEAK if there is one and nothing for WORN. This is the routed truth-channel hole (FT-31); until the Graph owner closes it, MVP Horror restricts GOUGE to hull segments (binding on the Horror Entities GDD, FT-31). Pillar 4(a) is a joint guarantee of this GDD (Graph descriptors) and Horror Entities (web-grammar footprints); neither may assume the other covers every footprint kind.
- **If more than `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP` 16 footprint writes arrive in a step** (global, across all callers; registry `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP`): the Graph drops the excess, so this system only ever sees accepted effects; a trail never shows a footprint the Graph did not accept (FT-31).

### Display, accessibility and the alarm

- **If reduced motion is on**: every moving medium holds frame 0, its authored hold frame; direction, density, size, extent and dullness continue; `present_time` still advances; no trail, hairline or audio is suppressed (FT-28, `PIPE:847`). A mist that does not drift is still a mist because its form rule carries it.
- **If reduce flashing is on** (the first-launch default): arc pops are replaced by the held glow at `ARC_HELD_GLOW_PM` (600 permille of peak) with the four static spark-tip pixels lit (D.8b, FT-29); the SHORT scorch still draws; no trail disappears (FT-29). *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If both reduced motion and reduce flashing are on**: the arc is a steady glow on its hold frame; the scorch, its extent and its dullness are unchanged; the only difference from a live arc is the absence of pops.
- **If k = 1 at 2160p** (the field is 36 times the area of the default): every on-screen stamp is still drawn; pressure degrades moving media first (frame count, then density, then `TRAIL_ANIM_FPS` halving, then far-from-beam frame hold), never stamps (FT-19, D.9). A sev 1-2 hairline (a 2 px composite) may not resolve at k = 1; that is accepted and logged and is not a licence to cull or to add a marker (FT-34). The hull-fatigue tell is then carried by the TICK acoustic class of the flow-zero row 5 pre-stage at the descriptor's anchor (consequence channel, Adaptive Audio, FT-34); no marker or layer is added.
- **If the Alarm Red sweep passes a lit trail**: the sweep tints lit albedo toward red, so loop hue may lose fidelity under it; the trail still identifies by form and polarity (the five behaviours differ in greyscale: FT-12), and the trail is never itself drawn in the alarm colour (FT-4). Hue fidelity under the sweep is a Lighting spike item.
- **If the window is resized or the display scale changes at run time**: `k_auto` and the field change; the next frame re-evaluates which anchors are in the field from the poll cache. No key is needed and no state is migrated.

### Layout, budget and engine

- **If a wall fitting has no floor-run geometry from Layout**: the wall part draws alone and the missing run is a lint error (Layout owes the start and direction of the floor run: FT-13), not a silent fallback; the trail is never hidden for lacking a run.
- **If `anchor_geometry` returns nothing for a descriptor's anchor** (a Layout read fails after a weld): the source stamp draws at the descriptor's own `anchor` with extent `MIN_EXTENT_PX`, never hidden (FT-19), and one diagnostic line is written.
- **If a subject is DESTROYED (`hp` 0)** (Derelict Authoring OQ-DA-11): this system draws whatever descriptor the Graph emits for it and decides nothing about emission (FT-3). The Graph's transition row gives a DESTROYED subject sev 4 and, for a hull segment, BREACH at `A_HOLE_MAX` (`LSG:242`), so `seg_b_breach` at `hp` 0 draws at size l (bent rib or hole) by row 5, and in its dried form once `intensity_pm` is 0 and `mode_steps` >= `DRIED_AFTER_STEPS`. If the Graph emits no descriptor for a DESTROYED subject, nothing is drawn (no decal, FT-3) and Derelict Authoring's fallback applies. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **If a floor run reaches a doorway or a room boundary**: the run stops at the sill; no opaque pixel is drawn in the neighbouring compartment (FT-37). This is Layout's rule (LY-8 e): a run that would cross is the Layout lint `FLOOR_RUN_CROSSES_BOUNDARY`, never a runtime clip.
- **If a wall fitting sits within 72 px of a door or cap seam**: the wall part is cropped to its host face and never drawn into the cap band (FT-13, `ART:1033`).
- **If `stamps_world` exceeds `TRAIL_MAX_STAMPS` 704, or the wall rows exceed `WALL_ROWS_MAX` 16, or an in-play `ALL` exceeds `TRAIL_RECONCILE_BUDGET_MS` 4 (a restore `ALL` exceeds `TRAIL_RESTORE_BUDGET_MS` 8)**: one diagnostic line is logged on the rising edge (FT-41 rule 5); every trail keeps drawing (a logging threshold, never a cut-off: FT-16, FT-19); the Pipeline's per-row z-band remedy applies to the Y-sorted layer, never fewer trails. The pool, pre-sized to `TRAIL_MAX_STAMPS`, grows past it and logs. *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review.)*
- **If `ALL` follows a 300-node breach**: one full re-read in the same frame (FT-16); the cost is D.7's `cost_all_us`; if it exceeds the threshold it is logged and the frame is not skipped.
- **If a particle path is used for venting and the spike has not passed**: it is not used; venting is stepped frames (FT-21). If the spike later passes, the 12-particle-emitter cap applies and exceeding it degrades moving media by the FT-19 order.

## Dependencies

*One row per neighbour this system must take from or give to. Only what is taken or given is listed; a neighbour's internals are never guessed. "Hard" = the system cannot render its first trail without it; "soft" = it enriches or constrains but the MVP trail renders without it. Status: designed / provisional as defined in Interactions with Other Systems. Among the systems designed in the same orchestrated run this system has **one soft dependent, Emitter State** (designed; its Dependencies row `EM:714` lists "Failure & Trail \| this depends on it \| Soft"); its other dependents are named by the index (Onboarding, In-World Instruments; Instruments is now designed, Onboarding is still provisional).*
